# Manifest — Group 3: Gauge Theory XII and XIII

Sources: Bär (content-map-wernli.md) §4.1, §4.2, §4.3, §5.1, §5.2, §5.3 and Appendix B (source typos 23–31). Haydys contributes nothing to these two chapters except cross-references noted in place (his I3.2.1, I4.3.2, I7.1.11, T7.2.6 belong to groups 1–2 and are only linked from here).

Standing rules used throughout this manifest (binding for the writers of both chapters):
- Anchor names from `anchors.md` are used byte-exactly. Pages of chapters I–XI that are not anchors are written as `[NEEDED FROM I–XI: …]` for the orchestrator to reconcile.
- Existing vault pages are LINKED, never rewritten, except those in `unproved-pages.txt`. The following flagged pages are re-proved in chapter XII under new names: `Thm - Homotopy Invariance of Singular Homology` → `Thm - Homotopy Invariance of Singular Homology (Gauge Theory)`; `Thm - Mayer-Vietoris for Singular Homology` → `Thm - Mayer-Vietoris Sequence for Singular Homology (Gauge Theory)`; `Thm - Hurewicz Theorem (Statement)` → `Thm - Hurewicz Theorem`; `Thm - Seifert-van Kampen Theorem (Statement)` → `Thm - Seifert-van Kampen Theorem for Two Open Sets` (the two-open-set case, fully proved, which is all the series needs); `Thm - Higher Homotopy Groups are Abelian` → `Thm - Higher Homotopy Groups are Abelian via the Eckmann-Hilton Argument`; `Thm - The de Rham Theorem (Full Proof)` / `(Statement)` → `Thm - De Rham Theorem via Smooth Singular Chains`; `Thm - Singular and Simplicial Homology Agree on Triangulable Spaces` is not needed (the fundamental class of a triangulated manifold is handled by local homology instead); `Thm - Euler Characteristic via Alternating Betti Numbers` is not needed (Euler characteristics are computed from Betti numbers by definition on the pages that use them, with finite generation proved). Flagged point-set pages (`Thm - Lebesgue Number Lemma`, `Thm - The Tube Lemma`, `Thm - Compact Subset of Hausdorff is Closed`, `Thm - Continuous Image of a Compact Space`, `Thm - Compact to Hausdorff Continuous Bijection is Homeomorphism`, `Thm - Tietze Extension Theorem`, `Thm - Paracompact Has Partitions of Unity`) are never cited: the Lebesgue number lemma gets its own page in §12.2 (it is reused by five pages); the other compactness facts are one-paragraph lemmas re-proved inline on the first page that needs them (named below). `Thm - Sard's Theorem` (DG IV) is flagged: chapter XIII cites the finite-dimensional Sard theorem only through chapter X (`[NEEDED FROM I–XI: finite-dimensional Sard theorem, proved as a lemma of Thm - Sard-Smale Theorem]`) and `Thm - Parametric Transversality`. `Thm - Brouwer Degree is a Homotopy Invariant` (RG IV) is flagged: degrees are defined homologically in chapter XII (`Def - R-Orientation and the Fundamental Class`, corollary "degree of a map between closed oriented manifolds") and never through the RG IV page.
- Every page's Notation section opens with the standing conventions of `conventions.md` that it depends on: homotopy groups via based maps $(S^n, NP) \to (X, x)$ or $(I^n, \partial I^n) \to (X, x)$ with concatenation in the first coordinate; singular homology with coefficients in a commutative ring $R$ with unit; orientations of topological manifolds via local homology generators; fundamental class $[M] \in H_n(M;R)$; intersection form as in Bär §5.1; unimodular means $\det = \pm 1$; $\overline{X}$ is $X$ with reversed orientation and $Q_{\overline{X}} = -Q_X$.
- **The identification $\operatorname{Pic}(M) \cong H^2(M;\mathbb{Z})$ IS provided by this group** (discharging manifest-1's deferral "identified only in chapter XII if group 3 proves it"). It is proved in full on the chapter XII page `Thm - Hermitian Line Bundles are Classified by the Integral Second Cohomology` (§12.7) by the Čech / transition-function route: a Hermitian line bundle is a $U(1)$-cocycle on a good cover, and the connecting map of $0 \to \mathbb{Z} \to \mathbb{R} \to U(1) \to 0$ in Čech cohomology (identified with singular cohomology on the good cover) sends it to its integral class. Chapters I–XI still write $\operatorname{Pic}(M)$ as manifest-1 §3 requires; ONLY chapter XIII invokes the identification, and only through that named page, on which "$\operatorname{Pic}(X)$ = isomorphism classes of Hermitian line bundles" and "$H^2(X;\mathbb{Z})$ = set of $U(1)$-bundles" become interchangeable.
- Source typos (Bär Appendix B, items 23–31) are corrected on the pages named in the coverage table, each with a note `(source typo: …)` giving the printed form.

---

### §12.1 Homotopy and Homotopy Groups
- `Def - Homotopy and Homotopy Equivalence` — type: definition (compound: homotopy, homotopy relative to a subset, homotopy equivalence and homotopy inverse, contractible space); source items: D4.1.1, D4.1.2, D4.1.3, D4.1.4, E4.1.1, E4.1.2, E4.1.3, E4.1.4, T4.1.1, T4.1.2, T4.1.3, T4.1.4; prereqs: [Def - Homotopy, Def - Homotopy Equivalence and Contractible Space, Def - Continuous Map, Def - Product Topology]; spec: States Bär's Definitions 4.1.1, 4.1.5, 4.1.7, 4.1.11 with $I = [0,1]$ and $C(X,Y)$ notation, correcting D4.1.2 to $f \circ g \simeq \mathrm{id}_Y$ and $g \circ f \simeq \mathrm{id}_X$ `(source typo 23: the source prints $\mathrm{id}_Y$ twice)`. Links the Topology IV pages for the bare notions and explains that this page adds the relative version and the notation used throughout chapters XII–XIII. Examples verified clause by clause: E4.1.1 ($\mathrm{id}_{\mathbb{R}^n} \simeq 0$ via $f(x,t) = tx$), E4.1.2 (any two maps $\mathbb{R}^n \to \mathbb{R}^n$ are homotopic by the straight-line homotopy), E4.1.3 ($\mathbb{R}^n$ contractible), E4.1.4 ($S^n \simeq \mathbb{R}^{n+1} \setminus \{0\}$ via inclusion and $y \mapsto y/\lVert y \rVert$ with $G(y,t) = (1 - t + t/\lVert y \rVert)\,y$, continuity of $G$ checked); non-example: $S^1$ is not contractible (proved on the page from `Thm - Pi_1 of S^1 is Z` and the homotopy invariance corollary of `Thm - Continuous Maps Induce Homomorphisms of Homotopy Groups`, cited forward with a wikilink). Corollaries proved in full on the page: T4.1.1 (homotopy is an equivalence relation: constant homotopy, reversal $\tilde f(x,t) = f(x,1-t)$, concatenation at $t = 1/2$ with continuity by the pasting lemma `Thm - The Pasting Lemma`), T4.1.4 (the same for relative homotopy, with the relative clause checked at each step), T4.1.2 (homotopy equivalence is an equivalence relation; transitivity: $(g' \circ g) \circ (f \circ f') \simeq g' \circ f' \simeq \mathrm{id}$ using the composition lemma X4.1.1 stated and proved here as Lemma), T4.1.3 (homeomorphisms are homotopy equivalences, converse false by E4.1.3). Calibration check: reader verifies $S^n \simeq \mathbb{R}^{n+1} \setminus \{0\}$, that a deformation retract gives a homotopy equivalence (link `Def - Deformation Retract`), and that homotopy relative to $A$ implies homotopy.
- `Def - Homotopy Groups` — type: definition (compound: $\pi_n(X,x)$ as based homotopy classes, the cube model, the concatenation product, the fundamental group and simple connectivity, the induced map $g_\sharp$); source items: D4.1.5, D4.1.6, D4.1.7, D4.1.8, T4.1.5, R4.1.1; prereqs: [Def - Homotopy and Homotopy Equivalence, Def - Path-Product and the Fundamental Group, Def - Simply Connected Space, Def - Higher Homotopy Group]; spec: Primary form $\pi_n(X,x) := \{f \in C(S^n,X) \mid f(NP) = x\}/\simeq \text{rel } \{NP\}$; equivalent cube form via a fixed $\psi : I^n \to S^n$ collapsing $\partial I^n$ to $NP$ and restricting to a homeomorphism of the interior (an explicit formula for $\psi$ is given, e.g. via the homeomorphism $I^n \cong \bar B^n$ and $\bar B^n / S^{n-1} \cong S^n$, link `Thm - The Sphere as Quotient of the Disk`); the bijection of the two models and the equivalence of the two relations are proved on `Thm - Group Structure on Homotopy Groups and the Cube Model`, wikilinked. Concatenation in the first coordinate (conventions.md) and $g_\sharp [f] := [g \circ f]$. T4.1.5 proved on the page (identification $\{f \in C(S^0, X) \mid f(NP) = x\} \cong X$ via $f \mapsto f(SP)$ and $f \simeq f'$ rel $NP$ iff $f(SP)$, $f'(SP)$ lie in the same path component; so $\pi_0(X,x)$ is the set of path components, pointed by the component of $x$, with no canonical group structure). R4.1.1 as a remark with the example $\pi_1(S^1 \vee S^1)$ non-abelian, referenced to `Ex - The Universal Cover of the Figure-Eight is the Cayley Graph of F_2` for the free-group identification (only the non-commutativity of the two generators is used, and this is re-derived on the page by the abelianisation exercise `Ex - Abelianization of the Free Group on Two Generators` — forward link). Convention callout: Bär's $S^n$-model versus Hatcher's $I^n$-model; both used. Examples: $\pi_n$ of a point is trivial (verified); non-example of a based map: a map with $f(NP) \ne x$ is not an element. Calibration check: compute $\pi_0$ of $\{0,1\}$, verify that the constant map is the neutral element in the cube model, write $g_\sharp$ for $g = $ inclusion.
- `Thm - Group Structure on Homotopy Groups and the Cube Model` — type: theorem; source items: T4.1.6, I4.1.5; prereqs: [Def - Homotopy Groups, Thm - The Fundamental Group is a Group, Thm - Reparametrization Lemma]; statement: For $n \ge 1$, composition with $\psi$ induces a bijection $\{g \in C(I^n,X) \mid g(\partial I^n) = \{x\}\}/\simeq \text{rel } \partial I^n \;\to\; \pi_n(X,x)$, and concatenation $[g_1] * [g_2] := [g_1 * g_2]$ is well defined and makes $\pi_n(X,x)$ a group with neutral element the class of the constant map and inverse $[g]^{-1} = [g \circ r_1]$, $r_1(t_1, \dots, t_n) = (1 - t_1, t_2, \dots, t_n)$; proof: source gives the bijection via $\psi$ and asserts the group axioms; the page proves: (Lemma 1) $\psi$ induces a homeomorphism $I^n/\partial I^n \cong S^n$ (compact-to-Hausdorff bijection lemma re-proved inline, since the vault page is flagged); (Lemma 2) based maps $S^n \to X$ correspond to maps $I^n \to X$ constant on $\partial I^n$ and based homotopies correspond to homotopies rel $\partial I^n$ (universal property of the quotient, link `Thm - Universal Property of the Quotient`, applied to $I^n \times I \to (I^n/\partial I^n) \times I$, which is a quotient map because $I$ is compact — prove this inline as Lemma 2a via the tube lemma re-proved on the spot); (Lemma 3) well-definedness of $*$ on classes; (Lemma 4) associativity and unit via explicit reparametrisation homotopies in the first coordinate (write the piecewise-linear reparametrisations and the straight-line homotopy between them, as in the proof of `Thm - The Fundamental Group is a Group`, which is the case $n = 1$ and is cited for the shape of the formulas but not as the proof); (Lemma 5) inverses. Reference: Hatcher §4.1, pp. 340–341; Bredon VII.2. spec: Why-true mechanism: "all group axioms are reparametrisations of the first coordinate, and any two reparametrisations of $I$ fixing the endpoints are homotopic rel endpoints by a straight line". Targets: $\pi_n$ of products (Ex in §12.3), functoriality page.
- `Thm - Higher Homotopy Groups are Abelian via the Eckmann-Hilton Argument` — type: theorem; source items: T4.1.7; prereqs: [Thm - Group Structure on Homotopy Groups and the Cube Model]; statement: For $n \ge 2$ and any pointed space $(X,x)$, $\pi_n(X,x)$ is abelian; proof: source gives a six-picture sketch; the page proves it in full by the Eckmann–Hilton route: define the second concatenation $*_2$ in the second coordinate, prove (Lemma 1) $*_2$ is well defined on classes and has the constant map as a two-sided unit up to homotopy rel $\partial I^n$; (Lemma 2) the interchange law $(g_1 *_1 g_2) *_2 (g_3 *_1 g_4) = (g_1 *_2 g_3) *_1 (g_2 *_2 g_4)$ holds on the nose (write both sides on the four sub-cubes); (Lemma 3) from interchange with units, $[g_1] *_1 [g_2] = [g_1] *_2 [g_2] = [g_2] *_1 [g_1]$ (the standard four-line Eckmann–Hilton computation, each equality justified by Lemma 2 with explicit substitutions of the constant map). Also record Bär's picture proof as the intuition in Why Is It True. Reference: Hatcher §4.1 p. 340 (the picture); for the interchange-law argument, tom Dieck, *Algebraic Topology* (2008), §2.3, or Bredon VII.2; the writer follows the interchange law exactly as written here. spec: Sources: any $H$-space has abelian $\pi_1$ (link `Ex - Pi_1 of a Topological Group is Abelian`); targets: the Hurewicz theorem for $n \ge 2$ needs no abelianisation (Bär Rem. 4.2.23).
- `Thm - Continuous Maps Induce Homomorphisms of Homotopy Groups` — type: theorem; source items: T4.1.8 (X4.1.1), T4.1.9, T4.1.10, T4.1.11, T4.1.12; prereqs: [Def - Homotopy Groups, Thm - Group Structure on Homotopy Groups and the Cube Model, Def - Homotopy and Homotopy Equivalence]; statement: (i) (Lemma 4.1.19) if $f_0 \simeq f_1$ rel $A$, $g_0 \simeq g_1$ rel $B$, $f_i(A) \subset B$, then $g_0 \circ f_0 \simeq g_1 \circ f_1$ rel $A$; (ii) for $g \in C(X,Y)$, $g_\sharp : \pi_n(X,x) \to \pi_n(Y,g(x))$, $[f] \mapsto [g \circ f]$, is well defined and a group homomorphism for $n \ge 1$; (iii) $g_0 \simeq g_1$ rel $\{x\}$ implies $(g_0)_\sharp = (g_1)_\sharp$; (iv) $(g \circ f)_\sharp = g_\sharp \circ f_\sharp$ and $(\mathrm{id}_X)_\sharp = \mathrm{id}$; (v) if $f : (X,x) \to (Y,y)$ and $g : (Y,y) \to (X,x)$ satisfy $g \circ f \simeq \mathrm{id}_X$ rel $\{x\}$ and $f \circ g \simeq \mathrm{id}_Y$ rel $\{y\}$, then $f_\sharp$ and $g_\sharp$ are mutually inverse isomorphisms; in particular pointed-homotopy-equivalent spaces have isomorphic homotopy groups and contractible spaces have trivial homotopy groups; proof: source: (i) exercise, (ii) full except the homomorphism property, (iii)–(v) full; the page proves (i) with the homotopy $H(x,t) = g_t(f_t(x))$ where $f_t = f(\cdot,t)$, $g_t = g(\cdot,t)$, continuity via the product map $X \times I \to Y \times I$; (ii) homomorphism: $g \circ (f_1 * f_2) = (g \circ f_1) * (g \circ f_2)$ on the nose; (v) also treats the subtlety that a free homotopy equivalence induces isomorphisms on $\pi_n$ (change-of-basepoint isomorphism along the track of the basepoint — prove the change-of-basepoint lemma $\beta_\gamma : \pi_n(X,x_0) \to \pi_n(X,x_1)$ for a path $\gamma$ as Lemma, Hatcher p. 341, since `Ex - Homotopy Groups of a Contractible Space Vanish` and §12.8 need it for unpointed contractible spaces). Reference: Hatcher §4.1 (pp. 341–342), Bredon VII.3. spec: Why-true: "composition is continuous in both arguments, so homotopies push forward". Targets: homotopy invariance of $\pi_n$ used everywhere; the covering-space computations of §12.2.
- `Ex - Composition Respects Relative Homotopy` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: X4.1.1; prereqs: [Def - Homotopy and Homotopy Equivalence]; spec: Prove Lemma 4.1.19 (statement (i) above) directly from the definitions, writing the homotopy $H(x,t) = g(f(x,t),t)$ and checking continuity and the relative clause; then deduce that $g_\sharp$ is well defined. Route: `Def - Homotopy and Homotopy Equivalence`, product topology continuity. Solution invokes `Thm - Continuous Maps Induce Homomorphisms of Homotopy Groups` only to compare with the general statement.
- `Ex - Homotopy Groups of a Contractible Space Vanish` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: T4.1.12 (last clause), E4.1.3; prereqs: [Def - Homotopy Groups, Thm - Continuous Maps Induce Homomorphisms of Homotopy Groups]; spec: Show $\pi_n(X,x) = 0$ for all $n \ge 0$ and all $x$ when $X$ is contractible, including the case where the contraction does not fix $x$ (use the change-of-basepoint lemma); apply to $\mathbb{R}^n$ and to any convex subset of $\mathbb{R}^N$. Route: `Thm - Continuous Maps Induce Homomorphisms of Homotopy Groups` (v) and its basepoint lemma.
- `Ex - The Punctured Plane is Not Contractible` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: E4.1.4, T4.1.13 (consequence), T4.1.3 (converse fails); prereqs: [Def - Homotopy and Homotopy Equivalence, Thm - Continuous Maps Induce Homomorphisms of Homotopy Groups, Thm - Pi_1 of S^1 is Z]; spec: Prove $\mathbb{R}^2 \setminus \{0\} \simeq S^1$ explicitly (E4.1.4 with $n = 1$), deduce $\pi_1(\mathbb{R}^2 \setminus \{0\}) \cong \mathbb{Z}$ from `Thm - Pi_1 of S^1 is Z` and homotopy invariance, hence $\mathbb{R}^2 \setminus \{0\}$ is not contractible although $\mathbb{R}^2$ is; conclude that "homotopy equivalent" is strictly weaker than "homeomorphic" ($\mathbb{R}^n \simeq \{0\}$) and strictly stronger than "same homotopy groups in degree 0". Key decision: choose the retraction $y \mapsto y/\lVert y \rVert$ and verify the homotopy formula's continuity away from $0$.
- `Exercise Index - §12.1 Homotopy and Homotopy Groups` — lists: [Ex - Composition Respects Relative Homotopy, Ex - Homotopy Groups of a Contractible Space Vanish, Ex - The Punctured Plane is Not Contractible]

### §12.2 Coverings, Lifting, and the Universal Covering
- `Def - Covering and the Universal Covering` — type: definition (compound: covering as a fibre bundle with discrete fibre; lift of a map through a point; universal covering of a connected manifold); source items: D4.1.9, D4.1.10, D4.1.11, E4.1.7, T4.1.16, T4.1.18; prereqs: [Def - Fibre Bundle, Def - Principal G-Bundle, Def - Covering Space, Def - Lift of a Map, Def - Universal Cover, Def - Simply Connected Space]; spec: Bär's definition (a fibre bundle in the sense of `Def - Fibre Bundle` whose fibre is a discrete space) is the primary form; the page proves on the spot that this is equivalent to the AT II definition `Def - Covering Space` (evenly covered neighbourhoods) — a short two-direction argument: local triviality with discrete fibre $F$ gives $p^{-1}(U) \cong U \times F = \bigsqcup_{f \in F} U \times \{f\}$, and conversely an evenly covered neighbourhood gives a local trivialisation with the discrete fibre $p^{-1}(y)$ (the fibres are all homeomorphic over a connected base — prove or restrict to connected bases as Bär does). Lift through $\tilde y$ as in D4.1.11. Universal covering of a connected manifold $X$: a covering $\bar X \to X$ with $\bar X$ simply connected; the page states existence and uniqueness up to isomorphism as forward links to `Thm - Existence and Uniqueness of the Universal Covering` and links `Def - Universal Cover` (AT II) noting the definitions agree. Examples verified: T4.1.16 ($\exp : \mathbb{R} \to S^1$, $t \mapsto e^{2\pi i t}$, is a covering and a principal $\mathbb{Z}$-bundle with action $(t,k) \mapsto t + k$: exhibit the local trivialisations over the two arcs $S^1 \setminus \{1\}$ and $S^1 \setminus \{-1\}$ with explicit branch formulas and check the cocycle condition and the freeness/transitivity of the $\mathbb{Z}$-action on fibres, invoking `Def - Principal G-Bundle`); $\mathbb{R}^n \to T^n$ is a principal $\mathbb{Z}^n$-bundle (product of the previous); T4.1.18 ($z \mapsto z^k$ is a $k$-fold covering and a principal $\mathbb{Z}/k$-bundle: trivialisations over the same two arcs with the $k$ branches of the $k$-th root, verified). Non-example: $[0,1) \to S^1$, $t \mapsto e^{2\pi i t}$, is a continuous bijection which is not a covering (no evenly covered neighbourhood of $1$; verified). Calibration check: identify the fibre of $z \mapsto z^k$ over $1$, write the transition function of $\exp$ between the two arcs, explain why the universal covering of $S^1$ is $\mathbb{R}$ once `Thm - Higher Homotopy Groups of the Circle Vanish` and simple connectivity of $\mathbb{R}$ are known.
- `Thm - Lebesgue Number Lemma for Compact Metric Spaces` — type: theorem; source items: (none in Bär; needed by the flagged vault page's replacement); prereqs: [Def - Metric Space, Def - Compact Space]; statement: For every open cover $\mathcal{U}$ of a compact metric space $(K,d)$ there is $\delta > 0$ such that every subset of $K$ of diameter less than $\delta$ lies in some member of $\mathcal{U}$; proof: source: none; the page proves it by the standard finite-subcover argument (pick a finite subcover $U_1, \dots, U_m$, set $f(x) = \max_i d(x, K \setminus U_i)$, show $f$ is continuous and positive, take $\delta = \min f$; the case $K = U_i$ for some $i$ treated separately). Reference: Munkres, *Topology*, Lemma 27.5. spec: purely technical page; used by `Thm - Lifting Criterion for Coverings`, `Thm - Homotopy Lifting Property for Fibre Bundles`, `Thm - Seifert-van Kampen Theorem for Two Open Sets`, `Thm - Small Chains Theorem via Barycentric Subdivision`, `Thm - Existence and Uniqueness of the Universal Covering`.
- `Thm - Lifting Criterion for Coverings` — type: theorem; source items: T4.1.19, T4.1.20, I4.1.3 (the "⇐" direction and uniqueness of lifts); prereqs: [Def - Covering and the Universal Covering, Def - Homotopy Groups, Thm - Continuous Maps Induce Homomorphisms of Homotopy Groups, Thm - Path Lifting and Homotopy Lifting, Thm - Lebesgue Number Lemma for Compact Metric Spaces]; statement: Let $p : \tilde Y \to Y$ be a covering, $\tilde y \in \tilde Y$, $y = p(\tilde y)$, $X$ path connected and locally path connected, $x \in X$, $f \in C(X,Y)$ with $f(x) = y$. (i) A lift $\tilde f$ of $f$ through $\tilde y$ exists if and only if $f_\sharp(\pi_1(X,x)) \subset p_\sharp(\pi_1(\tilde Y, \tilde y))$. (ii) (Uniqueness) Two lifts of $f$ that agree at one point of the connected space $X$ agree everywhere. (iii) (Cor. 4.1.32) If $X$ is simply connected then every $f \in C(X,Y)$ lifts to every covering of $Y$, through any prescribed point of the fibre over $f(x)$; proof: source: "⇒" full, "⇐" omitted, local path-connectedness hypothesis missing (added here; Bär's manifolds satisfy it); the page proves "⇐" by defining $\tilde f(x') := \widetilde{f \circ \gamma}(1)$ for a path $\gamma$ from $x$ to $x'$ (path lifting from `Thm - Path Lifting and Homotopy Lifting`), independence of $\gamma$ via the hypothesis and homotopy lifting, continuity via local path connectedness and an evenly covered neighbourhood; uniqueness via the open-and-closed argument on the set where two lifts agree. Cross-reference `Thm - Lifting Criterion for Continuous Maps` (AT II, proof-complete) as the same result in the AT II formulation. Reference: Hatcher Prop. 1.33 and Prop. 1.34. spec: Sources: simply connected domain (iii); domain a sphere $S^n$, $n \ge 2$ (via `Thm - Spheres of Dimension at Least Two are Simply Connected`). Targets: $\pi_n(S^1) = 0$ for $n \ge 2$; orientability of simply connected manifolds (§12.8); uniqueness of universal coverings.
- `Thm - Seifert-van Kampen Theorem for Two Open Sets` — type: theorem; source items: I4.2.6 (the Seifert–van Kampen input Bär needs), used for T4.1.14, T4.2.17 and T5.3.3; prereqs: [Def - Homotopy Groups, Thm - Lebesgue Number Lemma for Compact Metric Spaces, Thm - The Fundamental Group is a Group, Def - Free Group and Free Product, Def - Normal Subgroup, Def - Quotient Group]; statement: Let $X = U \cup V$ with $U$, $V$ open and path connected, $U \cap V$ path connected, $x \in U \cap V$. Then the homomorphism $\Phi : \pi_1(U,x) * \pi_1(V,x) \to \pi_1(X,x)$ induced by the inclusions is surjective, and its kernel is the normal subgroup generated by the elements $i_{U\sharp}(\gamma)\, i_{V\sharp}(\gamma)^{-1}$, $\gamma \in \pi_1(U \cap V, x)$. Corollaries: (a) if $U \cap V$ is simply connected then $\pi_1(X,x) \cong \pi_1(U,x) * \pi_1(V,x)$; (b) if $U$ and $V$ are simply connected and $U \cap V$ is path connected then $X$ is simply connected; proof: source: none (cited as Seifert–van Kampen); the page proves surjectivity by the Lebesgue-number subdivision of a loop into pieces in $U$ or $V$ joined to $x$ inside $U \cap V$ (Hatcher, proof of Thm 1.20, first half), and the kernel statement by the two-open-set case of Hatcher's factorisation argument (subdivide a null-homotopy $I \times I \to X$ into small rectangles each mapped into $U$ or $V$, perturb the subdivision so that each vertex lies in $U$, $V$ or $U \cap V$ consistently, and rewrite the word step by step through the rectangles, each step being one of the two allowed moves — Hatcher pp. 45–46, specialised to two sets so that the "triple intersections" clause is vacuous); the free product and normal closure are taken from `Def - Free Group and Free Product`, `Def - Normal Subgroup`. The page names the flagged page `Thm - Seifert-van Kampen Theorem (Statement)` that it replaces for the series. Reference: Hatcher Thm 1.20 (two-set case). spec: Why-true: "a loop in $X$ is a product of loops in $U$ and $V$ because a compact interval only crosses the boundary between $U$ and $V$ finitely often, and the only relations come from loops in the overlap read in the two ways"; Targets: `Thm - Spheres of Dimension at Least Two are Simply Connected`, `Thm - Connected Sum of Simply Connected Manifolds is Simply Connected`, `Thm - Existence of an Exotic R^4` ($\pi_1(V) = 0$), and the exercise on $\pi_1(S^1 \vee S^1)$ used in §12.6.
- `Thm - Spheres of Dimension at Least Two are Simply Connected` — type: theorem; source items: T4.1.14, E4.1.6, T4.1.15 (the part $\pi_1(S^n) = 0$, $n \ge 2$); prereqs: [Thm - Seifert-van Kampen Theorem for Two Open Sets, Def - Homotopy and Homotopy Equivalence]; statement: For $n \ge 2$, $S^n$ is simply connected; proof: source omitted; the page covers $S^n = (S^n \setminus \{NP\}) \cup (S^n \setminus \{SP\})$, both homeomorphic to $\mathbb{R}^n$ by stereographic projection (explicit formulas) hence contractible, with intersection homeomorphic to $\mathbb{R}^n \setminus \{0\}$, path connected for $n \ge 2$ (explicit paths avoiding $0$), and applies the previous theorem. spec: Sources: any space covered by two contractible opens with connected intersection. Targets: lifting from spheres; $\pi_n(S^1) = 0$; $\pi_1$ of connected sums.
- `Thm - Existence and Uniqueness of the Universal Covering` — type: theorem; source items: T4.1.17, I4.1.2; prereqs: [Def - Covering and the Universal Covering, Thm - Lifting Criterion for Coverings, Thm - Path Lifting and Homotopy Lifting, Def - Topological Manifold]; statement: Every connected topological manifold $X$ (more generally every path connected, locally path connected, semilocally simply connected space) admits a covering $p : \bar X \to X$ with $\bar X$ simply connected; if $p' : \bar X' \to X$ is another such covering then for any $\bar x \in \bar X$, $\bar x' \in \bar X'$ over the same point there is a unique homeomorphism $h : \bar X \to \bar X'$ with $p' \circ h = p$ and $h(\bar x) = \bar x'$; if $X$ is a smooth manifold, $\bar X$ carries a unique smooth structure making $p$ a local diffeomorphism; proof: source omitted; the page follows Hatcher Thm 1.38 (construction of $\bar X$ as homotopy classes of paths from a basepoint, the basis of open sets $U_{[\gamma]}$, the verification that $p$ is a covering and that $\bar X$ is simply connected) and Prop. 1.37/1.34 for uniqueness (lift $p$ through $p'$ both ways, use uniqueness of lifts); manifolds are locally contractible (charts), hence semilocally simply connected; the smooth structure is pulled back through charts of $X$ restricted to evenly covered sets (verify the transition maps are smooth). Cross-reference `Def - Universal Cover` and `Thm - Galois Correspondence for Covering Spaces` (AT II) as the classification context. spec: Why-true: "a point of $\bar X$ is a point of $X$ together with a homotopy class of ways of reaching it; simple connectivity of $\bar X$ is the statement that a loop of ways is trivial". Targets: orientation covering (§12.8), flat connections and monodromy (chapter V anchor `Thm - Flat Connections and Monodromy Representations of the Fundamental Group`, which uses the universal covering).
- `Thm - Higher Homotopy Groups of the Circle Vanish` — type: theorem; source items: T4.1.21, E4.1.8; prereqs: [Thm - Lifting Criterion for Coverings, Thm - Spheres of Dimension at Least Two are Simply Connected, Ex - Homotopy Groups of a Contractible Space Vanish, Def - Covering and the Universal Covering]; statement: $\pi_n(S^1, 1) = \{1\}$ for all $n \ge 2$; proof: source full: given $u : (S^n, NP) \to (S^1, 1)$, lift through $0 \in \mathbb{R}$ (possible since $S^n$ is simply connected) to $\tilde u$, then $[u] = \exp_\sharp [\tilde u] = 0$ because $\pi_n(\mathbb{R}, 0) = 0$; the page writes it out with the covering $\exp$ verified on the Def page. spec: Sources: any space with a contractible covering space has vanishing $\pi_n$, $n \ge 2$ (the general statement is `Ex - Homotopy Groups of a Covering Agree Above Degree One`); Targets: $\pi_n(T^k) = 0$, $n \ge 2$; the Hopf-bundle computation of §12.6.
- `Ex - The Power Map is a k-Fold Covering` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: T4.1.18, E4.1.7; prereqs: [Def - Covering and the Universal Covering, Def - Principal G-Bundle]; spec: Prove that $S^1 \to S^1$, $z \mapsto z^k$ ($k \ge 1$) is a covering with fibre of cardinality $k$ and a principal $\mathbb{Z}/k\mathbb{Z}$-bundle for the action $z \cdot [m] = z e^{2\pi i m/k}$; write the two local trivialisations and the transition function; identify the induced map on $\pi_1(S^1) \cong \mathbb{Z}$ as multiplication by $k$ (using `Thm - Pi_1 of S^1 is Z`). Route: `Def - Covering and the Universal Covering`, `Def - Principal G-Bundle`, `Def - Transition Functions and the Cocycle Condition`.
- `Ex - Coverings of the Torus by Euclidean Space` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: T4.1.16 (torus part), E4.1.7; prereqs: [Def - Covering and the Universal Covering, Thm - Existence and Uniqueness of the Universal Covering]; spec: Show $\mathbb{R}^n \to T^n = (S^1)^n$, $(t_j) \mapsto (e^{2\pi i t_j})$, is a covering and a principal $\mathbb{Z}^n$-bundle, that it is the universal covering of $T^n$, and that the deck group is $\mathbb{Z}^n$ acting by translations; deduce $\pi_1(T^n) \cong \mathbb{Z}^n$ from the uniqueness statement and `Thm - Galois Correspondence for Covering Spaces`. Route: product of coverings is a covering (prove), simple connectivity of $\mathbb{R}^n$.
- `Ex - Maps from Simply Connected Spaces Lift` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: T4.1.20; prereqs: [Thm - Lifting Criterion for Coverings]; spec: Deduce Cor. 4.1.32 from the lifting criterion, then apply it to show that every map $S^n \to \mathbb{RP}^n$ ($n \ge 2$) lifts to $S^n$ and that every map $S^2 \to S^1$ is null-homotopic (lift to $\mathbb{R}$ and contract). Route: `Thm - Lifting Criterion for Coverings` (iii), `Thm - Spheres of Dimension at Least Two are Simply Connected`.
- `Ex - Higher Homotopy Groups of the Torus Vanish` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: T4.1.21 (extension), E4.1.8; prereqs: [Thm - Higher Homotopy Groups of the Circle Vanish, Thm - Lifting Criterion for Coverings, Ex - Coverings of the Torus by Euclidean Space]; spec: Prove $\pi_n(T^k) = 0$ for $n \ge 2$ in two ways: by lifting $S^n \to T^k$ to $\mathbb{R}^k$ (the covering exercise), and — after §12.3 — by `Ex - Homotopy Groups of a Product`; compare. Key decision: which covering to use and why simple connectivity of $S^n$ is the hypothesis that makes the lift exist.
- `Exercise Index - §12.2 Coverings, Lifting, and the Universal Covering` — lists: [Ex - The Power Map is a k-Fold Covering, Ex - Coverings of the Torus by Euclidean Space, Ex - Maps from Simply Connected Spaces Lift, Ex - Higher Homotopy Groups of the Torus Vanish]

### §12.3 Fibre Bundles and the Long Exact Homotopy Sequence
- `Def - Boundary Homomorphism of a Fibre Bundle` — type: definition; source items: D4.1.12 (→ link `Def - Exact Sequence of Groups`, AT III, for the definition of exactness; this page restates it), D4.1.13, R4.1.2; prereqs: [Def - Fibre Bundle, Def - Homotopy Groups, Def - Exact Sequence of Groups, Thm - Homotopy Lifting Property for Fibre Bundles]; spec: For a fibre bundle $p : E \to B$ (link `Def - Fibre Bundle`), $e_0 \in E$, $b_0 = p(e_0)$, $F = p^{-1}(b_0)$ with $J^{n-1} := \overline{\partial I^n \setminus (I^{n-1} \times \{0\})}$: given $u \in C(I^n, B)$ with $u(\partial I^n) = \{b_0\}$, the homotopy lifting property (forward link) applied to the homotopy $u : I^{n-1} \times I \to B$ (last coordinate as time, in the reversed direction so that the lift starts on $I^{n-1} \times \{1\}$) with initial lift the constant map $e_0$ on $I^{n-1} \times \{1\}$ — more precisely, the relative version with the lift prescribed on $J^{n-1}$ — yields $\tilde u \in C(I^n, E)$ with $p \circ \tilde u = u$ and $\tilde u(J^{n-1}) = \{e_0\}$; define $\partial [u] := [\tilde u|_{I^{n-1} \times \{0\}}] \in \pi_{n-1}(F, e_0)$. Bär's description (lifting the family of straight lines from $I^{n-1} \times \{1\}$ continuously in the initial point, R4.1.2 and figure) is given as the mental picture, and the page explains that continuity in the initial point is exactly the homotopy lifting property. The page proves: (a) $\tilde u|_{I^{n-1} \times \{0\}}$ maps $\partial I^{n-1}$ to $e_0$ and lands in $F$; (b) $\partial[u]$ is independent of the choice of lift $\tilde u$ and of the representative $u$ (both via the relative homotopy lifting property applied to $I^n \times I$ with the lift prescribed on $J^n$ — the standard argument, Hatcher p. 376 or Bredon VII.6); (c) $\partial$ is a homomorphism for $n \ge 2$ (lift $u_1 * u_2$ by concatenating lifts in the first coordinate). Examples: the trivial bundle has $\partial = 0$ (verified: lift $u$ as $(u, e_0)$); the covering $\exp : \mathbb{R} \to S^1$ has $\partial : \pi_1(S^1, 1) \to \pi_0(\mathbb{Z}, 0) = \mathbb{Z}$ sending the standard generator to $1$ (verified by lifting $t \mapsto e^{2\pi i t}$). Calibration check: compute $\partial$ for $z \mapsto z^k$; explain why $\partial$ lands in the fibre.
- `Thm - Homotopy Lifting Property for Fibre Bundles` — type: theorem; source items: I4.1.3 (fibre-bundle part), I4.1.4 (continuity of the lifted family); prereqs: [Def - Fibre Bundle, Thm - Lebesgue Number Lemma for Compact Metric Spaces, Def - Homotopy and Homotopy Equivalence]; statement: Let $p : E \to B$ be a fibre bundle (locally trivial, with any fibre $F$). (i) For every $n \ge 0$, every homotopy $G : I^n \times I \to B$ and every lift $g_0 : I^n \times \{0\} \to E$ of $G|_{I^n \times \{0\}}$ there is a homotopy $\tilde G : I^n \times I \to E$ with $p \circ \tilde G = G$ and $\tilde G|_{I^n \times \{0\}} = g_0$. (ii) (Relative form) The same holds with the lift prescribed on $I^n \times \{0\} \cup \partial I^n \times I$; equivalently, since $(I^n \times I, I^n \times \{0\} \cup \partial I^n \times I) \cong (I^n \times I, I^n \times \{0\})$, on $J^n$. (iii) Consequently $p$ has the homotopy lifting property with respect to all CW complexes — not needed and not proved; the page states only (i)–(ii); proof: source: asserted; the page follows Hatcher Prop. 4.48 (fibre bundles are Serre fibrations): Lebesgue-number subdivision of $I^n \times I$ into small cubes mapped into trivialising opens, and inductive lifting cube by cube in a fixed order using the trivialisation $p^{-1}(U) \cong U \times F$ where lifting is projection onto the second factor composed with a retraction $I^n \times I \to I^n \times \{0\} \cup \partial I^n \times I$ (explicit radial retraction formula); the tube lemma needed to ensure a subdivision exists is re-proved inline as Lemma 1 (the vault page is flagged); the homeomorphism of pairs in (ii) is written explicitly (Hatcher p. 376, "$J^n$" trick). Reference: Hatcher Prop. 4.48 and the paragraph following Thm 4.41; Bredon VII.6; Steenrod §11. spec: Why-true: "locally a bundle is a product and products lift trivially; compactness of the cube makes the local lifts finite in number and the retraction glues them". Sources: coverings (discrete fibre) — then the lift is unique; principal bundles; associated bundles. Targets: the boundary homomorphism, the long exact sequence, and — in chapter XIII — homotopy invariance of pull-back bundles is NOT derived here (that is chapter III's business).
- `Thm - Long Exact Homotopy Sequence of a Fibre Bundle` — type: theorem; source items: T4.1.22, I4.1.4, R4.1.2; prereqs: [Def - Boundary Homomorphism of a Fibre Bundle, Thm - Homotopy Lifting Property for Fibre Bundles, Thm - Continuous Maps Induce Homomorphisms of Homotopy Groups, Def - Exact Sequence of Groups]; statement: For a fibre bundle $p : E \to B$, $e_0 \in E$, $b_0 = p(e_0)$, $F = E_{b_0}$, $\iota : F \hookrightarrow E$, the sequence $\cdots \xrightarrow{\partial} \pi_n(F, e_0) \xrightarrow{\iota_\sharp} \pi_n(E, e_0) \xrightarrow{p_\sharp} \pi_n(B, b_0) \xrightarrow{\partial} \pi_{n-1}(F, e_0) \xrightarrow{\iota_\sharp} \cdots \xrightarrow{p_\sharp} \pi_1(B, b_0) \xrightarrow{\partial} \pi_0(F, e_0) \xrightarrow{\iota_\sharp} \pi_0(E, e_0)$ is exact (exactness at the $\pi_0$ terms in the sense of pointed sets); proof: source: only $\mathrm{im}\, \iota_\sharp \subset \ker p_\sharp$ and the construction of $\partial$; the page proves exactness at all six positions in each degree following Bredon VII.6 Thm 6.7 directly from the (relative) homotopy lifting property (each inclusion of kernel in image is a lifting argument with an explicit homotopy; each inclusion of image in kernel is a one-line computation), with the pointed-set statements at the end handled separately; optionally records Hatcher Thm 4.41 (via relative homotopy groups) as an alternative route in a remark. Cross-reference `Thm - Long Exact Sequence of a Fibration` (AT III) for Serre fibrations; the page proves the fibre-bundle case in full independently. spec: Why-true: "a map to $B$ that lifts to $E$ and starts in the fibre is a loop in the fibre up to the ambiguity measured by $\partial$". Sources: principal bundles (fibre $G$), coverings (fibre discrete, so $\pi_n(\tilde Y) \cong \pi_n(Y)$ for $n \ge 2$), the Hopf bundle, the frame bundle of a manifold. Targets: `Thm - Third Homotopy Group of the Two-Sphere`; $\pi_2$ of a Lie group; chapter III's classification arguments; chapter XIII's `Thm - Even Intersection Form iff Spin` (clutching argument uses $\pi_1(SO(n))$ facts from chapter VIII).
- `Ex - Homotopy Groups of a Product` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: T4.1.23, E4.1.9; prereqs: [Thm - Long Exact Homotopy Sequence of a Fibre Bundle, Def - Boundary Homomorphism of a Fibre Bundle]; spec: For the trivial bundle $E = B \times F$ with $\hat p : E \to F$ the second projection, show $\iota_\sharp$ is injective and $\hat p_\sharp$ surjective (from $\hat p \circ \iota = \mathrm{id}_F$), deduce $\partial = 0$ and short exact sequences $0 \to \pi_n(F) \to \pi_n(E) \to \pi_n(B) \to 0$, and that $p_\sharp \times \hat p_\sharp : \pi_n(B \times F, (b_0, e_0)) \to \pi_n(B, b_0) \times \pi_n(F, e_0)$ is an isomorphism (Bär's injectivity argument: $p_\sharp x = 0 \Rightarrow x = \iota_\sharp y$ and $0 = \hat p_\sharp \iota_\sharp y = y$). Then give the direct proof without the sequence (components of a map to a product). Key decision: recognising a section of $p$ forces the sequence to split.
- `Ex - Homotopy Groups of the Hopf Bundle in Low Degrees` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: T4.1.24 (partial: the sequence itself), E4.1.10; prereqs: [Thm - Long Exact Homotopy Sequence of a Fibre Bundle, Def - The Hopf Bundle, Thm - Higher Homotopy Groups of the Circle Vanish, Thm - Spheres of Dimension at Least Two are Simply Connected]; spec: From $S^1 \to S^3 \xrightarrow{H} S^2$ (link `Def - The Hopf Bundle`, chapter III) derive $\pi_2(S^2) \cong \pi_1(S^1) \cong \mathbb{Z}$ (using $\pi_2(S^3) = 0$ and $\pi_1(S^3) = 0$ — the former is only available after §12.6; state it as a hypothesis here and close the loop in `Thm - Third Homotopy Group of the Two-Sphere`) and $H_\sharp : \pi_k(S^3) \to \pi_k(S^2)$ is an isomorphism for all $k \ge 3$. Route: exactness plus `Thm - Higher Homotopy Groups of the Circle Vanish`. Companion: `Ex - Long Exact Sequence of the Hopf Fibration` (AT III).
- `Ex - Homotopy Groups of a Covering Agree Above Degree One` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: (standard; generalises T4.1.21); prereqs: [Thm - Long Exact Homotopy Sequence of a Fibre Bundle, Def - Covering and the Universal Covering]; spec: For a covering $p : \tilde Y \to Y$ prove $p_\sharp : \pi_n(\tilde Y, \tilde y) \to \pi_n(Y, y)$ is an isomorphism for $n \ge 2$ and injective for $n = 1$, using that a discrete fibre has $\pi_k = 0$ for $k \ge 1$; recover $\pi_n(S^1) = 0$ and $\pi_n(\mathbb{RP}^m) \cong \pi_n(S^m)$ for $n \ge 2$.
- `Exercise Index - §12.3 Fibre Bundles and the Long Exact Homotopy Sequence` — lists: [Ex - Homotopy Groups of a Product, Ex - Homotopy Groups of the Hopf Bundle in Low Degrees, Ex - Homotopy Groups of a Covering Agree Above Degree One]

### §12.4 Singular Homology, Homotopy Invariance, and Excision
- `Def - Chain Complex and Its Homology` — type: definition (compound: chain complex of abelian groups or $R$-modules, cycles, boundaries, homology, chain map, chain homotopy); source items: D4.2.1, D4.2.2, T4.2.1; prereqs: [Def - Abelian Group, Def - Kernel and Image, Def - Quotient Group, Def - Chain Map and Chain Homotopy, Def - Exact Sequence and Short Exact Sequence]; spec: Bär's Definition 4.2.1 ($\mathrm{im}\, f_k \subset \ker f_{k-1}$, i.e. $f_{k-1} \circ f_k = 0$) and $H_k := \ker f_k / \mathrm{im}\, f_{k+1}$; chain maps and chain homotopies restated from `Def - Chain Map and Chain Homotopy` (linked). Corollaries proved on the page: T4.2.1 (a complex is exact iff all $H_k = 0$); chain maps induce maps on homology; chain homotopic chain maps induce the same map on homology (the three-line computation $f - g = \partial P + P \partial$ kills cycles modulo boundaries). Examples: the complex $0 \leftarrow R \xleftarrow{0} R \xleftarrow{1} R \xleftarrow{0} \cdots$ of a point (verified: $H_0 = R$, $H_k = 0$ otherwise — this is used again on `Thm - Homology of a Point and of a Path-Connected Space`); a non-example: a sequence with $f_{k-1} f_k \ne 0$. Calibration check: compute $H_*$ of $0 \to \mathbb{Z} \xrightarrow{2} \mathbb{Z} \to 0$.
- `Def - Singular Homology with Coefficients` — type: definition (compound: standard simplex and its sides, singular simplex, singular chains with coefficients in a commutative ring $R$ with unit, boundary operator, cycles, boundaries, $H_n(X;R)$, induced maps); source items: D4.2.3, D4.2.4, D4.2.5, D4.2.6, E4.2.1, R4.2.1, T4.2.2 (→ link `Thm - d-Squared-is-Zero for Singular Boundaries`), T4.2.3, T4.2.4(1), T4.2.6; prereqs: [Def - Chain Complex and Its Homology, Def - The Standard p-Simplex, Def - Singular Simplex, Def - Singular Chain, Def - The Boundary Operator, Thm - d-Squared-is-Zero for Singular Boundaries, Def - Singular Homology, Def - Free Module]; spec: $\Delta^n = \{(t_0, \dots, t_n) \in \mathbb{R}^{n+1} \mid t_i \ge 0, \sum t_i = 1\}$, sides $\iota^n_k : \Delta^{n-1} \to \Delta^n$ inserting $0$ in position $k$, $C_n(X;R)$ the free $R$-module on $C(\Delta^n, X)$, $\partial_n \sigma = \sum_{k=0}^n (-1)^k \sigma \circ \iota^n_k$ (E4.2.1 pictures of $\Delta^0, \Delta^1, \Delta^2$ and the five side maps written as formulas), $Z_n$, $B_n$, $H_n(X;R)$. $\partial \circ \partial = 0$ is wikilinked to the proof-complete AT I page (its statement restated). Proved on the page: T4.2.3 ($f_* \partial = \partial f_*$, since $f \circ (\sigma \circ \iota^n_k) = (f \circ \sigma) \circ \iota^n_k$), so $f_*$ is a chain map and $f_*[z] := [f_* z]$ is well defined (R4.2.1); T4.2.4(1) functoriality; T4.2.6 ($C_n(\emptyset; R) = 0$). Convention callout: Bär allows any commutative ring with unit; the page fixes the same and notes that $H_n(X;\mathbb{Z}) \otimes R \to H_n(X;R)$ is not in general an isomorphism (example $\mathbb{RP}^2$ with $R = \mathbb{Z}/2$, stated as a warning with a forward link to the universal coefficient page, not as a proved claim). Relation to `Def - Singular Homology` (AT I, integer coefficients): same complex tensored with $R$. Examples: singular $0$-simplices are points and $H_0$ counts path components (forward link); a non-example: a map $\Delta^n \to X$ that is not continuous is not a simplex. Calibration check: write $\partial$ of a singular $2$-simplex, verify $\partial \partial \sigma = 0$ for $n = 2$ by hand, check $f_*$ on a $1$-cycle.
- `Thm - Homology of a Point and of a Path-Connected Space` — type: theorem; source items: T4.2.4(3), T4.2.8, I4.2.4; prereqs: [Def - Singular Homology with Coefficients, Def - Chain Complex and Its Homology, Def - Path-Connected Space]; statement: (i) $H_n(\{p\}; R) = R$ for $n = 0$ and $0$ for $n \ge 1$. (ii) For any space $X$, $H_0(X;R)$ is the free $R$-module on the set of path components of $X$; in particular $H_0(X;R) \cong R$ when $X$ is path connected, the isomorphism $\varepsilon : H_0(X;R) \to R$, $\sum \alpha_k x_k \mapsto \sum \alpha_k$, being the augmentation; proof: source: (i) full (Bär's computation: one simplex $\sigma_n$ per degree, $\partial \sigma_n = \sigma_{n-1}$ or $0$ according to the parity of $n$, so the complex is $0 \leftarrow R \xleftarrow{0} R \xleftarrow{1} R \xleftarrow{0} \cdots$), (ii) omitted; the page proves (ii) via the augmentation: $\varepsilon$ is surjective, its kernel is $\mathrm{im}\, \partial_1$ (a $0$-chain $\sum \alpha_k x_k$ with $\sum \alpha_k = 0$ is a boundary of a sum of paths from a fixed base point when $X$ is path connected), and the general case splits over path components (Ex in this section). Reference: Hatcher Prop. 2.6, 2.7. spec: Sources: connected manifolds are path connected; Targets: every Mayer–Vietoris computation of §12.5 starts from this page.
- `Thm - Homotopy Invariance of Singular Homology (Gauge Theory)` — type: theorem; source items: T4.2.4(2), I4.2.2, T4.2.5; prereqs: [Def - Singular Homology with Coefficients, Def - Chain Complex and Its Homology, Def - Homotopy and Homotopy Equivalence]; statement: If $f, g : X \to Y$ are homotopic then $f_* = g_* : H_n(X;R) \to H_n(Y;R)$ for all $n$; consequently a homotopy equivalence induces isomorphisms on all $H_n(\cdot;R)$ and $X \simeq Y$ implies $H_n(X;R) \cong H_n(Y;R)$ (T4.2.5); proof: source: omitted ("to be done in a lecture course"); the page follows Hatcher Thm 2.10: the prism operator $P : C_n(X) \to C_{n+1}(Y)$, $P(\sigma) = \sum_i (-1)^i F \circ (\sigma \times \mathrm{id}) |_{[v_0, \dots, v_i, w_i, \dots, w_n]}$, with the subdivision of $\Delta^n \times I$ into the $n+1$ simplices $[v_0, \dots, v_i, w_i, \dots, w_n]$ written out with explicit affine parametrisations; (Lemma 1) the geometric identity $\partial(\Delta^n \times I) = \Delta^n \times \{1\} - \Delta^n \times \{0\} - \partial\Delta^n \times I$ at the level of the subdivided chains (every cancelling pair of interior faces identified explicitly); (Lemma 2) $\partial P + P \partial = g_* - f_*$ (the term-by-term computation, each cancellation justified); then the corollary from `Def - Chain Complex and Its Homology`. The page names the flagged AT I page it replaces. Reference: Hatcher Thm 2.10 (pp. 111–113). spec: Why-true: "a homotopy sweeps a simplex through a prism, and the prism's boundary is the difference of the two ends plus the swept boundary"; Sources: deformation retracts, contractible spaces, the radial homotopy equivalences $F_{x,r}$ of §12.8; Targets: every computation in §12.5.
- `Def - Relative Singular Homology` — type: definition; source items: (Hatcher §2.1; needed for excision and local homology, not in Bär); prereqs: [Def - Singular Homology with Coefficients, Def - Chain Complex and Its Homology]; spec: For $A \subset X$, $C_n(X, A; R) := C_n(X;R)/C_n(A;R)$ with the induced boundary (well defined since $\partial C_n(A) \subset C_{n-1}(A)$, verified), $H_n(X, A; R)$; maps of pairs induce maps; the short exact sequence $0 \to C_*(A) \to C_*(X) \to C_*(X,A) \to 0$ of chain complexes; reduced homology $\tilde H_n$ (link `Def - Augmentation and Reduced Homology`) and $H_n(X, x_0) \cong \tilde H_n(X)$ as a proved corollary (after the pair sequence page — forward link). Examples: $H_n(X, X) = 0$ (verified), $H_n(X, \emptyset) = H_n(X)$ (verified); non-example: $H_n(X,A)$ is not $H_n(X)/H_n(A)$ in general (the pair $(D^2, S^1)$, stated with a forward link to the computation on `Thm - Long Exact Sequence of a Pair in Singular Homology`). Calibration check: describe a relative $1$-cycle in $(D^2, S^1)$; explain why a chain in $A$ is zero in $C_n(X,A)$.
- `Thm - Zig-Zag Lemma for Short Exact Sequences of Chain Complexes` — type: theorem; source items: (Hatcher Thm 2.16, needed for the pair sequence and Mayer–Vietoris); prereqs: [Def - Chain Complex and Its Homology, Def - Exact Sequence and Short Exact Sequence]; statement: A short exact sequence $0 \to A_\bullet \xrightarrow{i} B_\bullet \xrightarrow{j} C_\bullet \to 0$ of chain complexes of $R$-modules induces a long exact sequence $\cdots \to H_n(A) \xrightarrow{i_*} H_n(B) \xrightarrow{j_*} H_n(C) \xrightarrow{\partial} H_{n-1}(A) \to \cdots$, with $\partial [c] := [i^{-1} \partial j^{-1} c]$; the construction is natural with respect to maps of short exact sequences; proof: source: none; the page gives the full diagram chase: well-definedness of $\partial$ (choice of preimage under $j$, choice of representative), $\partial$ is a homomorphism, exactness at the three spots (six inclusions, each written out), naturality. Reference: Hatcher Thm 2.16, pp. 116–117. spec: Why-true: "the connecting map is the boundary that the quotient hid"; Targets: pair sequence, Mayer–Vietoris, universal coefficients (§12.7), compactly supported cohomology (§12.8).
- `Thm - Long Exact Sequence of a Pair in Singular Homology` — type: theorem; source items: (Hatcher Thm 2.16 applied to pairs); prereqs: [Def - Relative Singular Homology, Thm - Zig-Zag Lemma for Short Exact Sequences of Chain Complexes, Thm - Homotopy Invariance of Singular Homology (Gauge Theory)]; statement: For $A \subset X$: $\cdots \to H_n(A;R) \to H_n(X;R) \to H_n(X,A;R) \xrightarrow{\partial} H_{n-1}(A;R) \to \cdots \to H_0(X,A;R) \to 0$ is exact and natural; the same with reduced homology when $A \ne \emptyset$; $\partial$ sends the class of a relative cycle $z$ to the class of $\partial z$; the long exact sequence of a triple $(X, A, B)$ likewise; if $A$ is a deformation retract of $X$ then $H_n(X, A) = 0$; and $H_n(D^k, S^{k-1}; R) \cong \tilde H_{n-1}(S^{k-1}; R)$; proof: page applies the zig-zag lemma, proves the reduced version and the triple version (Hatcher p. 118), and the deformation-retract corollary via homotopy invariance and exactness; the disc computation is deferred to §12.5 (needs sphere homology) and stated there. Reference: Hatcher §2.1 pp. 117–118. spec: Targets: local homology $H_n(M, M \setminus \{x\})$ in §12.8; the relative fundamental class.
- `Thm - Small Chains Theorem via Barycentric Subdivision` — type: theorem; source items: (Hatcher Prop. 2.21; the engine behind I4.2.3 and excision); prereqs: [Def - Singular Homology with Coefficients, Def - Chain Complex and Its Homology, Thm - Lebesgue Number Lemma for Compact Metric Spaces]; statement: Let $\mathcal{U} = \{U_j\}$ be a family of subsets of $X$ whose interiors cover $X$, and $C_n^{\mathcal{U}}(X;R) \subset C_n(X;R)$ the submodule spanned by simplices with image in some $U_j$. Then the inclusion $\iota : C_\bullet^{\mathcal{U}}(X) \to C_\bullet(X)$ is a chain homotopy equivalence: there is a chain map $\rho : C_\bullet(X) \to C_\bullet^{\mathcal{U}}(X)$ with $\rho \iota = \mathrm{id}$ and $\mathrm{id} - \iota \rho = \partial D + D \partial$ for a chain homotopy $D$; moreover $\rho$ and $D$ are natural and take chains in $A$ to chains in $A$ for any $A \subset X$; proof: source: none; the page follows Hatcher Prop. 2.21 in its four steps as four lemmas: (Lemma 1) barycentric subdivision of linear chains $S : LC_n(Y) \to LC_n(Y)$ with $\partial S = S \partial$ and the cone operator $b : LC_n \to LC_{n+1}$ with $\partial b + b \partial = \mathrm{id} - [b]$ (explicit formulas, induction on $n$); (Lemma 2) the chain homotopy $T$ with $\partial T + T \partial = \mathrm{id} - S$ on linear chains; (Lemma 3) the diameter estimate: each simplex of $S(\Delta^n)$ has diameter at most $\tfrac{n}{n+1} \operatorname{diam}(\Delta^n)$ (the geometric lemma on barycentres, proved with the explicit inequality); (Lemma 4) transport to singular chains by $S\sigma := \sigma_* S(\Delta^n)$, $T\sigma := \sigma_* T(\Delta^n)$, and, for each $\sigma$, the least $m(\sigma)$ with $S^{m}\sigma \in C^{\mathcal{U}}$ (exists by the Lebesgue number lemma applied to $\sigma^{-1}(\mathcal{U})$ on $\Delta^n$), $D\sigma := \sum_{0 \le i < m(\sigma)} T S^i \sigma$, $\rho := \mathrm{id} - \partial D - D \partial$; verification that $\rho$ lands in $C^{\mathcal{U}}$, is a chain map, and $\rho \iota = \mathrm{id}$ (since $m = 0$ on $C^{\mathcal{U}}$). Reference: Hatcher Prop. 2.21, pp. 119–124. spec: Why-true: "subdividing enough times makes every simplex small, and subdivision is chain-homotopic to the identity"; Targets: excision, Mayer–Vietoris, the proof of Poincaré duality via small chains is not needed (Hatcher's PD proof uses MV for $H_c$, which uses excision).
- `Thm - Excision Theorem for Singular Homology` — type: theorem; source items: (Hatcher Thm 2.20; needed for local homology and Poincaré duality); prereqs: [Thm - Small Chains Theorem via Barycentric Subdivision, Def - Relative Singular Homology, Thm - Long Exact Sequence of a Pair in Singular Homology]; statement: (i) If $Z \subset A \subset X$ with $\overline{Z} \subset \operatorname{int} A$, the inclusion $(X \setminus Z, A \setminus Z) \hookrightarrow (X, A)$ induces isomorphisms $H_n(X \setminus Z, A \setminus Z; R) \cong H_n(X, A; R)$ for all $n$. (ii) Equivalently, for $A, B \subset X$ with $\operatorname{int} A \cup \operatorname{int} B = X$, the inclusion $(B, A \cap B) \hookrightarrow (X, A)$ induces isomorphisms on homology; proof: source: none; the page derives (ii) from the small chains theorem with $\mathcal{U} = \{A, B\}$ (the quotient $C^{\mathcal{U}}_n(X)/C_n(A) \cong C_n(B)/C_n(A \cap B)$ as complexes, and the chain equivalence $C^{\mathcal{U}}(X) \simeq C(X)$ descends to the quotients by $C(A)$ because $\rho$ and $D$ preserve $C(A)$ — Hatcher's argument via the five lemma is replaced by the direct chain-homotopy argument Hatcher gives on p. 124), and (i) from (ii) with $B = X \setminus Z$. Reference: Hatcher Thm 2.20 and the proof on p. 124. spec: Targets: local homology of a manifold; the Mayer–Vietoris sequence; Poincaré duality.
- `Ex - Homology of the Empty Set and of a Disjoint Union` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: T4.2.6, T4.2.7; prereqs: [Def - Singular Homology with Coefficients, Thm - Homology of a Point and of a Path-Connected Space]; spec: Prove $H_n(\emptyset; R) = 0$ and, for $X = X_0 \sqcup X_1$ (topological sum), $H_n(X_0;R) \oplus H_n(X_1;R) \cong H_n(X;R)$ by showing the chain complex of $X$ is the direct sum of the chain complexes (every simplex has connected image, hence lies in one summand) — Bär's route via Mayer–Vietoris with empty intersection is given as a second solution after §12.5. Route: connectedness of $\Delta^n$ (link `Thm - Continuous Image of a Connected Space`), `Def - Disjoint Union (Topological Sum)`.
- `Ex - Chain Homotopic Chain Maps Induce Equal Maps on Homology` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: (drills `Def - Chain Complex and Its Homology`); prereqs: [Def - Chain Complex and Its Homology]; spec: Prove that if $f - g = \partial P + P \partial$ then $f_* = g_*$ on homology, and that chain homotopy equivalences induce isomorphisms; then use it to show a chain complex with a contracting homotopy ($\mathrm{id} = \partial P + P \partial$) is exact. Route: definitions only.
- `Ex - Relative Homology of a Pair with a Deformation Retract` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: (drills the pair sequence); prereqs: [Thm - Long Exact Sequence of a Pair in Singular Homology, Thm - Homotopy Invariance of Singular Homology (Gauge Theory)]; spec: Show $H_n(X, A; R) = 0$ for all $n$ when $A$ is a deformation retract of $X$; compute $H_n(D^k, D^k \setminus \{0\}; R)$ from this and the pair sequence once `Thm - Homology of Spheres` is available (state that dependency; give the $k = 1$ case by hand now: $H_1(D^1, D^1 \setminus \{0\}) \cong R$). Key decision: exactness plus an isomorphism on two out of three terms forces the third to vanish.
- `Exercise Index - §12.4 Singular Homology, Homotopy Invariance, and Excision` — lists: [Ex - Homology of the Empty Set and of a Disjoint Union, Ex - Chain Homotopic Chain Maps Induce Equal Maps on Homology, Ex - Relative Homology of a Pair with a Deformation Retract]

### §12.5 Mayer–Vietoris and Homology Computations
- `Thm - Mayer-Vietoris Sequence for Singular Homology (Gauge Theory)` — type: theorem; source items: T4.2.4(4), I4.2.3, T4.2.7 (second proof); prereqs: [Thm - Small Chains Theorem via Barycentric Subdivision, Thm - Zig-Zag Lemma for Short Exact Sequences of Chain Complexes, Def - Singular Homology with Coefficients]; statement: For open $X_0, X_1 \subset X$ with $X_0 \cup X_1 = X$, $j_\nu : X_\nu \hookrightarrow X$, $i_\nu : X_0 \cap X_1 \hookrightarrow X_\nu$, the sequence $\cdots \to H_n(X_0 \cap X_1; R) \xrightarrow{(i^0_*, i^1_*)} H_n(X_0;R) \oplus H_n(X_1;R) \xrightarrow{j^0_* - j^1_*} H_n(X;R) \xrightarrow{\partial} H_{n-1}(X_0 \cap X_1; R) \to \cdots \to H_0(X;R) \to 0$ is exact and natural; the reduced version is exact when $X_0 \cap X_1 \ne \emptyset$; $\partial [z]$ is computed by writing $z = z_0 + z_1$ with $z_\nu \in C_n(X_\nu)$ and taking $[\partial z_0] = -[\partial z_1]$; proof: source: omitted; the page proves it from the short exact sequence $0 \to C_n(X_0 \cap X_1) \to C_n(X_0) \oplus C_n(X_1) \to C_n^{\{X_0, X_1\}}(X) \to 0$ (exactness verified directly), the zig-zag lemma, and the small chains theorem identifying $H_n(C^{\mathcal{U}}(X)) \cong H_n(X)$; names the flagged AT I page it replaces; Bär's sign convention $(j^0_*, -j^1_*)$ is adopted (conventions: the middle map is $(a,b) \mapsto j^0_* a - j^1_* b$) with a Convention callout that Hatcher uses the same sign. Reference: Hatcher pp. 149–150. spec: Why-true: "a cycle of $X$ is, after subdivision, a sum of a chain in $X_0$ and a chain in $X_1$, and their common boundary is the connecting class"; Targets: every computation below; Bär's T4.3.7 (fundamental class); K3's Euler characteristic (chapter XIII).
- `Thm - Homology of Spheres` — type: theorem; source items: T4.2.9, E4.2.2, I4.1.1 (the homological input for $\pi_n(S^n)$); prereqs: [Thm - Mayer-Vietoris Sequence for Singular Homology (Gauge Theory), Thm - Homotopy Invariance of Singular Homology (Gauge Theory), Thm - Homology of a Point and of a Path-Connected Space, Ex - Homology of the Empty Set and of a Disjoint Union]; statement: For $m \ge 1$: $H_k(S^m; R) \cong R$ for $k \in \{0, m\}$ and $0$ otherwise; $H_k(S^0;R) \cong R^2$ for $k = 0$ and $0$ otherwise; in reduced form $\tilde H_k(S^m;R) \cong R$ for $k = m$ and $0$ otherwise, for all $m \ge 0$. Consequently $H_n(D^k, S^{k-1}; R) \cong R$ for $n = k$ and $0$ otherwise, and $\tilde H_n(\mathbb{R}^k \setminus \{0\}; R) \cong \tilde H_n(S^{k-1};R)$; proof: source full (Bär's three-step induction with $D_\pm = S^m \setminus \{\mp \text{pole}\}$, $D_+ \cap D_- \simeq S^{m-1}$; the $S^1$ step: with the adopted sign convention the map $H_0(D_+ \cap D_-) \cong R^2 \to H_0(D_+) \oplus H_0(D_-) \cong R^2$ is $(a,b) \mapsto (a + b, a + b)$, Bär's matrix $\begin{pmatrix} 1 & 1 \\ 1 & 1 \end{pmatrix}$, so $H_1(S^1) \cong \ker = \{(x,-x)\} \cong R$; the writer re-derives the matrix from the chosen sign convention rather than copying it); the page writes out the explicit homotopy equivalences $D_\pm \simeq \{\text{pt}\}$ and $D_+ \cap D_- \simeq S^{m-1}$ (radial deformation via stereographic coordinates), and the two corollaries via the pair sequence and homotopy invariance. Cross-reference `Thm - Singular Homology of the Sphere` (AT I) and `Ex - Computing H_n of S^n via Mayer-Vietoris`. spec: Targets: $\pi_n(S^n)$ via Hurewicz, no-retraction theorem, local homology of $\mathbb{R}^n$, the degree.
- `Thm - Homology of Complex Projective Space` — type: theorem; source items: T4.2.10, E4.2.3, I4.2.7 (the two homotopy equivalences); prereqs: [Thm - Mayer-Vietoris Sequence for Singular Homology (Gauge Theory), Thm - Homology of Spheres, Def - Homotopy and Homotopy Equivalence]; statement: $H_k(\mathbb{CP}^n; R) \cong R$ for $k = 0, 2, 4, \dots, 2n$ and $0$ otherwise; moreover the inclusion $\mathbb{CP}^{n-1} \hookrightarrow \mathbb{CP}^n$ induces isomorphisms on $H_k$ for $k \le 2n - 2$; proof: source full modulo the asserted homotopy equivalences; the page uses Bär's cover: in the affine chart $\mathbb{C}^n + e_{n+1} \subset \mathbb{C}^{n+1}$ (lines not contained in $\{z_{n+1} = 0\}$ meet it in exactly one point), $X_0 := \{\ell \mid \ell \cap B_2 \ne \emptyset\} \cong B_2$ (contractible) and $X_1 := \{\ell \mid \ell \cap \overline{B_1} = \emptyset\}$ which deformation retracts onto $\mathbb{CP}^{n-1} = \{z_{n+1} = 0\}$ (explicit deformation: scale the affine part outward, $\ell = [w : 1] \mapsto [w : t]$, $t \to 0$; continuity checked in homogeneous coordinates), $X_0 \cap X_1 \cong B_2 \setminus \overline{B_1} \simeq S^{2n-1}$; MV then gives Bär's isomorphisms, with the $k = 1$ step corrected: the map $H_0(S^{2n-1}) \to H_0(\{p\}) \oplus H_0(\mathbb{CP}^{n-1})$ is injective `(source typo 24: the source writes $H_0(S^1;R)$ for $H_0(S^{2n-1};R)$)`; the base case $\mathbb{CP}^1 \cong S^2$ (explicit homeomorphism via $[z_0 : z_1] \mapsto$ stereographic coordinates). spec: Targets: chapter XIII ($H_2(\mathbb{CP}^2) \cong \mathbb{Z}$ generated by a line), E4.3.5.
- `Def - Connected Sum of Manifolds` — type: definition; source items: D4.2.7; prereqs: [Def - Topological Manifold, Def - Smooth Manifold, Def - Manifold with Boundary and Induced Orientation, Def - Orientation of a Smooth Manifold]; spec: For connected $n$-manifolds $M$, $N$ and embeddings $\phi_M : \bar B^n \to M$, $\phi_N : \bar B^n \to N$ (orientation-preserving and orientation-reversing respectively when $M, N$ are oriented, so that $M \# N$ is oriented compatibly with $M \setminus \phi_M(B^n)$ and $N \setminus \phi_N(B^n)$), $M \# N := (M \setminus \phi_M(B^n)) \cup_{\partial} (N \setminus \phi_N(B^n))$ glued along $\phi_M(S^{n-1}) \ni \phi_M(y) \sim \phi_N(y) \in \phi_N(S^{n-1})$; in the smooth case the gluing is smoothed with a collar $S^{n-1} \times (-1,1)$ (Bär's "small neck"), and the page gives the explicit open decomposition $M \# N = \dot M \cup \dot N$ with $\dot M \cap \dot N \cong S^{n-1} \times (0,1)$ used in the homology computation. Convention callout: the page states that independence of $M \# N$ from the choices (up to diffeomorphism) is a theorem (Palais / Cerf disc theorem) that the series does NOT prove and does NOT use — every statement in chapters XII–XIII is proved for the connected sum formed with any fixed choice of discs, and the notation $M \# N$ always refers to some such choice; the orientation convention for $\overline{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ summands is recorded here. Examples verified: $M \# S^n \cong M$ (explicit), $S^2 \# T^2$ is a torus (via the $\dot{S^2} \cong$ disc picture; as a homology computation only). Calibration check: identify $\dot M \cap \dot N$, explain the orientation reversal on one side, compute $\chi(M \# N) = \chi(M) + \chi(N) - 2$ for surfaces after the homology theorem.
- `Thm - Homology of Punctured Manifolds and Connected Sums` — type: theorem; source items: T4.2.11, E4.2.4; prereqs: [Thm - Mayer-Vietoris Sequence for Singular Homology (Gauge Theory), Thm - Homology of Spheres, Def - Connected Sum of Manifolds, Def - Topological Manifold]; statement: For an $n$-manifold $M$ and $x \in M$, $\dot M := M \setminus \{x\}$: the inclusion induces isomorphisms $H_k(\dot M; R) \to H_k(M;R)$ for $k \notin \{0, 1, n-1, n\}$ (and for $k = 1$ when $n \ge 3$, an isomorphism as well — proved: the map $H_1(\dot M) \to H_1(M)$ is surjective with kernel the image of $H_1(S^{n-1}) = 0$; the page proves the sharper statement: iso for $1 \le k \le n-2$, and for $k = 0$ when $n \ge 2$). For a connected sum $M \# N = \dot M \cup \dot N$ with $\dot M \cap \dot N \simeq S^{n-1}$: $H_k(M;R) \oplus H_k(N;R) \cong H_k(M \# N; R)$ for $k \notin \{0, 1, n-1, n\}$, and for $k = 1$ when $n \ge 3$ as well; in particular $H_2(M \# N;\mathbb{Z}) \cong H_2(M) \oplus H_2(N)$ for closed $4$-manifolds; proof: source full (MV with $X_0 = \dot M$, $X_1 = B \ni x$ a chart ball, $X_0 \cap X_1 \simeq S^{n-1}$); the page writes both MV sequences in full and derives the ranges. spec: Targets: chapter XIII's $Q_{M \# N} = Q_M \oplus Q_N$; the $S^2 \times S^2$ computation.
- `Thm - Connected Sum of Simply Connected Manifolds is Simply Connected` — type: theorem; source items: T4.2.17, I4.2.6; prereqs: [Thm - Seifert-van Kampen Theorem for Two Open Sets, Def - Connected Sum of Manifolds, Thm - Spheres of Dimension at Least Two are Simply Connected]; statement: (i) For a connected topological $n$-manifold $M$ with $n \ge 3$ and $x \in M$, the inclusion $\dot M = M \setminus \{x\} \hookrightarrow M$ induces an isomorphism $\pi_1(\dot M) \to \pi_1(M)$. (ii) If $M$, $N$ are simply connected topological manifolds of dimension $n \ge 3$ then $M \# N$ is simply connected. (iii) For $n = 2$ the statement (ii) holds as well, because the only simply connected closed surface is $S^2$ and $S^2 \# S^2 \cong S^2$; the page proves only the direction it needs — that $S^2 \# S^2 \cong S^2$ by an explicit homeomorphism — and records that Bär's "dimension $\ge 2$" (Rem. 4.2.25) is used in the series only for $n = 4$; proof: source omitted; (i) apply `Thm - Seifert-van Kampen Theorem for Two Open Sets` to $M = \dot M \cup B$ with $B$ a chart ball around $x$: $\dot M \cap B \cong B \setminus \{x\} \simeq S^{n-1}$ is simply connected for $n \ge 3$, so $\pi_1(M) \cong \pi_1(\dot M) * \pi_1(B) = \pi_1(\dot M)$; (ii) apply it to $M \# N = \dot M \cup \dot N$ with $\dot M \cap \dot N \cong S^{n-1} \times (0,1)$ simply connected, giving $\pi_1(M \# N) \cong \pi_1(\dot M) * \pi_1(\dot N) = 0$ by (i). Purely topological; no smoothing is needed. spec: Sources: $\pi_1$ of punctured manifolds; Targets: chapter XIII standing hypotheses for $k\mathbb{CP}^2 \# l \overline{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ and $\pi_1(V) = 0$ in the exotic $\mathbb{R}^4$ argument.
- `Thm - Homology of a Compact Smooth Manifold is Finitely Generated` — type: theorem; source items: (implicit in every use of Betti numbers and signatures in Bär §5; not stated in the source); prereqs: [Thm - Mayer-Vietoris Sequence for Singular Homology (Gauge Theory), Thm - Homotopy Invariance of Singular Homology (Gauge Theory), Def - The Riemannian Exponential Map, Thm - The Gauss Lemma, Thm - Existence of Riemannian Metrics via Partitions of Unity]; statement: (i) If a space $X$ admits a finite open cover $\{U_1, \dots, U_m\}$ such that every non-empty finite intersection $U_{i_1} \cap \cdots \cap U_{i_k}$ is contractible (a finite good cover), then $H_n(X;R)$ is a finitely generated $R$-module for every $n$ and vanishes for $n \ge m$; for $R = \mathbb{Z}$ it is a finitely generated abelian group, so Betti numbers $b_n := \operatorname{rank} H_n(X;\mathbb{Z})$ and the Euler characteristic $\chi(X) := \sum (-1)^n b_n$ are defined. (ii) Every compact smooth manifold, with or without boundary, admits a finite good cover; hence its homology is finitely generated in every degree and vanishes above the dimension; proof: source: none; (i) by induction on $m$ using MV and the fact that $\{U_1 \cap U_m, \dots, U_{m-1} \cap U_m\}$ is a good cover of $U_m \cap (U_1 \cup \cdots \cup U_{m-1})$ (Bott–Tu Prop. 5.3.1 argument transposed to singular homology; the vanishing above degree $m - 1$ needs $H_n(\text{contractible}) = 0$ for $n \ge 1$); (ii) via geodesically convex balls: (Lemma A) for a Riemannian metric, every point has a neighbourhood $W$ and $\varepsilon > 0$ such that every geodesic ball $B(y, \varepsilon)$, $y \in W$, is geodesically convex — any two of its points are joined by a unique minimising geodesic lying in the ball (do Carmo, *Riemannian Geometry*, Ch. 3 Prop. 4.2 (Whitehead); Lee, *Introduction to Riemannian Manifolds*, Thm 6.17), proved on the page from the exponential map and the Gauss lemma (linked RG II pages) via the standard second-variation-free argument on the distance function's Hessian along geodesics inside a normal ball; (Lemma B) a finite intersection of geodesically convex balls is geodesically convex, and a non-empty geodesically convex open set is contractible (geodesic contraction to any of its points, with continuity from the smooth dependence of geodesics on endpoints inside a convex normal ball); (Lemma C) for a compact manifold with boundary, choose a metric that is a product $g_{\partial W} + dt^2$ on a collar $\partial W \times [0, \delta)$ (collar existence for smooth manifolds with boundary: flow of an inward-pointing vector field, proved on the page, Lee Thm 9.25) and note that the intersection of a small geodesic ball of the double $DW$ centred on $W$ with $W$ is star-shaped along geodesics from its centre, hence contractible, and intersections of such half-balls are again geodesically convex in $DW$ intersected with $W$. Reference: Bott–Tu Thm 5.1 and Prop. 5.3.1; do Carmo III.4.2; Lee (Riemannian) Thm 6.17. spec: Why-true: "finitely many contractible pieces glued along contractible pieces can only produce finitely much homology"; Sources: any compact smooth manifold; Targets: Betti numbers, Euler characteristic, signature (chapter XIII), Prop. 3.38-type duality statements.
- `Ex - No Retraction of the Ball onto Its Boundary` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: T4.2.12, E4.2.5; prereqs: [Thm - Homology of Spheres, Def - Singular Homology with Coefficients]; spec: Prove there is no continuous $f : \bar B^{n+1} \to S^n$ with $f|_{S^n} = \mathrm{id}$ (Bär's argument: $\mathrm{id} = (f \circ \iota)_* = f_* \iota_*$ on $H_n(S^n;\mathbb{Z}) \cong \mathbb{Z}$ would factor through $H_n(\bar B^{n+1};\mathbb{Z}) = 0$); note the case $n = 0$ separately. Route: functoriality, contractibility of the ball.
- `Ex - Brouwer Fixed Point Theorem in All Dimensions` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: (standard consequence of E4.2.5); prereqs: [Ex - No Retraction of the Ball onto Its Boundary]; spec: Deduce that every continuous $g : \bar B^{n+1} \to \bar B^{n+1}$ has a fixed point by constructing the retraction from a fixed-point-free map (ray from $g(x)$ through $x$, explicit formula and continuity). Companion: `Ex - The Brouwer fixed-point theorem` (Topology IV) and `Ex - The Brouwer Fixed Point Theorem in Dimension 2 via Pi_1` (AT II).
- `Ex - Homology of a Wedge of Spheres` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: (standard drill; not used by any proof); prereqs: [Thm - Mayer-Vietoris Sequence for Singular Homology (Gauge Theory), Thm - Homology of Spheres]; spec: Compute $\tilde H_k(S^n \vee S^n; R) \cong R^2$ for $k = n$ and $0$ otherwise, via MV with open sets each deformation retracting onto one sphere; generalise to a finite wedge; compute $H_*(S^1 \vee S^1)$ and compare with the torus (link `Ex - Computing the Homology of the Torus`, AT I).
- `Exercise Index - §12.5 Mayer–Vietoris and Homology Computations` — lists: [Ex - No Retraction of the Ball onto Its Boundary, Ex - Brouwer Fixed Point Theorem in All Dimensions, Ex - Homology of a Wedge of Spheres]

### §12.6 The Hurewicz Theorem
- `Def - Hurewicz Homomorphism` — type: definition (compound: the fixed generator $[c] \in H_n(S^n;\mathbb{Z})$, the Hurewicz map $h$, commutator subgroup and abelianisation, the induced map $h : \pi_n(X,x)^{abel} \to H_n(X;\mathbb{Z})$); source items: D4.2.8, D4.2.9, T4.2.13 (well-definedness), T4.2.14, Rem. 4.2.23; prereqs: [Def - Homotopy Groups, Def - Singular Homology with Coefficients, Thm - Homology of Spheres, Thm - Homotopy Invariance of Singular Homology (Gauge Theory), Def - Normal Subgroup, Def - Quotient Group, Def - Hurewicz Map]; spec: Fix once for all $n \ge 1$ a generator $[c_n] \in H_n(S^n;\mathbb{Z}) \cong \mathbb{Z}$ compatible with the suspension isomorphisms of `Thm - Homology of Spheres` (the page fixes it recursively through the Mayer–Vietoris connecting map, and for $n = 1$ as the class of the loop $t \mapsto e^{2\pi i t}$ regarded as a singular $1$-simplex, verifying it is a cycle generating $H_1(S^1;\mathbb{Z})$); for path connected $X$, $x \in X$, $h([f]) := f_*([c_n])$. Proved on the page: T4.2.13 (well defined: $f \simeq f'$ rel $NP$ implies $f_* = f'_*$ by homotopy invariance); D4.2.9 with the verification that $[G,G]$ is normal and $G^{abel}$ abelian and the universal property (every homomorphism to an abelian group factors uniquely); T4.2.14 ($h$ kills $[\pi_n, \pi_n]$ because $H_n$ is abelian — this needs $h$ to be a homomorphism, wikilinked forward to `Thm - The Hurewicz Map is a Homomorphism`); Rem. 4.2.23: for $n \ge 2$, $\pi_n^{abel} = \pi_n$ by `Thm - Higher Homotopy Groups are Abelian via the Eckmann-Hilton Argument`. Relation to `Def - Hurewicz Map` (AT III): same map; this page fixes the generator and the coefficient conventions. Examples: $h : \pi_1(S^1) \to H_1(S^1)$ sends the standard generator to $[c_1]$ (verified); $h = 0$ on $\pi_3(S^2)$ because $H_3(S^2) = 0$ (verified, R4.2.2). Calibration check: explain why $h$ needs a base point but its image does not; compute $h$ of the constant map; write $G^{abel}$ for $G = S_3$.
- `Thm - The Hurewicz Map is a Homomorphism` — type: theorem; source items: I4.2.5 (first half), T4.2.13 (second clause); prereqs: [Def - Hurewicz Homomorphism, Thm - Homology of Spheres, Ex - Homology of a Wedge of Spheres, Thm - Group Structure on Homotopy Groups and the Cube Model]; statement: For $n \ge 1$, $h : \pi_n(X,x) \to H_n(X;\mathbb{Z})$ satisfies $h([f] * [g]) = h([f]) + h([g])$; proof: source omitted; the page factors $f * g$ as $S^n \xrightarrow{c} S^n \vee S^n \xrightarrow{f \vee g} X$ where $c$ collapses the equator, shows $c_*[c_n] = \iota_{1*}[c_n] + \iota_{2*}[c_n]$ in $H_n(S^n \vee S^n) \cong \mathbb{Z}^2$ (using the two collapse maps $q_1, q_2 : S^n \vee S^n \to S^n$, which are homotopy equivalences on one summand and constant on the other, and the identification of `Ex - Homology of a Wedge of Spheres`; $q_i \circ c \simeq \mathrm{id}_{S^n}$ verified by an explicit homotopy), and concludes by functoriality. Reference: Spanier Ch. 7 §4 (Thm 7.4.3, the Hurewicz map is a homomorphism); the writer follows the pinch-map argument exactly as written here. spec: Targets: $h$ factors through the abelianisation; the Hurewicz theorem.
- `Thm - Hurewicz Theorem` — type: theorem; source items: T4.2.15, I4.2.5 (second half), I5.1.1 (chapter XIII's Step 1); prereqs: [Def - Hurewicz Homomorphism, Thm - The Hurewicz Map is a Homomorphism, Thm - Small Chains Theorem via Barycentric Subdivision, Thm - Homotopy Invariance of Singular Homology (Gauge Theory), Thm - Long Exact Sequence of a Pair in Singular Homology, Thm - Higher Homotopy Groups are Abelian via the Eckmann-Hilton Argument]; statement: Let $X$ be path connected, $x \in X$, $m \ge 1$, and $\pi_k(X,x) = 0$ for $1 \le k \le m - 1$. Then $H_k(X;\mathbb{Z}) = 0$ for $1 \le k \le m-1$ and $h : \pi_m(X,x)^{abel} \to H_m(X;\mathbb{Z})$ is an isomorphism; for $m \ge 2$ this reads $h : \pi_m(X,x) \cong H_m(X;\mathbb{Z})$; proof: source omitted; the page proves the case $m = 1$ by Hatcher Thm 2A.1 (surjectivity: every $1$-cycle is homologous to a sum of loops at $x$ by joining vertices to $x$; injectivity: a loop that bounds a $2$-chain is a product of commutators — the explicit rewriting of $\partial \sum \sigma_i$ as a product of loops $[\sigma_i|_{\text{edges}}]$, Hatcher pp. 166–167), and the case $m \ge 2$ by the Eilenberg-subcomplex route: (Lemma 1) for an $(m-1)$-connected space $X$, the subcomplex $C_\bullet^{(m)}(X, x) \subset C_\bullet(X)$ spanned by singular simplices sending the $(m-1)$-skeleton of $\Delta^n$ to $x$ is a chain deformation retract of $C_\bullet(X)$ (inductive construction, simplex by simplex over the skeleta of $\Delta^n$, of a chain map $C_\bullet(X) \to C_\bullet^{(m)}$ homotopic to the identity, using the vanishing of $\pi_k$, $k \le m-1$, to extend maps from $\partial \Delta^{k+1}$ to $\Delta^{k+1}$: Spanier, *Algebraic Topology*, Ch. 7 §5, the Eilenberg-subcomplex lemma preceding the Hurewicz theorem — the writer follows Spanier's Ch. 7 §5 argument or tom Dieck §20.1); (Lemma 2) in $C^{(m)}_\bullet$ every $m$-simplex is a based map $(\Delta^m/\partial \Delta^m, *) = (S^m, NP) \to (X, x)$, every $(m-1)$-chain is a multiple of the constant simplex, and the boundary of an $(m+1)$-simplex $\tau$ is $\sum (-1)^k \tau \circ \iota^{m+1}_k$ whose Hurewicz-class sum vanishes (the faces of a based $(m+1)$-simplex give the relation $\sum_k (-1)^k [\tau \circ \iota_k] = 0$ in $\pi_m(X,x)$: Spanier 7.5.2 / Hatcher's Lemma in the proof of 4.32, proved via the homotopy $\Delta^{m+1} \to X$ restricted to the boundary); (Lemma 3) the resulting map $H_m(C^{(m)}) \to \pi_m(X,x)^{abel}$, $\sum n_i \sigma_i \mapsto \sum n_i [\sigma_i]$, is well defined by Lemma 2 and inverse to $h$; (Lemma 4) $H_k(C^{(m)}) = 0$ for $1 \le k \le m-1$ (no non-constant simplices in those degrees). Reference: Spanier Ch. 7 §5 (Thm 7.5.4, absolute Hurewicz theorem, and the Eilenberg-subcomplex lemma preceding it); alternative route recorded in a remark: Hatcher Thm 4.32 via CW approximation, homotopy excision and cellular homology (not followed, since the series does not build CW theory). The page names the flagged page `Thm - Hurewicz Theorem (Statement)` it replaces. spec: Why-true: "below the connectivity there is nothing, and at the connectivity a simplex whose boundary sits at the base point is a sphere"; Sources: simply connected spaces ($m = 2$: $\pi_2 \cong H_2$ — the chapter XIII input), $(n-1)$-connected spheres; Targets: $\pi_n(S^n)$, $\pi_3(S^2)$, representability of $H_2$ by maps of spheres (chapter XIII), Whitehead's theorem context (imported in XIII).
- `Thm - Homotopy Groups of Spheres Below the Dimension` — type: theorem; source items: T4.2.16, E4.2.7, R4.2.2, T4.1.15, I4.1.1 (the remaining parts: $\pi_i(S^n) = 0$ for $i < n$ and $\pi_n(S^n) \cong \mathbb{Z}$ generated by $[\mathrm{id}]$); prereqs: [Thm - Hurewicz Theorem, Thm - Homology of Spheres, Thm - Spheres of Dimension at Least Two are Simply Connected, Thm - Pi_1 of S^1 is Z]; statement: For $n \ge 1$: $\pi_m(S^n, NP) = 0$ for $1 \le m < n$, and $h : \pi_n(S^n, NP) \to H_n(S^n;\mathbb{Z}) \cong \mathbb{Z}$ is an isomorphism, with $[\mathrm{id}_{S^n}] \mapsto [c_n]$, so $\pi_n(S^n) \cong \mathbb{Z}$ generated by $[\mathrm{id}]$; the hypothesis of the Hurewicz theorem cannot be dropped: $\pi_3(S^2) \cong \mathbb{Z}$ (forward link) while $H_3(S^2;\mathbb{Z}) = 0$; proof: source full (iterated Hurewicz from $\pi_1(S^n) = 0$ for $n \ge 2$ and `Thm - Homology of Spheres`); $n = 1$ from the AT II page. spec: Targets: degree theory, the Hopf bundle computation, chapter XIII's representability step.
