- `Thm - Third Homotopy Group of the Two-Sphere` — type: theorem; source items: T4.1.24, E4.1.10; prereqs: [Thm - Long Exact Homotopy Sequence of a Fibre Bundle, Def - The Hopf Bundle, Thm - Homotopy Groups of Spheres Below the Dimension, Thm - Higher Homotopy Groups of the Circle Vanish]; statement: From the Hopf bundle $S^1 \to S^3 \xrightarrow{H} S^2$: $\pi_3(S^1) = 0 \to \pi_3(S^3) \xrightarrow{H_\sharp} \pi_3(S^2) \to \pi_2(S^1) = 0$ is exact, so $H_\sharp$ is an isomorphism and $\pi_3(S^2) \cong \pi_3(S^3) \cong \mathbb{Z}$, generated by $[H]$; also $\pi_2(S^2) \cong \pi_1(S^1) \cong \mathbb{Z}$ from the same sequence (consistency with the previous page); proof: source full given $\pi_3(S^3) \cong \mathbb{Z}$; the page assembles the ingredients, noting the Hopf bundle is a fibre bundle by chapter III's `Def - The Hopf Bundle`. spec: companion `Ex - Pi_3 of S^2 is Z via the Hopf Map` (AT III) is cross-referenced.
- `Ex - Abelianization of the Free Group on Two Generators` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: T4.2.18, E4.2.6; prereqs: [Def - Hurewicz Homomorphism, Def - Free Group and Free Product]; spec: Prove $(\mathbb{Z} * \mathbb{Z})^{abel} \cong \mathbb{Z}^2$ via the universal properties (a homomorphism from the free product to an abelian group is a pair of homomorphisms from $\mathbb{Z}$). Then, using `Thm - Seifert-van Kampen Theorem for Two Open Sets`, show $\pi_1(S^1 \vee S^1) \cong \mathbb{Z} * \mathbb{Z}$ and via Hurewicz $H_1(S^1 \vee S^1;\mathbb{Z}) \cong \mathbb{Z}^2$, consistent with `Ex - Homology of a Wedge of Spheres`.
- `Ex - First Homology of the Torus via Hurewicz` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: (drill of T4.2.15 with $m = 1$); prereqs: [Thm - Hurewicz Theorem, Ex - Coverings of the Torus by Euclidean Space]; spec: From $\pi_1(T^2) \cong \mathbb{Z}^2$ deduce $H_1(T^2;\mathbb{Z}) \cong \mathbb{Z}^2$ and identify the images of the two standard loops as the generators; compare with `Ex - Computing the Homology of the Torus` (AT I).
- `Ex - Hurewicz Fails Without the Vanishing Hypothesis` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: R4.2.2; prereqs: [Thm - Third Homotopy Group of the Two-Sphere, Thm - Homology of Spheres]; spec: Verify that $h : \pi_3(S^2) \to H_3(S^2;\mathbb{Z})$ is the zero map on a non-trivial group and explain which hypothesis of `Thm - Hurewicz Theorem` fails ($\pi_2(S^2) \ne 0$).
- `Ex - Degree of a Self-Map of the Sphere via Hurewicz` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: (standard; prepares the degree used in §12.8 and chapter XIII linking numbers); prereqs: [Thm - Homotopy Groups of Spheres Below the Dimension, Thm - Homology of Spheres]; spec: Define $\deg f \in \mathbb{Z}$ for $f : S^n \to S^n$ by $f_*[c_n] = (\deg f)[c_n]$, show it is a homotopy invariant, multiplicative under composition, equals $k$ for $z \mapsto z^k$ on $S^1$ (via `Ex - The Power Map is a k-Fold Covering` and Hurewicz), and that $\deg$ identifies $\pi_n(S^n) \to \mathbb{Z}$ with the Hurewicz isomorphism. Also show the antipodal map of $S^n$ has degree $(-1)^{n+1}$ (reflections have degree $-1$: compute on the MV connecting map).
- `Exercise Index - §12.6 The Hurewicz Theorem` — lists: [Ex - Abelianization of the Free Group on Two Generators, Ex - First Homology of the Torus via Hurewicz, Ex - Hurewicz Fails Without the Vanishing Hypothesis, Ex - Degree of a Self-Map of the Sphere via Hurewicz]

### §12.7 Cohomology, Universal Coefficients, and Products
- `Def - Hom and Ext of Abelian Groups` — type: definition (compound: $\operatorname{Hom}(A, G)$, free resolutions, $\operatorname{Ext}(A, G)$, computation for finitely generated $A$); source items: (Hatcher §3.1; algebraic input for universal coefficients); prereqs: [Def - Abelian Group, Def - Free Module, Def - Exact Sequence and Short Exact Sequence, Thm - Classification of Finitely Generated Abelian Groups]; spec: $\operatorname{Hom}(A,G)$ with its functoriality; a free resolution $0 \to F_1 \to F_0 \to A \to 0$ (every abelian group has one of length one: $F_0$ free on $A$, $F_1$ its kernel, which is free — the page proves that subgroups of free abelian groups are free only for finitely generated groups via `Thm - Smith Normal Form` / `Thm - Submodules of Free Modules over a Euclidean Domain`, and restricts $\operatorname{Ext}$ to finitely generated $A$, which is all the series needs, with a remark on the general case); $\operatorname{Ext}(A,G) := \operatorname{coker}(\operatorname{Hom}(F_0, G) \to \operatorname{Hom}(F_1, G))$, independence of the resolution proved on the page (Hatcher Lemma 3.1(b) for the two-term case: comparison of resolutions and chain homotopy). Corollaries proved: $\operatorname{Ext}(A \oplus B, G) \cong \operatorname{Ext}(A,G) \oplus \operatorname{Ext}(B,G)$, $\operatorname{Ext}(\mathbb{Z}, G) = 0$, $\operatorname{Ext}(\mathbb{Z}/n, G) \cong G/nG$; hence $\operatorname{Ext}(A, \mathbb{Z}) \cong T(A)$ (torsion subgroup) and $\operatorname{Ext}(A, F) = 0$ for a field $F$ of characteristic $0$ or for $F = \mathbb{R}$, $\mathbb{Q}$, and $\operatorname{Ext}(A,\mathbb{Z}/2) \cong \operatorname{Hom}(T(A), \mathbb{Z}/2)$; the page states and proves the cases actually used: $G = \mathbb{Z}$, $G = \mathbb{R}$, $G = \mathbb{Q}$, $G = \mathbb{Z}/2$. Calibration check: compute $\operatorname{Ext}(\mathbb{Z}/6, \mathbb{Z})$, $\operatorname{Ext}(\mathbb{Z}^3, \mathbb{Z}/2)$, $\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb{Z}/2, \mathbb{Z})$.
- `Def - Singular Cohomology with Coefficients and the Kronecker Pairing` — type: definition (compound: cochains $C^n(X;G) = \operatorname{Hom}(C_n(X;\mathbb{Z}), G)$, coboundary $\delta = \partial^*$, $H^n(X;G)$, relative cohomology, induced maps, the Kronecker pairing $\langle \cdot, \cdot \rangle : H^n(X;G) \times H_n(X;\mathbb{Z}) \to G$, the evaluation map $H^n(X;G) \to \operatorname{Hom}(H_n(X;\mathbb{Z}), G)$); source items: (Hatcher §3.1; needed for the cup-product form and Poincaré duality; links `Def - Singular Cohomology` AT I); prereqs: [Def - Singular Homology with Coefficients, Def - Relative Singular Homology, Def - Chain Complex and Its Homology, Def - Singular Cohomology, Def - Hom and Ext of Abelian Groups]; spec: Definitions with $\delta \delta = 0$ verified from $\partial \partial = 0$; contravariant functoriality and homotopy invariance (dualise the prism operator: proved on the page as a corollary of the chain-level identity of `Thm - Homotopy Invariance of Singular Homology (Gauge Theory)`); the long exact sequence of a pair in cohomology (dualise the split short exact sequence of chain complexes — the splitting is proved: $C_n(X,A)$ is free, so $0 \to C^n(X,A) \to C^n(X) \to C^n(A) \to 0$ is exact); the Mayer–Vietoris sequence in cohomology (same, dualising the small-chains short exact sequence, with the zig-zag lemma); the Kronecker pairing is well defined and bilinear (verified). For $G = R$ a commutative ring, $H^n(X;R)$ is an $R$-module. Examples verified: $H^0(X;G) = $ locally constant functions to $G$ ... precisely $\operatorname{Hom}(H_0(X), G)$ = maps from the set of path components to $G$; $H^n(\{p\};G) = 0$ for $n \ge 1$. Non-example: cohomology is not the dual of homology in general (forward link to the universal coefficient page, $\mathbb{RP}^2$-type torsion, stated as a warning). Calibration check: write $\delta \phi$ for a $0$-cochain, verify contravariance on a composite, compute $H^1(S^1;\mathbb{Z})$ from the pairing once the UCT is known.
- `Thm - Universal Coefficient Theorem for Cohomology` — type: theorem; source items: (Hatcher Thm 3.2; needed for unimodularity over $\mathbb{Z}$, the de Rham identification, and the parity/spin argument); prereqs: [Def - Singular Cohomology with Coefficients and the Kronecker Pairing, Def - Hom and Ext of Abelian Groups, Thm - Zig-Zag Lemma for Short Exact Sequences of Chain Complexes]; statement: For any space $X$ (or pair), any abelian group $G$ and any $n$, there is a natural short exact sequence $0 \to \operatorname{Ext}(H_{n-1}(X;\mathbb{Z}), G) \to H^n(X;G) \xrightarrow{\text{ev}} \operatorname{Hom}(H_n(X;\mathbb{Z}), G) \to 0$ which splits (non-naturally). Corollaries: (a) if $H_{n-1}(X;\mathbb{Z})$ is free (in particular zero) then $H^n(X;G) \cong \operatorname{Hom}(H_n(X;\mathbb{Z}), G)$; (b) for $G = \mathbb{R}$ or $\mathbb{Q}$, $H^n(X;G) \cong \operatorname{Hom}(H_n(X;\mathbb{Z}), G)$ always; (c) if $H_*(X;\mathbb{Z})$ is finitely generated then $H^n(X;\mathbb{Z}) \cong F_n \oplus T_{n-1}$ where $F_n$ is the free part of $H_n$ and $T_{n-1}$ the torsion of $H_{n-1}$; (d) if $H_{n-1}(X;\mathbb{Z})$ is free then $H^n(X;\mathbb{Z}/2) \cong \operatorname{Hom}(H_n(X;\mathbb{Z}), \mathbb{Z}/2)$; proof: source: none; the page follows Hatcher Thm 3.2 for free chain complexes: the short exact sequence $0 \to Z_n \to C_n \to B_{n-1} \to 0$ splits (since $B_{n-1}$ is free as a subgroup of a free abelian group; the singular chain groups are not finitely generated, so the page proves "every subgroup of a free abelian group is free" in full generality as Lemma 0, by transfinite induction over a well-ordered basis — Lang, *Algebra*, Appendix 2 §2 — with no restriction to countable complexes), dualises, and follows Hatcher's proof exactly: the sequence $0 \to \operatorname{Hom}(B_{n-1}, G) \to \operatorname{Hom}(C_n, G) \to \operatorname{Hom}(Z_n, G) \to 0$, its long exact sequence, and the identification of the connecting map with the map induced by $B_{n-1} \hookrightarrow Z_{n-1}$, whose cokernel is $\operatorname{Ext}(H_{n-1}, G)$ by the free resolution $0 \to B_{n-1} \to Z_{n-1} \to H_{n-1} \to 0$; splitting from the splitting of $C_n \to B_{n-1}$; naturality. Reference: Hatcher Thm 3.2, pp. 195–198. spec: Why-true: "cohomology is the dual of homology up to the torsion that duality cannot see, and that torsion is the $\operatorname{Ext}$ of the degree below"; Sources: simply connected spaces ($H_1 = 0$ so $H^2 \cong \operatorname{Hom}(H_2, \mathbb{Z})$ — the chapter XIII input); real coefficients (de Rham); Targets: $H^2(X;\mathbb{Z})$ free for simply connected $X$; unimodularity of the intersection form; $H^2(X;\mathbb{Z}/2) = \operatorname{Hom}(H_2, \mathbb{Z}/2)$ for the spin criterion.
- `Def - Cup Product` — type: definition; source items: (Hatcher §3.2; needed for the cup-product form); prereqs: [Def - Singular Cohomology with Coefficients and the Kronecker Pairing]; spec: For a commutative ring $R$, $(\phi \smile \psi)(\sigma) := \phi(\sigma|_{[v_0, \dots, v_k]}) \, \psi(\sigma|_{[v_k, \dots, v_{k+l}]})$ with the front and back faces written as explicit compositions with affine maps $\Delta^k \to \Delta^{k+l}$, $\Delta^l \to \Delta^{k+l}$; the Leibniz formula $\delta(\phi \smile \psi) = \delta\phi \smile \psi + (-1)^k \phi \smile \delta\psi$ (proved on the page: Hatcher Lemma 3.6, the term-by-term cancellation written out); hence $\smile$ descends to $H^k(X;R) \times H^l(X;R) \to H^{k+l}(X;R)$; relative versions $H^k(X,A) \times H^l(X,B) \to H^{k+l}(X, A \cup B)$ for $A, B$ open (or $A$, $B$ with $\operatorname{int}$ covering $A \cup B$; the page proves the case needed later: $A$, $B$ open, via the small chains theorem, Hatcher p. 209). Examples verified: cup product with the unit $1 \in H^0$; the cup product of two $1$-cocycles on $S^1$ is zero for degree reasons; non-example: the cup product is not commutative at cochain level (an explicit computation on $\Delta^2$). Calibration check: check the Leibniz sign for $k = 1$, $l = 1$; write the front $1$-face of a $2$-simplex; verify naturality $f^*(\alpha \smile \beta) = f^*\alpha \smile f^*\beta$ at cochain level.
- `Thm - Properties of the Cup Product` — type: theorem; source items: (Hatcher Props 3.10, 3.11, Thm 3.14); prereqs: [Def - Cup Product, Thm - Small Chains Theorem via Barycentric Subdivision, Def - Chain Complex and Its Homology]; statement: On $H^*(X;R)$, $R$ commutative: the cup product is associative, has the unit $1 \in H^0(X;R)$ (the class of the augmentation cocycle), is natural ($f^*(\alpha \smile \beta) = f^*\alpha \smile f^*\beta$), and is graded commutative: $\alpha \smile \beta = (-1)^{kl} \beta \smile \alpha$ for $\alpha \in H^k$, $\beta \in H^l$; proof: associativity, unit and naturality at cochain level (verified); graded commutativity by Hatcher Thm 3.14: the chain map $\rho : C_n(X) \to C_n(X)$, $\rho(\sigma) = \varepsilon_n \, \sigma \circ \bar\rho_n$ (reversal of vertex order, $\varepsilon_n = (-1)^{n(n+1)/2}$), is chain homotopic to the identity (the explicit prism-like chain homotopy $P(\sigma) = \sum_i (-1)^i \varepsilon_{n-i} \, (\sigma \circ \pi)|_{[v_0, \dots, v_i, w_n, \dots, w_i]}$ with the verification of $\partial P + P \partial = \rho - \mathrm{id}$ written out), and $\rho^*(\phi \smile \psi) = (-1)^{kl} \rho^*\psi \smile \rho^*\phi$ at cochain level. Reference: Hatcher pp. 210–212. spec: Targets: symmetry of the cup-product intersection form in dimension $4$ ($k = l = 2$, sign $+1$); Poincaré duality's cap-product identities.
- `Def - Cap Product` — type: definition; source items: (Hatcher §3.3; needed for Poincaré duality); prereqs: [Def - Cup Product, Def - Singular Homology with Coefficients]; spec: $\frown : C_k(X;R) \times C^l(X;R) \to C_{k-l}(X;R)$, $\sigma \frown \phi := \phi(\sigma|_{[v_0, \dots, v_l]}) \, \sigma|_{[v_l, \dots, v_k]}$ for $k \ge l$; the boundary formula $\partial(\sigma \frown \phi) = (-1)^l (\partial \sigma \frown \phi - \sigma \frown \delta \phi)$ (proved on the page, Hatcher p. 240); descent to $H_k(X;R) \times H^l(X;R) \to H_{k-l}(X;R)$; relative versions $H_k(X,A) \times H^l(X) \to H_{k-l}(X,A)$ and $H_k(X, A) \times H^l(X, A) \to H_{k-l}(X)$; the page states precisely the versions Hatcher uses in the proof of 3.30 and 3.43: $H_k(X, A \cup B) \times H^l(X, A) \to H_{k-l}(X, B)$ for $A, B$ open (or with interiors covering); naturality: $f_*(\alpha) \frown \phi = f_*(\alpha \frown f^*\phi)$ (proved at chain level); compatibility with the Kronecker pairing: $\langle \phi, \alpha \frown \psi \rangle = \langle \psi \smile \phi, \alpha \rangle$ (proved). Examples verified: cap with $1 \in H^0$ is the identity; for $\alpha \in H_n$ and $\phi \in H^n$, $\alpha \frown \phi = \langle \phi, \alpha \rangle \cdot [\text{point}] \in H_0$. Calibration check: compute the boundary formula's sign for $l = 1$; write $\sigma \frown \phi$ for a $2$-simplex and a $1$-cochain; verify naturality on an inclusion.
- `Thm - De Rham Theorem via Smooth Singular Chains` — type: theorem; source items: I5.3.7 (both halves: smooth singular chains compute singular homology; integration identifies de Rham cohomology with singular cohomology with real coefficients), R5.3.4 (fn. 2), D5.3.3 (smooth singular chains); prereqs: [Def - Singular Cohomology with Coefficients and the Kronecker Pairing, Thm - Universal Coefficient Theorem for Cohomology, Def - de Rham Cohomology, Thm - The Mayer-Vietoris Sequence, Thm - Homotopy Invariance of de Rham Cohomology, Thm - The Poincaré Lemma on a Star-Shaped Region, Thm - Stokes' Theorem on Manifolds, Thm - Homology of a Compact Smooth Manifold is Finitely Generated, Thm - Mayer-Vietoris Sequence for Singular Homology (Gauge Theory), Thm - Small Chains Theorem via Barycentric Subdivision]; statement: Let $M$ be a smooth manifold. (i) A singular simplex $\sigma : \Delta^k \to M$ is smooth if it extends to a smooth map on a neighbourhood of $\Delta^k$ in its affine hull (equivalently: smooth in the interior with all derivatives extending continuously, Bär's fn. 2); the smooth singular chains $C_k^\infty(M;\mathbb{Z}) \subset C_k(M;\mathbb{Z})$ form a subcomplex and the inclusion induces isomorphisms $H_k^\infty(M;\mathbb{Z}) \cong H_k(M;\mathbb{Z})$ for all $k$; the same with any coefficients. (ii) Integration $I : \Omega^k(M) \to C^k_\infty(M;\mathbb{R})$, $I(\omega)(\sigma) := \int_{\Delta^k} \sigma^*\omega$, is a cochain map (Stokes), and the induced map $H^k_{dR}(M) \to H^k_\infty(M;\mathbb{R}) \cong H^k(M;\mathbb{R})$ is an isomorphism for every $k$. (iii) Consequently, for a compact $M$, the pairing $H^k_{dR}(M) \times H_k(M;\mathbb{Z}) \to \mathbb{R}$, $([\omega], [c]) \mapsto \int_c \omega$, identifies $H^k_{dR}(M) \cong \operatorname{Hom}(H_k(M;\mathbb{Z}), \mathbb{R})$, and $H^k_{dR}(M;\mathbb{Z}) := \{[\omega] \mid \int_c \omega \in \mathbb{Z} \ \forall [c] \in H_k(M;\mathbb{Z})\}$ corresponds to $\operatorname{Hom}(H_k(M;\mathbb{Z}), \mathbb{Z}) \cong H^k(M;\mathbb{Z})/\text{torsion}$ (via the universal coefficient theorem); proof: source: none ("one can show"); the page follows Lee, *Introduction to Smooth Manifolds* (2nd ed.), Thm 18.7 (smooth singular homology equals singular homology: the smoothing operator via Whitney approximation on simplices — the page proves the needed relative Whitney approximation for maps from $\Delta^k$ inductively over faces, which is a special case of `[NEEDED FROM chapter XIII §13.1: Thm - Continuous Maps into a Manifold are Homotopic to Smooth Maps, relative version]` — the orchestrator should place that theorem in chapter XII §12.7 or earlier, OR the writer proves on this page the simplex version directly following Lee Thm 18.7's proof) and Thm 18.14 (the de Rham theorem: verify the isomorphism for open balls (Poincaré lemma on both sides), for disjoint unions, and inductively for a finite union of opens via the two Mayer–Vietoris sequences and the five lemma, then for arbitrary manifolds via a "good cover"-free induction on the sets in a countable cover using Lee's Lemma 18.13 ("de Rham cover" induction); Bredon V.9 as alternative). The five lemma is proved on the page as Lemma. Reference: Lee (2nd ed.) Ch. 18, Thms 18.7 and 18.14; Bredon V.9; Bott–Tu §I.5 for the intuition. The page names the flagged AT I and DG X pages it replaces. spec: Why-true: "both theories satisfy the same Mayer–Vietoris and both vanish on balls, so a finite induction identifies them, and integration is the natural transformation"; Sources: any smooth manifold; Targets: chapter XIII `Thm - Intersection Form via de Rham Cohomology` (Bär's step (c)), the classification of $U(1)$-bundles by $c_1$ in chapter III (which needs $H^2_{dR}(M;\mathbb{Z}) \leftrightarrow H^2(M;\mathbb{Z})/\text{torsion}$).
- `Thm - Hermitian Line Bundles are Classified by the Integral Second Cohomology` — type: theorem; source items: (the identification $\operatorname{Pic}(M) \cong H^2(M;\mathbb{Z})$ that chapters III–XI deferred to chapter XII, in the form chapter XIII needs; carries Haydys I2.4.6 and Bär I5.3.1/fn. 3 in the de Rham + Čech form); prereqs: [Def - Singular Cohomology with Coefficients and the Kronecker Pairing, Thm - Universal Coefficient Theorem for Cohomology, Thm - De Rham Theorem via Smooth Singular Chains, Thm - Homology of a Compact Smooth Manifold is Finitely Generated, Def - Principal G-Bundle, Def - Transition Functions and the Cocycle Condition, Def - Fibre Bundle, Def - Partition of Unity on a Manifold]; statement: Let $M$ be a smooth manifold admitting a finite good cover (every compact manifold does, by `Thm - Homology of a Compact Smooth Manifold is Finitely Generated` Lemma). (i) Isomorphism classes of Hermitian line bundles (equivalently principal $U(1)$-bundles) form an abelian group $\operatorname{Pic}(M)$ under tensor product, and $L \mapsto [\{g_{\alpha\beta}\}]$ identifies $\operatorname{Pic}(M)$ with the Čech cohomology $\check H^1(M; \underline{U(1)})$ of the sheaf of smooth $U(1)$-valued functions on a good cover. (ii) The connecting homomorphism $\delta$ of the coefficient sequence $0 \to \mathbb{Z} \to \mathbb{R} \xrightarrow{\exp(2\pi i \cdot)} U(1) \to 0$ is an isomorphism $\delta : \check H^1(M; \underline{U(1)}) \xrightarrow{\cong} \check H^2(M; \mathbb{Z})$, because $\check H^k(M; \underline{\mathbb{R}}) = 0$ for $k \ge 1$ on a good cover (the sheaf of smooth functions is fine: a partition of unity subordinate to the cover gives an explicit contracting homotopy of the Čech complex — proved on the page). (iii) On a good cover $\check H^\bullet(M; \mathbb{Z}) \cong H^\bullet(M; \mathbb{Z})$ (singular), the Čech–singular comparison, proved on the page by the same Mayer–Vietoris induction over the cover as `Thm - Homology of a Compact Smooth Manifold is Finitely Generated`, using that every non-empty finite intersection is contractible. Composing, $c_1 := \delta : \operatorname{Pic}(M) \xrightarrow{\cong} H^2(M; \mathbb{Z})$ is a group isomorphism, natural in $M$; and it is compatible with the de Rham first Chern class of chapter VI, sending $L$ under $H^2(M;\mathbb{Z}) \to H^2_{dR}(M)$ (`Thm - De Rham Theorem via Smooth Singular Chains`) to $[\tfrac{i}{2\pi} F_A]$; proof: (i) the bijection between line bundles and $U(1)$-cocycles from `Def - Transition Functions and the Cocycle Condition` (isomorphic bundles give cohomologous cocycles; every cocycle is realised — verified); (ii) fineness of $\underline{\mathbb{R}}$ and the long exact Čech sequence of the coefficient sequence; (iii) the Čech–singular comparison for good covers (Mayer–Vietoris induction / the nerve); compatibility by tracing $\tfrac{1}{2\pi i}\log g_{\alpha\beta}$ through the connecting map and its image in real cohomology, which is the curvature class (done in full). What is proved and what would be needed: the page PROVES $\operatorname{Pic}(M) \cong H^2(M;\mathbb{Z})$ for every manifold with a finite good cover (in particular every compact four-manifold of chapters XII–XIII), using only Čech machinery over a good cover and the singular tools of chapter XII; it does NOT use sheaf cohomology of the structure sheaf or the exponential sequence of holomorphic line bundles (which would need $\check H^\bullet(M;\mathcal{O})$). Reference: Bott–Tu, *Differential Forms in Algebraic Topology*, §I.5 and §II.10–11 (the Čech–de Rham and Čech–singular comparisons); Griffiths–Harris Ch. 1 §1 (the exponential sequence for line bundles, in the smooth category); Hatcher §3.1 (the singular side). spec: this is the page manifest-1's header defers to; it discharges the deferred identification and removes the standing restriction for chapter XIII on the pages that cite it. Why-true: "a line bundle is glued from $U(1)$ transition data, and the obstruction to taking a global logarithm of that data is exactly an integral second cohomology class". Targets: chapter XIII `Thm - Reducible Instantons Correspond to Classes of Self-Intersection One` and `Def - Fake Complex Projective Plane and the E8 Manifold` (each $\alpha \in H^2(X;\mathbb{Z})$ is $c_1$ of a unique Hermitian line bundle); chapter III's `Thm - Classification of Principal U(1)-Bundles by the First Chern Class` (retroactively identifies its $\operatorname{Pic}$ with $H^2(-;\mathbb{Z})$).
- `Ex - Cohomology of Spheres and Complex Projective Space via Universal Coefficients` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: (drill); prereqs: [Thm - Universal Coefficient Theorem for Cohomology, Thm - Homology of Spheres, Thm - Homology of Complex Projective Space]; spec: Compute $H^k(S^n;\mathbb{Z})$ and $H^k(\mathbb{CP}^n;\mathbb{Z})$ from the homology and the UCT; note all groups are free.
- `Ex - Torsion in Homology Shifts Up in Cohomology` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: (drill of corollary (c)); prereqs: [Thm - Universal Coefficient Theorem for Cohomology, Def - Hom and Ext of Abelian Groups]; spec: For a space with finitely generated homology prove $T(H^n(X;\mathbb{Z})) \cong T(H_{n-1}(X;\mathbb{Z}))$ and $\operatorname{rank} H^n = \operatorname{rank} H_n$; apply to $\mathbb{RP}^2$ (using $H_1(\mathbb{RP}^2) = \mathbb{Z}/2$ from `Ex - Singular Homology of RP^2`, AT I) to get $H^2(\mathbb{RP}^2;\mathbb{Z}) = \mathbb{Z}/2$ while $\operatorname{Hom}(H_2, \mathbb{Z}) = 0$.
- `Ex - The Kronecker Pairing is Perfect over a Field` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: (drill of corollary (b)); prereqs: [Thm - Universal Coefficient Theorem for Cohomology, Def - Singular Cohomology with Coefficients and the Kronecker Pairing]; spec: Show that for $G = \mathbb{R}$ (or $\mathbb{Q}$) evaluation $H^n(X;G) \to \operatorname{Hom}(H_n(X;\mathbb{Z}), G)$ is an isomorphism, and that for finitely generated homology $\dim_{\mathbb{R}} H^n(X;\mathbb{R}) = b_n(X)$; deduce that the de Rham cohomology of a compact manifold has dimension $b_n$ (via `Thm - De Rham Theorem via Smooth Singular Chains`).
- `Exercise Index - §12.7 Cohomology, Universal Coefficients, and Products` — lists: [Ex - Cohomology of Spheres and Complex Projective Space via Universal Coefficients, Ex - Torsion in Homology Shifts Up in Cohomology, Ex - The Kronecker Pairing is Perfect over a Field]

### §12.8 Orientations, the Fundamental Class, and Poincaré Duality
- `Def - Local Homology and Local Orientation` — type: definition (compound: local homology $H_n(M, M \setminus \{x\}; R)$ of a topological $n$-manifold, the radial identifications of Bär's setup, local $R$-orientation $\mu_x$ as a generator, continuity of a family of local orientations); source items: R4.3.1, T4.3.1, (Hatcher §3.3 pp. 233–234); prereqs: [Def - Topological Manifold, Thm - Excision Theorem for Singular Homology, Thm - Long Exact Sequence of a Pair in Singular Homology, Thm - Homology of Spheres, Thm - Homotopy Invariance of Singular Homology (Gauge Theory)]; spec: For $x$ in a topological $n$-manifold $M$ and a chart ball $B \ni x$: $H_n(M, M \setminus \{x\}; R) \cong H_n(B, B \setminus \{x\}; R) \cong \tilde H_{n-1}(B \setminus \{x\}; R) \cong \tilde H_{n-1}(S^{n-1}; R) \cong R$ (excision, the pair sequence, homotopy invariance, spheres — each isomorphism written out); Bär's setup: $\dot B(x,r) := B(x,r) \setminus \{x\}$ in $\mathbb{R}^n$, $F_{x,r} : S^{n-1} \to \dot B(x,r)$, $y \mapsto x + \tfrac{r}{2} y$, a homotopy equivalence, and T4.3.1 proved: for $r_1 < r_2$, $(F_{x,r_2})_* = (\iota_{r_1, r_2})_* (F_{x,r_1})_*$ because $\iota_{r_1,r_2} \circ F_{x,r_1} \simeq F_{x,r_2}$ by the radial homotopy $y \mapsto x + \tfrac{1}{2}((1-t) r_1 + t r_2) y$; the identification $H_{n-1}(\dot B(x,r); R) \cong H_n(B(x,r), \dot B(x,r); R) \cong H_n(\mathbb{R}^n, \mathbb{R}^n \setminus \{x\}; R)$ relating Bär's and Hatcher's pictures (proved). A local $R$-orientation at $x$ is a generator $\mu_x$ of the $R$-module $H_n(M, M \setminus \{x\}; R) \cong R$ (for $R = \mathbb{Z}$: one of two; for $R = \mathbb{Z}/2$: unique); for a chart ball $B \ni x, y$, the isomorphisms $H_n(M, M \setminus B) \to H_n(M, M \setminus \{x\})$ (the map $H_n(M, M \setminus B) \to H_n(M, M \setminus \{x\})$ is an isomorphism for a ball $B$ in a chart, proved via excision to $(\mathbb{R}^n, \mathbb{R}^n \setminus B) \to (\mathbb{R}^n, \mathbb{R}^n \setminus \{x\})$ and the deformation retraction of $\mathbb{R}^n \setminus \{x\}$ onto $\mathbb{R}^n \setminus B$ for a closed ball $B$ centred at $x$, and then for $y \in B$ by a further deformation) let one transport $\mu_x$ to $\mu_y$; a family $(\mu_x)_{x \in M}$ is continuous (locally consistent) if every $x$ has a chart ball $B$ and a class $\mu_B \in H_n(M, M \setminus B; R)$ restricting to $\mu_y$ for all $y \in B$. Examples verified: $\mathbb{R}^n$ with $\mu_x$ the image of a fixed generator under translation; $S^{n-1} \cong$ boundary picture; non-example: on the Möbius strip no continuous $\mathbb{Z}$-family exists (forward link to `Def - The R-Orientation Covering`, where it is proved). Calibration check: identify $H_1(\mathbb{R}, \mathbb{R} \setminus \{0\}; \mathbb{Z})$ with $\mathbb{Z}$ and the two generators with the two "directions"; explain why $\mathbb{Z}/2$ has a unique generator.
- `Def - R-Orientation and the Fundamental Class` — type: definition (compound: $R$-orientation preserving homeomorphism, $R$-oriented atlas and $R$-orientation, $R$-orientable manifold, the fundamental class $[M]$, the homology class represented by a map, the degree); source items: D4.3.1, D4.3.2, D4.3.4, D4.3.5, E4.3.1, E4.3.2, E4.3.3, E4.3.4, T4.3.2, T4.3.3, T4.3.4; prereqs: [Def - Local Homology and Local Orientation, Def - Coordinate Chart and Atlas, Def - Topological Manifold, Thm - Existence and Uniqueness of the Fundamental Class]; spec: Bär's Definition 4.3.1 (a homeomorphism $\Phi : U \to V$ of open subsets of $\mathbb{R}^n$ is $R$-orientation preserving at $x$ iff $(\Phi|_{\dot B(x,r)})_* \circ (F_{x,r})_* = (F_{\Phi(x), \varrho})_*$ on $H_{n-1}(S^{n-1};R)$ for all admissible $r, \varrho$), Definition 4.3.5 ($R$-oriented atlas, $R$-orientation as a maximal $R$-oriented atlas, $R$-orientable), with T4.3.3 proved (one pair of radii suffices: the two-radii diagram of Rem. 4.3.4 commutes for all radii by T4.3.1, so the condition for one $(r, \varrho)$ implies it for all), T4.3.2 proved ($R = \mathbb{Z}/2$: the only automorphism of $\mathbb{Z}/2$ is the identity, so every homeomorphism is $\mathbb{Z}/2$-orientation preserving), T4.3.4 proved (every manifold is $\mathbb{Z}/2$-orientable: any atlas is $\mathbb{Z}/2$-oriented). Then the EQUIVALENCE theorem, proved on the page: an $R$-orientation in Bär's sense is the same as a continuous family of local $R$-orientations $(\mu_x)$ in the sense of `Def - Local Homology and Local Orientation` (Hatcher's definition): a chart $\Phi$ at $x$ transports the standard generator of $H_n(\mathbb{R}^n, \mathbb{R}^n \setminus 0)$ (fixed once for all via the fixed generator $[c_{n-1}]$ of $H_{n-1}(S^{n-1})$ and the radial identification) to $\mu_x$; chart changes are $R$-orientation preserving at $x$ iff they induce the identity on $H_n(\mathbb{R}^n, \mathbb{R}^n \setminus 0) \cong R$, i.e. the two transported generators agree; continuity of the family is local constancy of the chart-transported generator. The fundamental class (Definition 4.3.10): for $M$ compact, connected, $R$-oriented, $[M] \in H_n(M;R)$ is the unique class whose image in $H_n(M, M \setminus \{x\}; R)$ is $\mu_x$ for every $x$ (existence and uniqueness: forward link to `Thm - Existence and Uniqueness of the Fundamental Class`); Bär's description via the distinguished isomorphism $H_n(M;R) \xrightarrow{\partial} H_{n-1}(\dot B; R) \cong H_{n-1}(S^{n-1}; R) \cong R$ of Rem. 4.3.9 is proved equivalent on that theorem page. A class $\alpha \in H_k(X;R)$ is represented by $f : M \to X$ ($M$ a compact connected $R$-oriented $k$-manifold) iff $\alpha = f_*[M]$ (Definition 4.3.12). The degree of a map $f : M \to N$ between closed connected oriented $n$-manifolds: the integer $d$ with $f_*[M] = d [N]$ (this is the vault's homological degree; the RG IV page `Thm - Brouwer Degree is a Homotopy Invariant` is flagged and not used; homotopy invariance of this degree is immediate from `Thm - Homotopy Invariance of Singular Homology (Gauge Theory)` and is proved here). Examples verified: E4.3.1 (the picture for $n = 2$, $R = \mathbb{Z}$: a reflection of $\mathbb{R}^2$ reverses the generator — computed via the antipodal/reflection degree of `Ex - Degree of a Self-Map of the Sphere via Hurewicz`); E4.3.2 ($R = \mathbb{Z}/2$); E4.3.3 ($\mathrm{id}_M$ represents $[M]$); E4.3.4 (any $f : \{p\} \to X$ represents a generator of $H_0(X;R) \cong R$ for path connected $X$, from `Thm - Homology of a Point and of a Path-Connected Space`); non-example: the Möbius strip / $\mathbb{RP}^2$ is not $\mathbb{Z}$-orientable (forward link to `Ex - Top Homology of a Non-Orientable Closed Manifold Vanishes` for the proof). Calibration check: verify that an orientation-reversing homeomorphism of $\mathbb{R}^n$ sends $\mu_0$ to $-\mu_0$; explain why $[\overline{M}] = -[M]$; check that a diffeomorphism of degree $-1$ exists on $S^n$.
- `Def - The R-Orientation Covering` — type: definition; source items: D4.3.3, I4.3.4 (topology on $\tilde M$); prereqs: [Def - R-Orientation and the Fundamental Class, Def - Local Homology and Local Orientation, Def - Covering and the Universal Covering]; spec: $\tilde M := \{(x, \mu_x) \mid x \in M, \mu_x \text{ a local } R\text{-orientation at } x\}$ (equivalently, Bär's $\bigsqcup_x \mathcal{K}_x/\sim$ with $\Phi \sim \Psi$ iff $\Phi \circ \Psi^{-1}$ is $R$-orientation preserving at $0$ — the bijection with Hatcher's description proved as on the previous page; note $\tilde M$ has fibres in bijection with the generators of $R$, so with the units $R^\times$), topologised by the basis $U(\mu_B) := \{(y, \mu_B|_y) \mid y \in B\}$ for chart balls $B$ and $\mu_B \in H_n(M, M \setminus B; R)$ a generator (Hatcher p. 234); proved on the page: this is a basis, $p : \tilde M \to M$ is a covering (a fibre bundle with discrete fibre the set of generators of $R$; for $R = \mathbb{Z}$ a two-sheeted covering), and continuous sections of $p$ correspond bijectively to $R$-orientations of $M$ (Bär's claim in Rem. 4.3.8); for $R = \mathbb{Z}$ the subspace $\tilde M_{\pm} \subset \tilde M$ of the two generators is the orientable double cover and the page proves it is connected iff $M$ is connected and non-orientable (link `Def - Orientable Double Cover`, AT II, and check the definitions agree). Examples: $\tilde{S^n} \cong S^n \sqcup S^n$ (verified), the Möbius strip's double cover is the annulus (link `Ex - The Orientable Double Cover of the Möbius Strip`, verified against the definition here). Calibration check: describe the fibre over a point for $R = \mathbb{Z}/3$; explain why a section over a chart ball always exists; why the covering is trivial iff $M$ is orientable (for connected $M$, $R = \mathbb{Z}$).
- `Thm - Simply Connected Manifolds are Orientable` — type: theorem; source items: T4.3.6; prereqs: [Def - The R-Orientation Covering, Thm - Lifting Criterion for Coverings]; statement: If $M$ is a simply connected (hence connected, locally path connected) topological manifold then $M$ is $R$-orientable for every $R$; more precisely the page states: (i) simply connected implies $R$-orientable; (ii) for connected $M$, $\mathbb{Z}$-orientable iff the orientation double cover is disconnected (Hatcher Prop. 3.25(a)); proof: source sketch (lift $\mathrm{id}_M$ through the covering by Lemma 4.1.31, the lift is a section, a section is an orientation); the page writes it: by `Thm - Lifting Criterion for Coverings` applied to $\mathrm{id}_M : M \to M$ and the covering $\tilde M \to M$ with $M$ simply connected, a lift $\tilde{\mathrm{id}}_M$ through any point $(x, \mu_x)$ exists; it is a continuous section, hence an $R$-orientation by the section–orientation correspondence proved on the Def page; (ii) by the same correspondence and the fact that a two-sheeted covering of a connected space admits a section iff it is disconnected. spec: Targets: chapter XIII standing hypotheses (simply connected $4$-manifolds are orientable, so "oriented" there is a choice of one of two orientations, which the intersection form's sign tracks).
- `Thm - Smooth Orientability Equals Z-Orientability` — type: theorem; source items: T4.3.5, I4.3.3; prereqs: [Def - R-Orientation and the Fundamental Class, Def - Orientation of a Smooth Manifold, Def - Oriented Atlas, Thm - A Manifold is Orientable iff it Admits a Nowhere-Vanishing Top Form]; statement: For a smooth manifold $M$, a smooth atlas is oriented in the sense of `Def - Oriented Atlas` (positive Jacobian determinants of chart changes) iff it is $\mathbb{Z}$-oriented in the sense of `Def - R-Orientation and the Fundamental Class`; hence $M$ is orientable as a smooth manifold iff it is $\mathbb{Z}$-orientable, and the two notions of orientation correspond bijectively; proof: source omitted; the page proves the key lemma: a diffeomorphism $\Phi$ between open subsets of $\mathbb{R}^n$ with $\Phi(x) = y$ induces on $H_n(\mathbb{R}^n, \mathbb{R}^n \setminus \{x\}) \to H_n(\mathbb{R}^n, \mathbb{R}^n \setminus \{y\})$ multiplication by the sign of $\det d\Phi_x$ (Lemma 1: $\Phi$ is isotopic near $x$ to its derivative $d\Phi_x$ via $\Phi_t(z) = (\Phi(x + t(z - x)) - \Phi(x))/t$ for $t \in (0,1]$, $\Phi_0 = d\Phi_x$, after translating $x$ and $y$ to $0$, all fixing $0$; Lemma 2: an element of $GL_n(\mathbb{R})$ with positive determinant is connected to the identity in $GL_n(\mathbb{R})$ (link `Ex - The orthogonal group has two components`, Topology IV, for $O(n)$ and polar decomposition, or direct Gaussian-elimination path), and one with negative determinant to a reflection; Lemma 3: a reflection acts by $-1$ on $H_n(\mathbb{R}^n, \mathbb{R}^n \setminus 0)$ (via the degree computation of `Ex - Degree of a Self-Map of the Sphere via Hurewicz`); Lemma 4: a homotopy through homeomorphisms fixing $0$ induces the same map on local homology). Reference: Hatcher p. 234 (remark); the page follows Lemmas 1–4 as written. spec: Targets: chapter XIII uses the smooth orientation of $X$ and its surfaces and needs the fundamental class of `Def - R-Orientation and the Fundamental Class` to be the one integration sees: this compatibility is Lemma 5 on the page: for a compact oriented smooth $n$-manifold, $\int_M : H^n_{dR}(M) \to \mathbb{R}$ corresponds under `Thm - De Rham Theorem via Smooth Singular Chains` to evaluation on $[M]$ — proved by checking on a single small chart ball that a positively oriented smooth $n$-simplex represents $\mu_x$ (integrate a bump form).
- `Thm - Existence and Uniqueness of the Fundamental Class` — type: theorem; source items: T4.3.7, R4.3.2, I4.3.1 (first form of Poincaré duality Bär imports: $\partial : H_n(M;R) \to H_{n-1}(\dot B; R)$ is an isomorphism), D4.3.4; prereqs: [Def - Local Homology and Local Orientation, Def - R-Orientation and the Fundamental Class, Thm - Mayer-Vietoris Sequence for Singular Homology (Gauge Theory), Thm - Excision Theorem for Singular Homology, Thm - Homology of Punctured Manifolds and Connected Sums]; statement: Let $M$ be a compact connected topological $n$-manifold, $R$ a commutative ring. (a) If $M$ is $R$-oriented with orientation $(\mu_x)$, there is a unique class $[M] \in H_n(M;R)$ whose image in $H_n(M, M \setminus \{x\}; R)$ is $\mu_x$ for every $x \in M$; the map $H_n(M;R) \to H_n(M, M \setminus \{x\}; R)$ is an isomorphism for every $x$, so $H_n(M;R) \cong R$ generated by $[M]$. (b) $H_i(M;R) = 0$ for $i > n$. (c) If $M$ is not $\mathbb{Z}$-orientable then $H_n(M;\mathbb{Z}) = 0$ and $H_n(M;\mathbb{Z}/2) \cong \mathbb{Z}/2$. (d) (Bär's form, Rem. 4.3.9, corrected) For $M$ closed connected $R$-oriented and $B \ni x$ a chart ball with $\dot B = B \setminus \{x\}$, the Mayer–Vietoris connecting map $\partial : H_n(M;R) \to H_{n-1}(\dot B;R)$ for the cover $M = \dot M \cup B$ is an isomorphism, and the composite $H_n(M;R) \xrightarrow{\partial} H_{n-1}(\dot B;R) \xrightarrow{(F_{x,r})_*^{-1}} H_{n-1}(S^{n-1};R) \cong R$ sends $[M]$ to $1$ when the orientation of $M$ is the one induced by the chart; `(source typo 25: Rem. 4.3.9 claims $H_{n-1}(\dot M) \oplus H_{n-1}(\{p\}) = 0$, which is false in general, e.g. for $M = T^n$; only the injectivity of $H_n(\dot M) \oplus H_n(B) \to H_n(M)$, i.e. $H_n(\dot M;R) = 0$, and the fact that $\partial$ is an isomorphism are needed, and both are proved here)`; proof: source: sketch with the isomorphism $\partial$ imported; the page follows Hatcher Lemma 3.27 (for a compact $A \subset M$ and a continuous family $(\mu_x)$ there is a unique $\mu_A \in H_n(M, M \setminus A; R)$ restricting to all $\mu_x$, and $H_i(M, M \setminus A; R) = 0$ for $i > n$; proved by induction: (1) $A$ compact convex in a chart, (2) $A = A_1 \cup A_2$ via Hatcher's relative Mayer–Vietoris sequence $\cdots \to H_i(M, M \setminus (A_1 \cup A_2)) \to H_i(M, M \setminus A_1) \oplus H_i(M, M \setminus A_2) \to H_i(M, M \setminus (A_1 \cap A_2)) \to \cdots$, derived on the page from the small-chains theorem applied to the cover $\{M \setminus A_1, M \setminus A_2\}$ of $M \setminus (A_1 \cap A_2)$, (3) $A$ compact in $\mathbb{R}^n$ by approximation with finite unions of cubes and a direct-limit/compactness argument, (4) general $A$ by a finite chart cover) and Thm 3.26 with $A = M$; (d) is proved by identifying the MV connecting map for $M = \dot M \cup B$ with the composite $H_n(M) \to H_n(M, \dot M) \cong H_n(B, \dot B) \cong H_{n-1}(\dot B)$ (naturality of connecting maps, excision) and using (a) plus $H_n(\dot M; R) = 0$ ($H_n(\dot M;R) = 0$ for the connected non-compact manifold $\dot M$: Hatcher Prop. 3.29, proved on the page: a class in $H_n(\dot M)$ is carried by a compact set, hence by Lemma 3.27 corresponds to a section of the orientation covering supported on that compact set, which must vanish on the unbounded component). Reference: Hatcher Thm 3.26, Lemma 3.27, Prop. 3.29, pp. 236–238. spec: Why-true: "a top-dimensional class is determined by its local pieces, and a compatible family of local pieces glues to exactly one class; without orientability the local pieces cannot be chosen compatibly"; Sources: closed oriented smooth manifolds (via the previous page), simply connected closed manifolds, $R = \mathbb{Z}/2$ for any closed manifold; Targets: Poincaré duality, degree theory, chapter XIII's representability and intersection numbers.
- `Thm - Triangulations Represent the Fundamental Class` — type: theorem; source items: T4.3.8, I4.3.4 (second part); prereqs: [Thm - Existence and Uniqueness of the Fundamental Class, Def - Local Homology and Local Orientation]; statement: Let $M$ be a closed connected $R$-oriented $n$-manifold and $T$ a triangulation, i.e. a homeomorphism $|K| \to M$ from a finite simplicial complex, with the $n$-simplices $\sigma_1, \dots, \sigma_N$ (as singular simplices via the vertex orderings) oriented compatibly with the orientation of $M$ (each $\sigma_i$ restricted to its interior carries $\mu_{\text{standard}}$ to $\mu_x$). Then $c := \sum_i \sigma_i$ is a cycle and $[c] = [M]$; proof: source omitted; the page proves (Lemma 1) $\partial c = 0$: each $(n-1)$-simplex is a face of exactly two $n$-simplices (assume, as part of the hypothesis "triangulation of a manifold", that each $(n-1)$-simplex is a face of exactly two $n$-simplices with opposite induced orientations (this is how Bär's "suitably parametrised, oriented simplices" is read), so that $\partial c = 0$ is a direct cancellation; (Lemma 2) for $x$ in the interior of $\sigma_i$, the image of $[c]$ in $H_n(M, M \setminus \{x\})$ equals the image of $\sigma_i$ alone (the other simplices are chains in $M \setminus \{x\}$), which is $\mu_x$ by the orientation hypothesis; by uniqueness in `Thm - Existence and Uniqueness of the Fundamental Class`, $[c] = [M]$. spec: the page states clearly that existence of triangulations of manifolds is NOT proved or used anywhere in the series; this theorem is only an interpretation of $[M]$ (Bär Rem. 4.3.11), used in chapter XIII to see that an embedded oriented surface $S \subset X$, triangulated, represents $\iota_*[S]$ as a sum of singular simplices (used in `Thm - Intersection Form via de Rham Cohomology` to compute $\int_S \omega$ as a sum over simplices — there the surface is given with an explicit smooth triangulation, e.g. from a smooth structure on $S$ via the standard triangulation of the sphere/torus).
- `Thm - Relative Fundamental Class of a Compact Manifold with Boundary` — type: theorem; source items: I4.3.2 (the input for Rem. 4.3.14), (Hatcher proves the relative fundamental class inside the proof of Thm 3.43 via a collar); prereqs: [Thm - Existence and Uniqueness of the Fundamental Class, Def - Manifold with Boundary and Induced Orientation, Def - Smooth Manifold with Boundary, Thm - Long Exact Sequence of a Pair in Singular Homology, Thm - Homology of a Compact Smooth Manifold is Finitely Generated]; statement: Let $W$ be a compact connected $R$-oriented topological $n$-manifold with boundary whose boundary has a collar (a neighbourhood of $\partial W$ homeomorphic to $\partial W \times [0,1)$ with $\partial W \leftrightarrow \partial W \times \{0\}$); every smooth manifold with boundary has a collar (proved on the page as Lemma 1 via the flow of an inward vector field, Lee Thm 9.25, or cited from `Thm - Homology of a Compact Smooth Manifold is Finitely Generated` Lemma C). Then there is a unique class $[W, \partial W] \in H_n(W, \partial W; R)$ restricting to $\mu_x$ in $H_n(W, W \setminus \{x\}; R)$ for every interior point $x$; $H_n(W, \partial W; R) \cong R$; and $\partial [W, \partial W] = [\partial W] \in H_{n-1}(\partial W; R)$ where $\partial W$ carries the induced orientation (the page fixes the induced orientation of $\partial W$ homologically — as the one for which this formula holds with the outward-normal-first convention — and proves in Lemma 2 that for smooth $W$ it agrees with `Def - Manifold with Boundary and Induced Orientation`, by a local computation in the half-space model $H^n = \{x_n \ge 0\}$: Lemma 2 computes, for the half-ball $D_+ = \bar B^n \cap H^n$, that the connecting map $H_n(D_+, \partial D_+) \to H_{n-1}(\partial D_+) $ followed by restriction to the flat part sends the relative generator to the generator of $H_{n-1}(\bar B^{n-1}, S^{n-2})$ determined by the outward-normal-first convention, by an explicit chain-level computation with one singular $n$-simplex; proof: source: none; the page follows Hatcher's proof of Thm 3.43 (pp. 253–254): let $W' := W \setminus (\partial W \times [0,1/2))$, a compact subset of the open manifold $M := W \setminus \partial W$; apply Hatcher Lemma 3.27 (from `Thm - Existence and Uniqueness of the Fundamental Class`) to $A = W'$ in $M$ to get $\mu_{W'} \in H_n(M, M \setminus W')$, identify $H_n(M, M \setminus W') \cong H_n(W, \partial W)$ via the collar (excision and homotopy invariance), and check the boundary formula via the pair sequence and the identification $H_{n-1}(\partial W \times (0, 1/2)) \cong H_{n-1}(\partial W)$; uniqueness from the uniqueness in Lemma 3.27; $\partial[W,\partial W]$ is the fundamental class of $\partial W$ because it restricts to a generator at every point of $\partial W$ (local computation of Lemma 2) and the fundamental class is characterised locally. Reference: Hatcher Thm 3.43 proof, Lemma 3.27; Bredon VI.9. spec: Targets: `Thm - Bounding Classes Vanish`, Poincaré–Lefschetz duality, cobordism invariance of the signature (chapter XIII).
- `Thm - Bounding Classes Vanish` — type: theorem; source items: T4.3.10, R4.3.3, I4.3.2; prereqs: [Thm - Relative Fundamental Class of a Compact Manifold with Boundary, Def - R-Orientation and the Fundamental Class, Thm - Long Exact Sequence of a Pair in Singular Homology]; statement: (i) If $W$ is a compact $R$-oriented $(k+1)$-manifold with collared boundary (e.g. smooth) and $\iota : \partial W \hookrightarrow W$, then $\iota_*[\partial W] = 0$ in $H_k(W;R)$, where $[\partial W] = \sum_j [\partial_j W]$ is the sum of the fundamental classes of the components of $\partial W$ with their induced orientations. (ii) (Bär, Rem. 4.3.14) If $\alpha \in H_k(X;R)$ is represented by $f : M \to X$, $W$ is a compact connected $R$-oriented $(k+1)$-manifold with collared boundary $\partial W = M$, and $F : W \to X$ is continuous with $F|_{\partial W} = f$, then $\alpha = 0$. (iii) More generally, if $\partial W = M_1 \sqcup \overline{M_2}$ and $F|_{M_i} = f_i$, then $(f_1)_*[M_1] = (f_2)_*[M_2]$ — the form used in chapter XIII Step 4; proof: source: (ii) full given (i), (i) imported; the page proves (i) from $\iota_*[\partial W] = \iota_* \partial [W, \partial W] = 0$ by exactness of the pair sequence $H_{k+1}(W, \partial W) \xrightarrow{\partial} H_k(\partial W) \xrightarrow{\iota_*} H_k(W)$, (ii) as Bär: $\alpha = f_*[M] = (F \circ \iota)_*[M] = F_*(\iota_*[M]) = 0$, (iii) from (i) with $[\partial W] = [M_1] - [M_2]$ (orientation reversal negates the fundamental class, proved on `Def - R-Orientation and the Fundamental Class`). spec: Why-true: "the boundary of a fundamental chain is the fundamental chain of the boundary, and boundaries are zero in homology"; Targets: $[S^2 \times \{p\}] = [S^2 \times \{p'\}]$ (chapter XIII, $Q_{S^2 \times S^2}$); the handle-attachment step; cobordism invariance arguments.
- `Def - Compactly Supported Cohomology and Direct Limits` — type: definition (compound: direct systems and direct limits of abelian groups, compactly supported cochains, $H^k_c(X;R)$, the description as $\varinjlim_K H^k(X, X \setminus K; R)$, and $H^k_c$ of $\mathbb{R}^n$); source items: (Hatcher §3.3 pp. 242–245); prereqs: [Def - Singular Cohomology with Coefficients and the Kronecker Pairing, Thm - Excision Theorem for Singular Homology, Thm - Homology of Spheres]; spec: Direct limits over a directed set with the universal property and the exactness of $\varinjlim$ (proved: a direct limit of exact sequences is exact, Hatcher p. 243); $C^k_c(X;R)$ := cochains vanishing on all chains in $X \setminus K$ for some compact $K$; $H^k_c(X;R)$; the isomorphism $H^k_c(X;R) \cong \varinjlim_K H^k(X, X \setminus K; R)$ over compact $K$ ordered by inclusion (proved); functoriality under proper maps and, covariantly, under inclusions of open sets $U \hookrightarrow X$ (extension by zero — the map $H^k_c(U) \to H^k_c(X)$, defined via $H^k(U, U \setminus K) \cong H^k(X, X \setminus K)$ by excision, proved well defined); computation $H^k_c(\mathbb{R}^n;R) \cong R$ for $k = n$ and $0$ otherwise (via the cofinal system of balls and $H^k(\mathbb{R}^n, \mathbb{R}^n \setminus B) \cong H^k(D^n, S^{n-1})$ from `Thm - Universal Coefficient Theorem for Cohomology` and `Thm - Homology of Spheres`). Examples verified: $H^*_c = H^*$ for compact $X$; $H^0_c(\mathbb{R}) = 0$ (verified directly). Calibration check: explain why $H^*_c$ is not homotopy invariant ($\mathbb{R}^n$ versus a point); why extension by zero is covariant; compute $H^1_c(S^1 \times \mathbb{R})$ from the Künneth-free argument via the cofinal annuli.
- `Thm - Poincare Duality` — type: theorem; source items: I4.3.1, I4.3.2 (as corollaries), I5.1.4 (chapter XIII's unimodularity input), I5.2.1 (homological definition of $Q_X$); prereqs: [Def - Cap Product, Def - Compactly Supported Cohomology and Direct Limits, Thm - Existence and Uniqueness of the Fundamental Class, Thm - Mayer-Vietoris Sequence for Singular Homology (Gauge Theory), Thm - Excision Theorem for Singular Homology, Thm - Universal Coefficient Theorem for Cohomology, Def - Local Homology and Local Orientation]; statement: (A) (Hatcher Thm 3.30) Let $M$ be a closed $R$-oriented $n$-manifold with fundamental class $[M]$. Then $D_M : H^k(M;R) \to H_{n-k}(M;R)$, $D_M(\phi) := [M] \frown \phi$, is an isomorphism for all $k$. (B) (Non-compact form, Hatcher Thm 3.35) For an $R$-oriented $n$-manifold $M$ (not necessarily compact), $D_M : H^k_c(M;R) \to H_{n-k}(M;R)$, defined as the direct limit of the maps $H^k(M, M \setminus K) \to H_{n-k}(M)$, $\phi \mapsto \mu_K \frown \phi$, is an isomorphism. (C) (Bär's first imported form, I4.3.1) For $M$ closed connected $R$-oriented and $B$ a chart ball around $x$, $\partial : H_n(M;R) \to H_{n-1}(\dot B;R)$ is an isomorphism — this is `Thm - Existence and Uniqueness of the Fundamental Class` (d), restated here because Bär attributes it to Poincaré duality. (D) (Bär's second imported form, I4.3.2) is `Thm - Bounding Classes Vanish` (i), likewise restated. (E) For $M$ closed connected $R$-oriented, $H^n(M;R) \cong R$ and $H_0(M;R) \cong R$, and the Kronecker pairing $H^n(M;R) \times H_n(M;R) \to R$ is perfect; proof: source: none (imported); the page follows Hatcher Thm 3.35 with (A) as the compact case: (Lemma 1) $D_U$ is an isomorphism for $U \cong \mathbb{R}^n$ (both sides $R$ in degree $n$ resp. $0$, and the cap product of the local generator with the dual generator is the point class: explicit check); (Lemma 2) if $M = U \cup V$ with $U$, $V$ open and $D_U$, $D_V$, $D_{U \cap V}$ isomorphisms then $D_M$ is an isomorphism (the Mayer–Vietoris sequence for $H^*_c$ — derived on the page from the relative MV sequence and the direct limit's exactness — mapped by $D$ to the Mayer–Vietoris sequence in homology, commutativity up to sign of every square proved (Hatcher's computation with the naturality of cap products and the sign $(-1)^k$ in the connecting square), and the five lemma (proved on `Thm - De Rham Theorem via Smooth Singular Chains` as a lemma; restated here)); (Lemma 3) if $M = \bigcup_i U_i$ is an increasing union of open sets with all $D_{U_i}$ isomorphisms then $D_M$ is an isomorphism (compactness of singular chains: every class in $H_*(M)$ and every class in $H^*_c(M)$ lives on some $U_i$; the direct limit argument); (Lemma 4) every open subset of $\mathbb{R}^n$ is handled by Lemmas 1–3 (write $U$ as a countable increasing union of finite unions of convex open sets, induct on the number of convex sets with Lemma 2 — intersections of convex sets are convex, hence homeomorphic to $\mathbb{R}^n$ or empty); (Lemma 5) general $M$: a Zorn's lemma argument on open subsets where $D$ is an isomorphism, or for second countable $M$ (all manifolds here) a countable chart cover and Lemmas 2–4. (E) from (A) with $k = n$ and `Thm - Universal Coefficient Theorem for Cohomology` (b)/(a) — precisely: $H^n(M;R) \cong H_0(M;R) \cong R$ by (A). Reference: Hatcher Thm 3.30, Thm 3.35, Lemma 3.36, pp. 241–248. spec: Why-true: "capping with the fundamental class turns a cochain that measures $k$-dimensional things into the $(n-k)$-dimensional thing it is dual to; locally this is the identity of $R$ and Mayer–Vietoris propagates it"; Sources: closed oriented smooth manifolds; simply connected closed manifolds; $\mathbb{Z}/2$ coefficients for arbitrary closed manifolds; Targets: `Thm - Cup Product Pairing is Unimodular on a Closed Oriented Manifold`, Poincaré–Lefschetz, chapter XIII throughout.
- `Thm - Cup Product Pairing is Unimodular on a Closed Oriented Manifold` — type: theorem; source items: I5.1.4 (the precise form needed for T5.1.10), I5.2.1; prereqs: [Thm - Poincare Duality, Thm - Universal Coefficient Theorem for Cohomology, Def - Cup Product, Def - Cap Product, Thm - Homology of a Compact Smooth Manifold is Finitely Generated]; statement: Let $M$ be a closed connected $R$-oriented $n$-manifold. (i) For $R$ a field, the pairing $H^k(M;R) \times H^{n-k}(M;R) \to R$, $(\alpha, \beta) \mapsto \langle \alpha \smile \beta, [M] \rangle$, is non-degenerate. (ii) For $R = \mathbb{Z}$ and $M$ with finitely generated homology (e.g. compact smooth), the pairing $H^k(M;\mathbb{Z})/\text{torsion} \times H^{n-k}(M;\mathbb{Z})/\text{torsion} \to \mathbb{Z}$ is unimodular: it induces an isomorphism $H^k/\text{torsion} \to \operatorname{Hom}(H^{n-k}/\text{torsion}, \mathbb{Z})$. (iii) In particular for a closed oriented $4$-manifold with $H_1(M;\mathbb{Z}) = 0$: $H^2(M;\mathbb{Z})$ is free, $H_2(M;\mathbb{Z}) \cong H^2(M;\mathbb{Z})$ is free, and the form $Q^{\smile}(\alpha, \beta) := \langle \alpha \smile \beta, [M] \rangle$ on $H^2(M;\mathbb{Z})$ is symmetric and unimodular, i.e. has determinant $\pm 1$ in any basis; proof: source: none; the page follows Hatcher Prop. 3.38: precisely: $\langle \alpha \smile \beta, [M]\rangle = \langle \beta, [M] \frown \alpha \rangle$ (the cap–cup identity from `Def - Cap Product`), so the pairing is the composite of $D_M$ (an isomorphism) with the evaluation $H_{n-k}(M) \to \operatorname{Hom}(H^{n-k}(M), R)$ which is an isomorphism for a field, and an isomorphism modulo torsion for $\mathbb{Z}$ by the universal coefficient theorem (the map $H_{n-k}/T \to \operatorname{Hom}(H^{n-k}/T, \mathbb{Z})$ is an isomorphism when homology is finitely generated: proved via UCT (c)); symmetry in (iii) from graded commutativity with $k = l = 2$ (`Thm - Properties of the Cup Product`); the determinant statement: a bilinear form on $\mathbb{Z}^r$ inducing an isomorphism onto the dual has matrix in $GL_r(\mathbb{Z})$, hence determinant $\pm 1$ (proved). Reference: Hatcher Prop. 3.38 and Cor. 3.39, p. 250. spec: Targets: `Thm - Intersection Form is Unimodular` (chapter XIII); Betti number symmetry exercise.
- `Thm - Poincare-Lefschetz Duality for Manifolds with Boundary` — type: theorem; source items: (Hatcher Thm 3.43; needed for `Thm - Cobordism Invariance of the Signature` in chapter XIII, i.e. for I5.3.4); prereqs: [Thm - Poincare Duality, Thm - Relative Fundamental Class of a Compact Manifold with Boundary, Def - Cap Product, Thm - Long Exact Sequence of a Pair in Singular Homology]; statement: Let $W$ be a compact $R$-oriented $n$-manifold with collared boundary $\partial W$ (e.g. smooth). Then cap product with $[W, \partial W]$ gives isomorphisms $H^k(W, \partial W; R) \to H_{n-k}(W;R)$ and $H^k(W;R) \to H_{n-k}(W, \partial W; R)$, and these fit into a commutative ladder (up to sign) between the long exact sequence of the pair $(W, \partial W)$ in cohomology and that in homology, with the vertical map $H^k(\partial W) \to H_{n-1-k}(\partial W)$ being Poincaré duality of the closed manifold $\partial W$; proof: source: none; the page follows Hatcher Thm 3.43 (pp. 253–254): with $M := W \setminus \partial W$ and the collar, $H^k(W, \partial W) \cong H^k_c(M)$ and $H_{n-k}(W) \cong H_{n-k}(M)$, and the first isomorphism is the non-compact Poincaré duality (B); the second isomorphism follows from the first, the ladder, and the five lemma (once the ladder is shown to commute up to sign, which is the naturality of cap products and the boundary formula of `Def - Cap Product`). Reference: Hatcher Thm 3.43. spec: Targets: chapter XIII's `Thm - Cobordism Invariance of the Signature` (the Lagrangian-subspace argument).
- `Thm - Homology of the Product of Two Two-Spheres` — type: theorem; source items: T4.3.9, E4.3.6; prereqs: [Thm - Mayer-Vietoris Sequence for Singular Homology (Gauge Theory), Thm - Homology of Punctured Manifolds and Connected Sums, Thm - Homology of Spheres, Def - R-Orientation and the Fundamental Class, Thm - Existence and Uniqueness of the Fundamental Class]; statement: $H_k(S^2 \times S^2; R) \cong R$ for $k \in \{0, 4\}$, $\cong R^2$ for $k = 2$, and $0$ for $k \in \{1, 3\}$ and $k > 4$; the maps $f_1 : S^2 \to S^2 \times S^2$, $x \mapsto (x, p_2)$, and $f_2 : x \mapsto (p_1, x)$ represent a basis $(f_1)_*[S^2]$, $(f_2)_*[S^2]$ of $H_2(S^2 \times S^2; R)$; proof: source full; the page: $\iota : (S^2 \times S^2)^{\cdot} := (S^2 \times S^2) \setminus \{(-p_1, -p_2)\} \hookrightarrow S^2 \times S^2$ induces an isomorphism on $H_2$ (punctured-manifold theorem, $n = 4$, $k = 2$); cover $(S^2 \times S^2)^{\cdot}$ by $X_0 := S^2 \times (S^2 \setminus \{-p_2\}) \simeq S^2 \times \{p_2\}$ and $X_1 := (S^2 \setminus \{-p_1\}) \times S^2 \simeq \{p_1\} \times S^2$ `(source typo 26: Ex. 4.3.13(4) prints $X_1 = (S^2 \setminus \{-p_2\}) \times S^2$; it must be $S^2 \setminus \{-p_1\}$)`, with $X_0 \cap X_1 \cong D^2 \times D^2$ contractible (explicit deformation retractions written); MV gives $0 \to H_2(X_0) \oplus H_2(X_1) \to H_2((S^2 \times S^2)^{\cdot}) \to H_1(\text{pt}) = 0$; the identification of the summands with $(f_1)_*[S^2]$, $(f_2)_*[S^2]$ via the fundamental class of $S^2$ representing the generator of $H_2(S^2)$; the remaining degrees ($k = 0, 1, 3, 4$) by MV on the same cover plus the punctured-manifold theorem and `Thm - Existence and Uniqueness of the Fundamental Class` for $H_4$ (orientability of the product from the smooth orientation, `Thm - Smooth Orientability Equals Z-Orientability`), $H_3$ and $H_4$: the writer derives $H_3$ and $H_4$ carefully from the two MV sequences (for $M = \dot M \cup B$ with $\dot M \cap B \simeq S^3$: $H_4(\dot M) = 0 \to H_4(M) \to H_3(S^3) = R \to H_3(\dot M) \oplus 0 \to H_3(M) \to H_2(S^3) = 0$; with $H_3(\dot M) = 0$ from the first cover, $H_4(M) \cong R$ and $H_3(M) = 0$). spec: Targets: chapter XIII's $Q_{S^2 \times S^2}$; also E4.3.5.
- `Ex - Every Topological Manifold is Orientable over Z mod 2` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: T4.3.4, E4.3.2; prereqs: [Def - R-Orientation and the Fundamental Class]; spec: Prove Rem. 4.3.6 from Ex. 4.3.3 (every homeomorphism is $\mathbb{Z}/2$-orientation preserving because $\operatorname{Aut}(\mathbb{Z}/2) = 1$); deduce that every closed connected manifold has $H_n(M;\mathbb{Z}/2) \cong \mathbb{Z}/2$ with a canonical fundamental class.
- `Ex - The Fundamental Class of the Sphere and the Identity Map` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: E4.3.3, E4.3.4; prereqs: [Def - R-Orientation and the Fundamental Class, Thm - Existence and Uniqueness of the Fundamental Class]; spec: Show $\mathrm{id}_M$ represents $[M]$; show that $[S^n]$ equals the fixed generator $[c_n]$ of `Def - Hurewicz Homomorphism` for the orientation induced by the standard orientation of $\mathbb{R}^{n+1}$ (check on one local homology group); show any map from a point represents a generator of $H_0$.
- `Ex - Projective Subspaces Represent the Generators of the Homology of CP^n` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: E4.3.5; prereqs: [Thm - Homology of Complex Projective Space, Def - R-Orientation and the Fundamental Class, Thm - Smooth Orientability Equals Z-Orientability]; spec: Show that the inclusion $\iota : \mathbb{CP}^k \hookrightarrow \mathbb{CP}^n$ represents a generator of $H_{2k}(\mathbb{CP}^n;\mathbb{Z})$ for $1 \le k \le n$, with $\mathbb{CP}^k$ carrying the complex orientation: from the MV computation, $H_{2k}(\mathbb{CP}^n) \cong H_{2k}(\mathbb{CP}^{n-1}) \cong \cdots \cong H_{2k}(\mathbb{CP}^k) \cong \mathbb{Z}$ generated by $[\mathbb{CP}^k]$ (the last by `Thm - Existence and Uniqueness of the Fundamental Class`), the isomorphisms being induced by inclusions. Key decision: track that every isomorphism in the induction is induced by an inclusion.
- `Ex - Top Homology of a Non-Orientable Closed Manifold Vanishes` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: (Hatcher 3.26(b), used for the non-example on the Def page); prereqs: [Thm - Existence and Uniqueness of the Fundamental Class, Def - The R-Orientation Covering]; spec: Prove statement (c) of `Thm - Existence and Uniqueness of the Fundamental Class` from the section–orientation correspondence: a class in $H_n(M;\mathbb{Z})$ gives a continuous family of local classes, i.e. a section of $\tilde M \to M$ with values in $\{k \mu_x\}$; on a connected non-orientable $M$ the section must take value $0$. Apply to $\mathbb{RP}^2$ and compare with `Ex - Singular Homology of RP^2` (AT I).
- `Ex - Betti Numbers of a Closed Oriented Manifold are Symmetric` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: (consequence of Poincaré duality); prereqs: [Thm - Poincare Duality, Thm - Universal Coefficient Theorem for Cohomology, Thm - Homology of a Compact Smooth Manifold is Finitely Generated]; spec: For a closed oriented smooth $n$-manifold show $b_k = b_{n-k}$ and that the torsion of $H_k(M;\mathbb{Z})$ is isomorphic to the torsion of $H_{n-k-1}(M;\mathbb{Z})$; deduce $H_{n-1}(M;\mathbb{Z})$ is free.
- `Ex - Euler Characteristic of an Odd-Dimensional Closed Manifold Vanishes` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: (consequence; used in chapter XIII for $\chi$ of the circle bundle in the K3 computation); prereqs: [Ex - Betti Numbers of a Closed Oriented Manifold are Symmetric, Ex - Every Topological Manifold is Orientable over Z mod 2]; spec: Prove $\chi(M) = 0$ for every closed smooth manifold of odd dimension, first in the orientable case from $b_k = b_{n-k}$, then in general using $\mathbb{Z}/2$-Betti numbers (prove that $\chi$ can be computed with $\mathbb{Z}/2$ coefficients via the universal coefficient theorem: $\sum (-1)^k \dim_{\mathbb{Z}/2} H^k(M;\mathbb{Z}/2) = \chi(M)$, since torsion contributes equally to consecutive degrees).
- `Exercise Index - §12.8 Orientations, the Fundamental Class, and Poincaré Duality` — lists: [Ex - Every Topological Manifold is Orientable over Z mod 2, Ex - The Fundamental Class of the Sphere and the Identity Map, Ex - Projective Subspaces Represent the Generators of the Homology of CP^n, Ex - Top Homology of a Non-Orientable Closed Manifold Vanishes, Ex - Betti Numbers of a Closed Oriented Manifold are Symmetric, Ex - Euler Characteristic of an Odd-Dimensional Closed Manifold Vanishes]

Topic page `Gauge Theory XII — Homotopy, Homology, Orientation, and Poincaré Duality`: Notation Registry with the standing conventions above; Motivation opens with the sentence "Everything chapter XIII does with a four-manifold is a statement about its second homology, and this chapter builds that group from nothing"; structural backbone display: $\pi_n \xrightarrow{h} H_n \xleftarrow{\ \frown [M]\ } H^{n-k}$; concept map with the eight sections; no `# Imported Results` section (the chapter imports nothing). Cross-chapter: chapter XIII depends on §12.2 (van Kampen), §12.5–§12.8 wholesale; chapter III depends on §12.3 (long exact sequence) and §12.7 (de Rham with integral classes) — the orchestrator should check that chapter III's `Thm - Classification of Principal U(1)-Bundles by the First Chern Class` cites `Thm - De Rham Theorem via Smooth Singular Chains` for its integrality statements rather than the flagged DG X page. Chapter XII also carries `Thm - Hermitian Line Bundles are Classified by the Integral Second Cohomology` (§12.7), which discharges manifest-1's deferred identification $\operatorname{Pic}(M) \cong H^2(M;\mathbb{Z})$; chapter XIII depends on it (the reducible-connection count in §13.7 and the Freedman realisation in §13.5 need every integral $H^2$ class to be a $c_1$).
