### §13.4 The Spin Condition and Rokhlin's Theorem
- `Thm - Even Intersection Form iff Spin` — type: theorem; source items: T5.1.14, I5.1.7; prereqs: [Def - Intersection Form of a Four-Manifold, Def - Spin Structure and Spin-c Structure, Thm - Every Class in the Second Homology of a Simply Connected Four-Manifold is Represented by an Embedded Surface, Ex - Self-Intersection Equals the Euler Number of the Normal Bundle, Thm - Universal Coefficient Theorem for Cohomology, Thm - Homology of a Simply Connected Closed Four-Manifold, Thm - Poincare-Hopf Theorem for Surfaces]; statement: Let $X$ be a simply connected compact oriented smooth four-manifold. Then $Q_X$ is even if and only if $X$ admits a spin structure; in that case the spin structure is unique (since $H^1(X;\mathbb{Z}/2) = 0$); proof: source omitted; the page proves, with the hypotheses `[NEEDED FROM I–XI: Stiefel–Whitney classes $w_1, w_2$ with naturality and the Whitney sum formula; $w_2(E) = e(E) \bmod 2$ for oriented rank-two bundles; a spin structure on an oriented manifold exists iff $w_2(TM) = 0$, and spin structures form a torsor over $H^1(M;\mathbb{Z}/2)$]` stated as explicit hypotheses with wikilinks to the chapter VIII/VI pages once named: (Lemma 1, Wu-type formula) for every embedded oriented closed surface $S \subset X$, $\langle w_2(TX), [S] \rangle \equiv Q_X([S],[S]) \pmod 2$: since $TX|_S = TS \oplus \nu_S$, $w_2(TX)|_S = w_2(TS) + w_1(TS) w_1(\nu_S) + w_2(\nu_S) = w_2(TS) + w_2(\nu_S)$ (both bundles oriented, so $w_1 = 0$); $\langle w_2(TS), [S] \rangle = \chi(S) \bmod 2 = 0$ ($w_2(TS) = e(TS) \bmod 2$ and $\langle e(TS), [S] \rangle = \chi(S)$ by `Thm - Poincare-Hopf Theorem for Surfaces` together with `[NEEDED FROM I–XI: the Euler class counts zeros of a transverse section]`, and $\chi(S) = 2 - 2g$ is even); $\langle w_2(\nu_S), [S] \rangle = \langle e(\nu_S), [S] \rangle \bmod 2 = Q_X([S],[S]) \bmod 2$ by `Ex - Self-Intersection Equals the Euler Number of the Normal Bundle`; (Lemma 2) $H^2(X;\mathbb{Z}/2) \cong \operatorname{Hom}(H_2(X;\mathbb{Z}), \mathbb{Z}/2)$ (universal coefficients, $H_1 = 0$), so $w_2(TX) = 0$ iff $\langle w_2(TX), a \rangle = 0$ for all $a \in H_2(X;\mathbb{Z})$, iff (by Lemma 1 and representability) $Q_X(a,a)$ is even for all $a$; (Conclusion) spin iff $w_2 = 0$ iff even; uniqueness from $H^1(X;\mathbb{Z}/2) = \operatorname{Hom}(H_1, \mathbb{Z}/2) = 0$. Reference: Gompf–Stipsicz, *4-Manifolds and Kirby Calculus*, §1.4 (Wu's formula in dimension four, Prop. 1.4.18, and the discussion of spin structures); Kirby, *The Topology of 4-Manifolds*, Ch. IV; Milnor–Stasheff §11 for the Wu formula in general (not needed). The page states clearly which of its inputs are NEEDED items and must be reconciled by the orchestrator, and that if the series does not provide Stiefel–Whitney classes the orchestrator must add `Def - Stiefel-Whitney Classes` and `Thm - Spin Structures Exist iff the Second Stiefel-Whitney Class Vanishes` to chapter VIII. spec: Why-true: "the second Stiefel–Whitney class of $X$ is exactly the mod-two self-intersection function, and a spin structure is exactly the vanishing of that class"; Targets: Rokhlin's theorem's hypothesis; $K3$ is spin; $\mathbb{CP}^2$ is not spin.
- `Thm - Rokhlin Theorem` — type: theorem; source items: T5.1.15, I5.1.8, I5.1.9; prereqs: [Thm - Even Intersection Form iff Spin, Def - Spinor Bundle and Dirac Operator, Def - Spin Structure and Spin-c Structure, Def - Fredholm Operator and Index, Thm - Elliptic Operators on Closed Manifolds are Fredholm, Def - Quaternions, Def - Pontryagin Classes]; statement: Let $X$ be a simply connected compact oriented smooth four-manifold with $Q_X$ even. Then $\operatorname{sign}(X) \in 16\mathbb{Z}$; proof: source: sketch; the page carries the pre-approved import callout (Import 3, the Atiyah–Singer index theorem) in the following exact form: "for a closed spin Riemannian four-manifold $X$ with spinor bundles $\slashed S^\pm$ and chiral Dirac operator $D^+ : \Gamma(\slashed S^+) \to \Gamma(\slashed S^-)$, $\operatorname{ind}_{\mathbb{C}} D^+ = \dim_{\mathbb{C}} \ker D^+ - \dim_{\mathbb{C}} \ker D^- = \langle \hat A(TX), [X] \rangle = -\tfrac{1}{24} \langle p_1(TX), [X] \rangle$; and for any closed oriented smooth four-manifold $\operatorname{sign}(X) = \tfrac13 \langle p_1(TX), [X] \rangle$ (Hirzebruch), so that $\operatorname{ind}_{\mathbb{C}} D^+ = -\tfrac18 \operatorname{sign}(X)$" (source: Lawson–Michelsohn, *Spin Geometry*, Thm III.13.10 (index theorem), Thm IV.1.1 ($\hat A$ formula for the Dirac operator) and the signature theorem stated in Ch. IV §1, plus Hirzebruch, *Topological Methods in Algebraic Geometry*, Thm 8.2.2); architecture paragraph: heat-kernel or K-theoretic proof, the difficulty being the local index density; why imported: book-length; `(source: Bär prints $\operatorname{ind}(D^+) = \tfrac18 \operatorname{sign}(X)$; the sign depends on the chirality convention for $\slashed S^\pm$ and does not affect the divisibility conclusion; the page records the convention it uses and the sign that goes with it)`). Then the page proves: (Lemma 1) $X$ is spin with a unique spin structure (`Thm - Even Intersection Form iff Spin`), so $\slashed S^\pm$ and $D^\pm$ exist (chapter VIII anchors), $D^\pm$ are elliptic, hence Fredholm with finite-dimensional kernels (chapter IX anchors); (Lemma 2) $\operatorname{sign}(X) = -8 \operatorname{ind}_{\mathbb{C}} D^+ \in 8\mathbb{Z}$ by the import; (Lemma 3, quaternionic structure) the bundles $\slashed S^\pm$ carry a parallel, $\mathbb{C}$-antilinear bundle map $J$ with $J^2 = -\mathrm{id}$ commuting with Clifford multiplication by real tangent vectors and with the spin connection, hence with $D^\pm$ (`[NEEDED FROM I–XI: the quaternionic structure on $\slashed S^\pm$ in dimension four, Haydys D4.1.4 / E4.1.4: $\slashed S^+ \oplus \slashed S^- = \mathbb{H} \oplus \mathbb{H}$ with Clifford multiplication by $h \in \mathbb{H} = \mathbb{R}^4$ given by $(v_1, v_2) \mapsto (h v_2, -\bar h v_1)$, and $J$ = right multiplication by $j$, which commutes with left multiplications and with the $Sp(1) \times Sp(1) = \operatorname{Spin}(4)$ action, hence is parallel]` — the page proves from this model that $J$ commutes with $D^\pm$ (in a local orthonormal frame $D = \sum e_i \cdot \nabla_{e_i}$ and both factors commute with $J$); (Lemma 4) $\ker D^\pm$ are $J$-invariant complex subspaces, hence quaternionic vector spaces, hence of even complex dimension (a finite-dimensional complex vector space with an antilinear $J$, $J^2 = -1$, is a free $\mathbb{H}$-module, so $\dim_{\mathbb{C}} = 2 \dim_{\mathbb{H}}$ — proved: choose $v \ne 0$, then $v, Jv$ are $\mathbb{C}$-independent, and induct on the $\mathbb{H}$-invariant complement); (Conclusion) $\operatorname{ind}_{\mathbb{C}} D^+ \in 2\mathbb{Z}$, so $\operatorname{sign}(X) = -8 \operatorname{ind} D^+ \in 16\mathbb{Z}$. spec: Why-true: "the index theorem makes the signature eight times an index, and quaternions make that index even"; Sources: even forms (spin, by the previous page), $c_1 = 0$ complex surfaces; Targets: $M_{E_8}$ is not smoothable; Bär's contrast with van der Blij (8 versus 16); the $11/8$ conjecture context.
- `Ex - Quaternionic Vector Spaces Have Even Complex Dimension` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: I5.1.9 (the algebraic half); prereqs: [Def - Quaternions, Thm - Rokhlin Theorem]; spec: Prove Lemma 4 of the Rokhlin page in full (a complex vector space with an antilinear $J$, $J^2 = -\mathrm{id}$, has even complex dimension), and show the analogous statement fails for $J^2 = +\mathrm{id}$ (a real structure).
- `Ex - CP^2 is Not Spin` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: (drill of T5.1.14); prereqs: [Thm - Even Intersection Form iff Spin, Thm - Intersection Form of the Complex Projective Plane]; spec: Deduce from $Q_{\mathbb{CP}^2} = (1)$ (odd) that $\mathbb{CP}^2$ admits no spin structure; deduce that $k\mathbb{CP}^2 \# l\overline{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ is not spin for $k + l \ge 1$, while $n(S^2 \times S^2)$ and $K3$ are spin.
- `Ex - Rokhlin Bound on the Signature of Spin Four-Manifolds` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: (drill); prereqs: [Thm - Rokhlin Theorem, Thm - The K3 Surface is Simply Connected with Intersection Form Minus Two E8 Plus Three H]; spec: Check Rokhlin's theorem on $S^4$, $S^2 \times S^2$, $n(S^2 \times S^2)$, $K3$, $K3 \# K3$; show that no simply connected smooth spin four-manifold has intersection form $E_8 \oplus H^{\oplus l}$ for any $l$.
- `Ex - Index of the Dirac Operator on the Four-Sphere Vanishes` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: (consistency check of the import); prereqs: [Thm - Weitzenbock Formula for the Dirac Operator, Thm - Rokhlin Theorem]; spec: Using the Weitzenböck formula (chapter VIII anchor; the vault's `Thm - Lichnerowicz Formula` is flagged and not cited) and the positive scalar curvature of the round $S^4$, show $\ker D^\pm = 0$, so $\operatorname{ind} D^+ = 0$, consistent with $\operatorname{sign}(S^4) = 0$; conclude the same for any positive-scalar-curvature spin four-manifold and deduce $\operatorname{sign} = 0$ for those (given the import).
- `Exercise Index - §13.4 The Spin Condition and Rokhlin's Theorem` — lists: [Ex - Quaternionic Vector Spaces Have Even Complex Dimension, Ex - CP^2 is Not Spin, Ex - Rokhlin Bound on the Signature of Spin Four-Manifolds, Ex - Index of the Dirac Operator on the Four-Sphere Vanishes]

### §13.5 Whitehead, Freedman, and Serre
Standing hypothesis of this section: $X$ is a simply connected, compact, oriented TOPOLOGICAL four-manifold (Bär §5.2); finite generation of its homology is part of the Freedman import when the manifold comes from Freedman's theorem, and is proved for smooth manifolds by `Thm - Homology of a Compact Smooth Manifold is Finitely Generated`.
- `Def - Linking Numbers and the Linking Matrix of Framed Solid Tori` — type: definition (compound: linking number of two disjoint oriented knots in $S^3$ via the homological degree of the Gauss map, self-linking number of an embedded solid torus, linking matrix of a family of disjoint embedded solid tori); source items: D5.2.1, E5.2.1; prereqs: [Def - R-Orientation and the Fundamental Class, Thm - Homology of Spheres, Ex - Degree of a Self-Map of the Sphere via Hurewicz, Def - Embedded Submanifold]; spec: For disjoint oriented smooth embeddings $K_1, K_2 : S^1 \to \mathbb{R}^3 = S^3 \setminus \{\infty\}$ define $\operatorname{lk}(K_1, K_2) := \deg\big(G : S^1 \times S^1 \to S^2\big)$, $G(s,t) := (K_1(s) - K_2(t))/\lVert K_1(s) - K_2(t) \rVert$, the homological degree ($G_*[S^1 \times S^1] = \operatorname{lk} \cdot [S^2]$, with the fundamental class of the torus from `Def - R-Orientation and the Fundamental Class` and the product orientation); for an embedding $f : \bar D^2 \times S^1 \to S^3$ the self-linking number $\operatorname{lk}(f,f) := \operatorname{lk}(f(0, \cdot), f(1, \cdot))$ and, for two such with disjoint images, $\operatorname{lk}(f_1, f_2) := \operatorname{lk}(f_1(0,\cdot), f_2(0,\cdot))$; the linking matrix $(\operatorname{lk}(f_i, f_j))_{ij}$. Proved on the page: symmetry $\operatorname{lk}(K_1, K_2) = \operatorname{lk}(K_2, K_1)$ (the Gauss map for the swapped pair is the antipode of $G$ precomposed with the factor-swap $(s,t) \mapsto (t,s)$ of $S^1 \times S^1$; that swap reverses the product orientation and so has degree $-1$, the antipodal map of $S^2$ has degree $(-1)^3 = -1$, and the two sign factors cancel, giving equality of the degrees), invariance under isotopy through disjoint pairs (homotopy invariance of the degree), and independence from the choice of point $\infty \notin K_1 \cup K_2$ (stated for orientation; proved via isotopy). Examples verified (E5.2.1): the standard unknotted solid torus $f_k(z, e^{i\theta}) := $ (the tubular neighbourhood of the unit circle in the $xy$-plane, with the boundary curve $f_k(1, \cdot)$ winding $k$ times around the core as it goes once along it — explicit parametrisation with a radius $\rho < 1$), has self-linking number $k$ (computed: the Gauss map degree for the core circle and the $(1,k)$-curve on the torus equals $k$ — by an explicit computation of the degree via a regular value, `Thm - Regular Value Theorem on Manifolds`, or via homotoping the $(1,k)$-curve to $k$ small meridians each linking once); Bär's three pictured embeddings realise $0$, $1$, $4$. Non-example: two disjoint circles in different half-spaces have $\operatorname{lk} = 0$ (the Gauss map misses a direction). Calibration check: compute $\operatorname{lk}$ of the Hopf link as $\pm 1$; explain why $\operatorname{lk}(f,f)$ depends on the framing $f$ and not only on the core; explain why a linking matrix is symmetric.
- `Def - Fake Complex Projective Plane and the E8 Manifold` — type: definition (compound: the two Freedman manifolds with form $(1)$, the $E_8$ manifold $M_{E_8}$, the manifolds $\pm m M_{E_8} \# n(S^2 \times S^2)$); source items: D5.2.2, D5.2.3, E5.2.2, E5.2.3, T5.2.3 (Freedman's theorem, including its existence sketch (a)–(c)), I5.2.3; prereqs: [Thm - Intersection Form is Unimodular, Def - Unimodular Symmetric Bilinear Form, Parity, and Signature, Def - Linking Numbers and the Linking Matrix of Framed Solid Tori, Def - The E8 Form and the K3 Surface, Def - Connected Sum of Manifolds]; spec: This page carries the pre-approved import callout (Import 1) `Imported without proof: Freedman's classification of simply connected closed topological four-manifolds`, stated exactly: (Existence) for every unimodular symmetric bilinear form $Q$ on a free $\mathbb{Z}$-module of finite rank there is a simply connected closed oriented topological four-manifold $X$ with finitely generated homology and $Q_X \cong Q$ (with $Q_X$ the cup-product form of `Thm - Intersection Form Equals the Cup Product Form` (i)); (Uniqueness) if $Q$ is even, $X$ is unique up to orientation-preserving homeomorphism; if $Q$ is odd there are exactly two such $X$ up to homeomorphism, distinguished by the Kirby–Siebenmann invariant. The callout states exactly this much; the sharper statement that the Kirby–Siebenmann invariant vanishes for smoothable manifolds and thereby singles out which of the two odd-case manifolds is smoothable is NOT part of Freedman's import and is recorded separately as `[PROPOSED IMPORT: the Kirby–Siebenmann invariant vanishes for smoothable manifolds and distinguishes the two odd-case manifolds]` on `Thm - Homeomorphism Classification of Simply Connected Smooth Four-Manifolds`; (Connected sums and the open case) the connected sum of two such manifolds is again one with $Q_{X_1 \# X_2} = Q_{X_1} \oplus Q_{X_2}$ (so that Bär's notation $\pm m M_{E_8} \# n(S^2 \times S^2)$ is meaningful in the topological category), and (open-manifold form, Freedman 1982 Thm 1.2') a contractible open topological four-manifold that is simply connected at infinity (has an end homeomorphic to $S^3 \times (0, \infty)$) is homeomorphic to $\mathbb{R}^4$. Source of a complete proof: Freedman, *The topology of four-dimensional manifolds*, J. Differential Geom. 17 (1982), Thm 1.5 and Cor. 1.2'; Freedman–Quinn, *Topology of 4-Manifolds*, Ch. 8–10. Architecture paragraph: Bär's sketch (a)–(c) (linking numbers; handle attachment along $f_i$ with linking matrix $Q$ producing a compact $X_1$ with $\partial X_1$ a homology sphere and $Q_{X_1} = Q$; the deep step (c), Freedman's theorem that every homology $3$-sphere bounds a contractible topological $4$-manifold $X_2$, so that $X := X_1 \cup_\partial X_2$ is closed simply connected with $Q_X \cong Q$; uniqueness via the $h$-cobordism theorem for topological simply connected $4$-manifolds, proved with Casson handles and the disc embedding theorem — where the difficulty lies). Why imported: book-length. Then the definitions: $*\mathbb{CP}^2$ := the manifold of Freedman's odd case with $Q = (1)$ not homeomorphic to $\mathbb{CP}^2$ (Bär: obtained from the trefoil with self-linking number one, versus the unknot for $\mathbb{CP}^2$); $M_{E_8}$ := the unique (even case) manifold with $Q \cong E_8$; the list $\pm m M_{E_8} \# n(S^2 \times S^2)$ with $-M_{E_8} := \overline{M_{E_8}}$. Examples: $\mathbb{CP}^2$ realises $(1)$ (verified via `Thm - Intersection Form of the Complex Projective Plane`); $S^2 \times S^2$ realises $H$; by uniqueness in the even case, $S^2 \times S^2$ is the Freedman manifold of $H$ and $K3 \approx 2\overline{M_{E_8}} \# 3(S^2 \times S^2)$ (from `Thm - The K3 Surface is Simply Connected with Intersection Form Minus Two E8 Plus Three H` and even-case uniqueness — with the orientation bookkeeping made explicit: $Q_{K3} = -2E_8 \oplus 3H$ is realised by $2\overline{M_{E_8}} \# 3(S^2 \times S^2)$; `(source: Bär writes $K3 \approx 2M_{E_8} \# 3(S^2 \times S^2)$ with $M_{E_8}$ of form $E_8$; the orientations must be reversed on the $E_8$ summands, or $K3$ read with the opposite orientation)`). Calibration check: explain why the even-case uniqueness is what makes "$M_{E_8}$" a well-defined object; why the odd case has two candidates for $(1)$; what "finitely generated homology" is needed for.
- `Thm - Intersection Form is a Homotopy Invariant` — type: theorem; source items: T5.2.1, I5.2.1, T5.2.2 (Whitehead's theorem, carried as an import callout on this page), I5.2.2; prereqs: [Thm - Intersection Form Equals the Cup Product Form, Thm - Poincare Duality, Thm - Properties of the Cup Product, Def - R-Orientation and the Fundamental Class, Thm - Homotopy Invariance of Singular Homology (Gauge Theory)]; statement: Let $X_1$, $X_2$ be closed oriented topological four-manifolds with $H_1 = 0$ and finitely generated homology, and $f : X_1 \to X_2$ a homotopy equivalence. Then $f_*[X_1] = \pm [X_2]$ and $Q_{X_2}(f_* a, f_* b) = \pm Q_{X_1}(a, b)$ with the same sign; hence $Q_{X_1} \cong Q_{X_2}$ or $Q_{X_1} \cong -Q_{X_2}$, and $Q_{X_1} \cong Q_{X_2}$ if $f$ preserves orientation; in particular rank, $|\operatorname{sign}|$ and parity of $Q_X$ are homotopy invariants; proof: source: omitted; the page: $f_*$ is an isomorphism on $H_4 \cong \mathbb{Z}$, so $f_*[X_1] = \pm[X_2]$; $Q_{X_2}(f_* a, f_* b) = \langle D_2^{-1} f_* a \smile D_2^{-1} f_* b, [X_2] \rangle$ and by naturality of cup and cap products $f^*(D_2^{-1} f_* a) = \pm D_1^{-1} a$ (from $f_*(f^* \phi \frown [X_1]) = \phi \frown f_*[X_1] = \pm \phi \frown [X_2]$), so $Q_{X_2}(f_*a, f_*b) = \pm \langle D_1^{-1}a \smile D_1^{-1} b, [X_1] \rangle$ — each identity justified. The page also carries the pre-approved import callout (Import 8) `Imported without proof: Whitehead's theorem on the homotopy type of simply connected four-manifolds`: for simply connected closed oriented topological four-manifolds $X_1, X_2$ with finitely generated homology, $Q_{X_1} \cong Q_{X_2}$ implies $X_1 \simeq X_2$ (the converse being this page's theorem); source: Milnor, *On simply connected 4-manifolds* (1958), Thm 3; J. H. C. Whitehead, *On simply connected 4-dimensional polyhedra* (1949); architecture: a closed topological manifold has the homotopy type of a CW complex (Milnor 1959), a simply connected four-dimensional CW complex is homotopy equivalent to $\bigvee S^2 \cup e^4$ with attaching map determined by the form, and an isomorphism of forms is realised by a map of the wedges (the "topological-manifold input" that conventions.md allows to import; the CW-theoretic part is also imported here rather than proved, since the series does not build CW theory — the orchestrator should confirm this reading of item 8). spec: Why-true: "the cup product form is defined by the cohomology ring and the fundamental class, and both are transported by a homotopy equivalence"; Targets: Freedman's uniqueness statements; the fake $\mathbb{CP}^2$ has the same homotopy type as $\mathbb{CP}^2$ (proved from the import).
- `Thm - The E8 Manifold is Not Smoothable` — type: theorem; source items: T5.2.4, E5.2.3; prereqs: [Def - Fake Complex Projective Plane and the E8 Manifold, Thm - Rokhlin Theorem, Thm - The E8 Form is Even Positive Definite and Unimodular, Thm - Intersection Form Equals the Cup Product Form]; statement: $M_{E_8}$ (existing by the Freedman import) admits no smooth structure; more generally no simply connected closed topological four-manifold with even intersection form of signature not divisible by $16$ is smoothable; proof: source full: $\operatorname{sign}(M_{E_8}) = 8$; a smooth structure would make $M_{E_8}$ a simply connected compact oriented smooth four-manifold with even $Q$ (the smooth intersection form of `Def - Intersection Form of a Four-Manifold` agrees with the topological cup-product form by `Thm - Intersection Form Equals the Cup Product Form`), contradicting `Thm - Rokhlin Theorem`. spec: Targets: the exotic $\mathbb{R}^4$ argument's list; Bär's homeomorphism classification.
- `Thm - Serre Classification of Indefinite Unimodular Forms` — type: theorem; source items: T5.2.6, I5.2.5, (D5.2.x normal forms $A^{odd}_{k,l}$, $A^{even}_{\pm k, l}$); prereqs: [Def - Unimodular Symmetric Bilinear Form, Parity, and Signature, Thm - Direct Sums of Unimodular Forms, Thm - The E8 Form is Even Positive Definite and Unimodular, Thm - Even Unimodular Forms Have Signature Divisible by Eight, Thm - Existence of Characteristic Elements, Thm - Smith Normal Form]; statement: (i) (Odd case) Every odd indefinite unimodular symmetric bilinear form on a free $\mathbb{Z}$-module of finite rank is isomorphic to $A^{odd}_{k,l} := \langle 1 \rangle^{\oplus k} \oplus \langle -1 \rangle^{\oplus l}$ with $k, l \ge 1$, $k + l = \operatorname{rk}$, $k - l = \operatorname{sign}$. (ii) (Even case) Every even indefinite unimodular form is isomorphic to $A^{even}_{\pm k, l} := (\pm E_8)^{\oplus k} \oplus H^{\oplus l}$ with $l \ge 1$, $8k + 2l = \operatorname{rk}$, $\pm 8k = \operatorname{sign}$. (iii) Hence two indefinite unimodular forms are isomorphic iff they have the same rank, signature and parity; proof: source: cited (Serre); the page follows Milnor–Husemoller, *Symmetric Bilinear Forms*, Ch. II §4 (indefinite odd forms) and §5 (indefinite even forms), and carries the pre-approved import callout (Import 4) `Imported without proof: the Hasse–Minkowski theorem and Meyer's theorem`, stated as: "a non-degenerate quadratic form over $\mathbb{Q}$ represents zero over $\mathbb{Q}$ iff it represents zero over $\mathbb{R}$ and over every $\mathbb{Q}_p$ (Hasse–Minkowski); in particular an indefinite non-degenerate rational form in five or more variables represents zero (Meyer)" — source: Serre, *A Course in Arithmetic*, Ch. IV Thm 8 and Cor. 2; Cassels, *Rational Quadratic Forms*, Ch. 6; architecture: the local–global principle via Dirichlet's theorem on primes in arithmetic progressions and the product formula for Hilbert symbols; imported because the analytic number theory it rests on is beyond the series. Everything else on the page is proved. (Lemma 1, isotropy) an indefinite unimodular integral form $Q$ of rank $\ge 2$ has a primitive isotropic vector $x \ne 0$ with $Q(x,x) = 0$: for rank $\ge 5$ this is Meyer applied to $Q \otimes \mathbb{Q}$ (clear denominators and divide by the gcd of the coordinates to make the rational isotropic vector primitive integral); for ranks $2, 3, 4$ the page proves it in full following Milnor–Husemoller Ch. II §4, computing the real and $p$-adic local invariants of a unimodular indefinite form of rank $\le 4$ and invoking only the rational Hasse–Minkowski statement named in the callout. (Lemma 2, splitting off a plane) if $Q$ is unimodular and $x$ is primitive isotropic then $Q(x,\cdot)$ is a primitive functional, so unimodularity yields $y$ with $Q(x,y) = 1$; the sublattice $\langle x, y \rangle$ has Gram matrix $\begin{pmatrix} 0 & 1 \\ 1 & a \end{pmatrix}$ (determinant $-1$, hence unimodular), a unimodular sublattice splits off orthogonally ($Q \cong \langle x, y \rangle \oplus \langle x, y \rangle^\perp$, proved), and $\begin{pmatrix} 0 & 1 \\ 1 & a \end{pmatrix} \cong H$ if $a$ is even, $\cong \langle 1 \rangle \oplus \langle -1 \rangle$ if $a$ is odd (explicit bases). (Lemma 3, odd case) by induction on the rank, using Lemma 2 together with the identity $H \oplus \langle 1 \rangle \cong \langle 1 \rangle \oplus \langle 1 \rangle \oplus \langle -1 \rangle$ (explicit) to turn any split-off $H$ into diagonal blocks once an odd $\langle \pm 1 \rangle$ is present: every odd indefinite unimodular form is isomorphic to $\langle 1 \rangle^{\oplus k} \oplus \langle -1 \rangle^{\oplus l}$ with $k, l \ge 1$, $k + l = \operatorname{rk}$, $k - l = \operatorname{sign}$; the page reproduces Milnor–Husemoller Ch. II §4 Thm 4.3 step by step. (Lemma 4, even case) split hyperbolic planes off the even indefinite $Q$ by Lemma 2 until the orthogonal complement $Q_0$ is definite and even; such a $Q_0$ has rank in $8\mathbb{Z}$ equal to $\pm \operatorname{sign}$ by `Thm - Even Unimodular Forms Have Signature Divisible by Eight` (proved from the ODD case of Serre through `Thm - Van der Blij Lemma`, so there is no circularity), and existence of the normal forms $(\pm E_8)^{\oplus k} \oplus H^{\oplus l}$ with the stated invariants comes from `Thm - The E8 Form is Even Positive Definite and Unimodular` and `Thm - Direct Sums of Unimodular Forms`; uniqueness is the cancellation lemma "$Q_1 \oplus \langle 1 \rangle \cong Q_2 \oplus \langle 1 \rangle$ with $Q_1, Q_2$ even implies $Q_1 \cong Q_2$", proved via characteristic vectors (a characteristic vector of a form $R$ is a $v$ with $R(v,w) \equiv R(w,w) \pmod 2$ for all $w$): for even $Q_i$ the characteristic vectors of $Q_i \oplus \langle 1 \rangle$ are exactly the $(2z, m)$ with $m$ odd, of norm $Q_i(2z,2z) + m^2$, so the only characteristic vectors of norm $1$ are $(0, \pm 1)$; any isomorphism $Q_1 \oplus \langle 1 \rangle \cong Q_2 \oplus \langle 1 \rangle$ preserves the form and hence the set of norm-one characteristic vectors, carries $(0, 1)$ to $(0, \pm 1)$, and therefore restricts to an isomorphism of the orthogonal complements $\langle 1 \rangle^\perp$, i.e. $Q_1 \cong Q_2$; two even indefinite unimodular forms of equal rank and signature become isomorphic after adding a $\langle 1 \rangle$ (they turn odd indefinite, so Lemma 3 applies), and the cancellation strips the $\langle 1 \rangle$ off, giving (ii) and (iii). Reference: Milnor–Husemoller, *Symmetric Bilinear Forms*, Ch. II §4 (Thm 4.3, indefinite odd forms) and §5 (indefinite even forms and the cancellation lemma); Serre, *A Course in Arithmetic*, Ch. V §2. spec: Why-true: "an indefinite unimodular form always contains a hyperbolic plane or a $\langle 1 \rangle \oplus \langle -1 \rangle$, and peeling these off reduces everything to rank, signature and parity; the only non-elementary input is that indefinite forms represent zero"; Sources: any indefinite intersection form; Targets: the K3 form; Bär's homeomorphism list; van der Blij (this manifest proves van der Blij via the odd case).
- `Def - Bernoulli Numbers and the Minkowski-Siegel Mass` — type: definition (compound: Bernoulli numbers in Bär's convention, the set $\mathcal{Q}_{8k}$ of isomorphism classes of even positive definite unimodular forms of rank $8k$, the automorphism group of a form, the mass); source items: D5.2.5, D5.2.6, R5.2.2, I5.2.7 (the mass formula part); prereqs: [Def - Unimodular Symmetric Bilinear Form, Parity, and Signature, Thm - Even Unimodular Forms Have Signature Divisible by Eight]; spec: Bernoulli numbers via $\frac{x}{e^x - 1} = 1 - \frac{x}{2} - \sum_{j \ge 1} \frac{(-1)^j B_j}{(2j)!} x^{2j}$, so $B_1 = \tfrac16$, $B_2 = \tfrac1{30}$, $B_3 = \tfrac1{42}$, $B_4 = \tfrac1{30}$ (the page computes the first eight from the recursion and warns that the modern convention $B_{2j}$ has alternating signs — Convention callout); $\mathcal{Q}_{8k}$ (rank of an even definite unimodular form is a multiple of $8$ by the theorem linked); $\operatorname{Aut}(Q)$ is finite for definite $Q$ (proved: finitely many vectors of bounded norm); this page carries the pre-approved import callout (Import 7) `Imported without proof: the Minkowski–Siegel mass formula`: $\sum_{Q \in \mathcal{Q}_{8k}} \frac{1}{\#\operatorname{Aut}(Q)} = 2^{1 - 8k} \frac{B_{2k}}{(4k)!} \prod_{j=1}^{4k-1} B_j =: a_{8k}$ in Bär's convention `(source typo 29: "Minkowski-Segal" for Minkowski–Siegel)`; source: Conway–Sloane, *Sphere Packings, Lattices and Groups*, Ch. 16 §2, and Serre, *A Course in Arithmetic*, Ch. VII (the Siegel formula in the case $k = 1$); architecture: Siegel's theta-series / Eisenstein-series identity; why imported: analytic number theory beyond the series. Examples: $a_8 = 1/696729600 \approx 1.43 \cdot 10^{-9}$, which equals $1/\#W(E_8)$ (verified by computation on `Ex - Computing the Mass Bound from Bernoulli Numbers`). Calibration check: recompute $B_1, B_2$; explain why $\#\operatorname{Aut} \ge 2$ always ($\pm \mathrm{id}$); explain why the mass is a lower bound for the number of classes.
- `Thm - Lower Bound on the Number of Even Definite Unimodular Forms` — type: theorem; source items: T5.2.10, E5.2.4, R5.2.1, I5.2.7 (the table); prereqs: [Def - Bernoulli Numbers and the Minkowski-Siegel Mass]; statement: $\#\mathcal{Q}_{8k} \ge a_{8k}$ for every $k \ge 1$ (with $a_{8k}$ as above); numerically $a_{32} \approx 4.03 \cdot 10^7$ and $a_{40} \approx 4.39 \cdot 10^{51}$, so there are more than forty million even positive definite unimodular forms of rank $32$; proof: source full: each summand of the mass is $\le 1$ (in fact $\le \tfrac12$), so $\#\mathcal{Q}_{8k} \ge a_{8k}$, with the mass formula as the explicitly named imported hypothesis; the numerical values are computed on the page from the Bernoulli numbers. The known exact counts ($1$ for $k = 1$, namely $E_8$; $2$ for $k = 2$; $24$ for $k = 3$, Niemeier; unknown for $k \ge 4$) are reported in a remark clearly marked as "reported values, not established in this series" `(source typo 29: "Niemeyer" for Niemeier)`; the uniqueness of $E_8$ in rank $8$ and Witt's count $2$ in rank $16$ are `[PROPOSED IMPORT: Witt's and Niemeier's classifications of even unimodular lattices in ranks 16 and 24]` — the theorem statement does not use them. R5.2.1 (motivation: definite forms are unclassifiable in practice, which is why Donaldson's theorem is striking) goes in Motivation. spec: Targets: the topic page's framing of Donaldson's theorem.
- `Ex - Linking Numbers of Standard Framed Solid Tori` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: E5.2.1; prereqs: [Def - Linking Numbers and the Linking Matrix of Framed Solid Tori]; spec: For the unknotted solid torus with framing $k$ (explicit parametrisation), compute the self-linking number $k$ by finding a regular value of the Gauss map and counting preimages with signs; produce embeddings with self-linking $0$, $1$, $4$ (Bär's figure) and a pair with mutual linking number $1$.
- `Ex - Homotopy Equivalent Four-Manifolds Have Equal Signature and Parity` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: (drill of T5.2.1); prereqs: [Thm - Intersection Form is a Homotopy Invariant]; spec: Deduce that $|\operatorname{sign}|$, $b_2$ and parity are homotopy invariants of simply connected closed oriented four-manifolds; show $\mathbb{CP}^2 \# \overline{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ and $S^2 \times S^2$ are not homotopy equivalent although they have the same $b_2$ and signature.
- `Ex - Serre Normal Forms for Small Rank` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: (drill of T5.2.6); prereqs: [Thm - Serre Classification of Indefinite Unimodular Forms]; spec: List all indefinite unimodular forms of rank $\le 4$ up to isomorphism, and all unimodular forms (definite included) of rank $\le 2$ (for rank $\le 2$ classify definite ones directly: $\langle 1 \rangle$, $\langle -1 \rangle$, $\langle 1 \rangle^2$, $\langle -1 \rangle^2$ — prove that a positive definite unimodular form of rank $\le 7$ is diagonal, or at least of rank $\le 2$ by reduction theory of binary forms).
- `Ex - Computing the Mass Bound from Bernoulli Numbers` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: E5.2.4; prereqs: [Def - Bernoulli Numbers and the Minkowski-Siegel Mass, Thm - Lower Bound on the Number of Even Definite Unimodular Forms]; spec: Compute $B_1, \dots, B_8$ in Bär's convention, then $a_8$, $a_{16}$, $a_{24}$, $a_{32}$ exactly as rational numbers and numerically; verify $a_8 = 1/696729600 = 1/\#W(E_8)$ against the order of the Weyl group of $E_8$ ($696729600 = 2^{14} 3^5 5^2 7$, stated as a check) and Bär's table values $a_8 \approx 1.43 \cdot 10^{-9}$, $a_{16} \approx 2.48 \cdot 10^{-18}$, $a_{24} \approx 7.93 \cdot 10^{-15}$, $a_{32} \approx 4.03 \cdot 10^7$.

### §13.6 Characteristic Elements and Van der Blij's Lemma
- `Def - Characteristic Element of a Unimodular Form` — type: definition (compound: the mod-two reduction $\bar H = H/2H$ with $\bar Q$, characteristic element, characteristic surface); source items: D5.2.4, (the $\bar H$, $\bar Q$ setup); prereqs: [Def - Unimodular Symmetric Bilinear Form, Parity, and Signature, Def - Quotient Group, Def - Intersection Form of a Four-Manifold]; spec: $w \in H$ is characteristic for $Q$ iff $Q(x,x) \equiv Q(w,x) \pmod 2$ for all $x \in H$; $\bar H := H/2H$ `(source: Bär writes $H/2\mathbb{Z}$)`, a vector space over $\mathbb{Z}/2$ of dimension $\operatorname{rk} H$, with the induced symmetric bilinear form $\bar Q$ (well defined, verified); the map $\bar f : \bar H \to \mathbb{Z}/2$, $\bar f(\xi) := \bar Q(\xi, \xi)$, is $\mathbb{Z}/2$-linear (verified: $\bar Q(\xi + \eta, \xi + \eta) = \bar f(\xi) + \bar f(\eta) + 2\bar Q(\xi,\eta)$); for $H = H_2(X;\mathbb{Z})$ an embedded oriented surface representing a characteristic element is a characteristic surface. Corollaries proved: $w$ characteristic iff $\bar w$ represents $\bar f$ under $\bar Q$; the set of characteristic elements is a coset of $2H$ (forward link to the existence theorem for non-emptiness); $0$ is characteristic iff $Q$ is even; for a diagonal form $\operatorname{diag}(\pm 1)$ the characteristic elements are exactly the vectors with all coordinates odd (verified). Examples: $H$ (hyperbolic): $w = 0$ and all of $2\mathbb{Z}^2$; $\langle 1 \rangle$: odd integers; non-example: $(1, 0)$ is not characteristic for $\langle 1 \rangle \oplus \langle 1 \rangle$. Calibration check: find a characteristic element of $E_8 \oplus \langle 1 \rangle$; explain why "characteristic" only depends on $w \bmod 2H$; identify the characteristic surfaces of $\mathbb{CP}^2$ (odd-degree curves).
- `Thm - Existence of Characteristic Elements` — type: theorem; source items: T5.2.7 (Lemma 5.2.8); prereqs: [Def - Characteristic Element of a Unimodular Form, Def - Unimodular Symmetric Bilinear Form, Parity, and Signature]; statement: If $Q$ is unimodular on a free $H$ of finite rank, then there exists a characteristic element $w \in H$, and the characteristic elements form exactly one coset $w + 2H$; proof: source full; the page: (Lemma 1) unimodularity gives that every $\mathbb{Z}$-linear $f : H \to \mathbb{Z}$ equals $Q(y, \cdot)$ for a unique $y$ (invert the Gram matrix over $\mathbb{Z}$, `Def - Unimodular Symmetric Bilinear Form, Parity, and Signature`); (Lemma 2) the same holds for $\bar Q$ on $\bar H$ over $\mathbb{Z}/2$ (reduce the inverse matrix mod $2$); (Lemma 3) $\bar f(\xi) = \bar Q(\xi,\xi)$ is linear, so $\bar f = \bar Q(\bar y, \cdot)$ for a unique $\bar y$; any lift $w$ of $\bar y$ is characteristic; uniqueness of $\bar y$ gives the coset statement. spec: Targets: van der Blij; the $8$-divisibility; Rokhlin's comparison.
- `Thm - Van der Blij Lemma` — type: theorem; source items: T5.2.8, I5.2.6; prereqs: [Thm - Existence of Characteristic Elements, Thm - Serre Classification of Indefinite Unimodular Forms, Def - Characteristic Element of a Unimodular Form, Thm - Direct Sums of Unimodular Forms]; statement: For $Q$ unimodular on free $H$ of finite rank and $w$ any characteristic element, $\operatorname{sign}(Q) \equiv Q(w,w) \pmod 8$; proof: source cited; the page proves it by the stabilisation route (which depends on the odd case of Serre's classification, hence on Import 4, as the page states): (Lemma 1) $Q(w,w) \bmod 8$ does not depend on the characteristic element: for $w' = w + 2y$, $Q(w',w') - Q(w,w) = 4Q(w,y) + 4Q(y,y) = 4(Q(w,y) + Q(y,y)) \equiv 0 \pmod 8$ since $Q(w,y) \equiv Q(y,y) \pmod 2$; (Lemma 2) characteristic elements and $Q(w,w) \bmod 8$ are preserved by isomorphisms and additive under direct sums ($w_1 \oplus w_2$ is characteristic for $Q_1 \oplus Q_2$ iff each $w_i$ is); (Lemma 3) for $Q' := Q \oplus \langle 1 \rangle \oplus \langle -1 \rangle$, which is odd, indefinite, unimodular of rank $r + 2$ and signature $\operatorname{sign} Q$, `Thm - Serre Classification of Indefinite Unimodular Forms` (i) gives $Q' \cong \langle 1 \rangle^{k} \oplus \langle -1 \rangle^{l}$ with $k - l = \operatorname{sign} Q$; (Lemma 4) for the diagonal form the characteristic elements are the all-odd vectors $(a_1, \dots, a_k, b_1, \dots, b_l)$ and $\sum a_i^2 - \sum b_j^2 \equiv k - l \pmod 8$ since odd squares are $\equiv 1 \pmod 8$; (Conclusion) $w' := (w, 1, 1)$ is characteristic for $Q'$ with $Q'(w',w') = Q(w,w) + 1 - 1 = Q(w,w)$, so by Lemmas 1–4, $Q(w,w) \equiv k - l = \operatorname{sign} Q \pmod 8$. A remark records the import-free alternative via Gauss sums (Milnor–Husemoller Appendix 4, Milgram's formula) and states that it is NOT followed here. Reference: Milnor–Husemoller, *Symmetric Bilinear Forms*, Ch. II Thm 5.2 (statement) and Appendix 4 (the Gauss-sum proof); Scorpan, *The Wild World of 4-Manifolds*, Ch. 3 (the stabilisation argument in expository form). The stabilisation proof as written here is self-contained given the odd case of Serre's classification. spec: Why-true: "after adding a hyperbolic pair $\langle 1 \rangle \oplus \langle -1 \rangle$ every unimodular form becomes diagonal, where characteristic vectors are the all-odd ones and odd squares are one modulo eight"; Targets: even forms have signature $\equiv 0 \pmod 8$; the comparison with Rokhlin; Serre's even case (which the manifest routes through the $8$-divisibility theorem — the writer must check the order: `Thm - Even Unimodular Forms Have Signature Divisible by Eight` as used inside `Thm - Serre Classification of Indefinite Unimodular Forms` (ii) must be proved WITHOUT the even case of Serre; it is, since van der Blij uses only the odd case (i)).
- `Thm - Even Unimodular Forms Have Signature Divisible by Eight` — type: theorem; source items: T5.2.9, Rem. 5.2.11; prereqs: [Thm - Van der Blij Lemma, Def - Characteristic Element of a Unimodular Form]; statement: If $Q$ is even and unimodular then $\operatorname{sign}(Q) \in 8\mathbb{Z}$; if moreover $Q$ is positive definite then $\operatorname{rk} Q = \operatorname{sign} Q \in 8\mathbb{Z}$; this is weaker than Rokhlin's theorem ($8$ rather than $16$) but applies to topological manifolds: every simply connected closed topological four-manifold with even intersection form has signature in $8\mathbb{Z}$; proof: source full: $w = 0$ is characteristic for even $Q$, so $\operatorname{sign} Q \equiv 0 \pmod 8$ by van der Blij; the manifold statement via `Thm - Intersection Form is Unimodular`. spec: Targets: Serre's even case; $M_{E_8}$ has the minimal possible signature; the mass formula's $\mathcal{Q}_{8k}$.
- `Ex - Characteristic Elements of the Diagonal Forms` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: (drill); prereqs: [Def - Characteristic Element of a Unimodular Form]; spec: Determine all characteristic elements of $\langle 1 \rangle^k \oplus \langle -1 \rangle^l$, of $H$, and of $E_8 \oplus \langle -1 \rangle$; verify van der Blij in each case by direct computation.
- `Ex - Characteristic Elements are Unique Modulo Two` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: (drill of T5.2.7); prereqs: [Thm - Existence of Characteristic Elements]; spec: Prove directly that the difference of two characteristic elements lies in $2H$ when $Q$ is unimodular, and give a non-unimodular example ($\langle 2 \rangle$) where characteristic elements do not exist.
- `Ex - Van der Blij for the Hyperbolic Form and for E8` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: (drill of T5.2.8); prereqs: [Thm - Van der Blij Lemma, Thm - The E8 Form is Even Positive Definite and Unimodular]; spec: Check $\operatorname{sign} \equiv Q(w,w) \pmod 8$ for $H$ with $w = 0$ and $w = (2, 0)$, for $E_8$ with $w = 0$, and for $E_8 \oplus \langle 1 \rangle$ with $w = (0, 1)$; then verify $Q(w,w) \equiv 9 \equiv \operatorname{sign} = 9 \pmod 8$.
- `Ex - The Signature of an Even Definite Form is Its Rank` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: (drill of T5.2.9 second clause); prereqs: [Thm - Even Unimodular Forms Have Signature Divisible by Eight, Thm - Direct Sums of Unimodular Forms]; spec: Show that an even positive definite unimodular form has rank in $8\mathbb{Z}$, that its minimal rank is $8$, and, using Rokhlin, that a smooth simply connected closed four-manifold with even positive definite form has $b_2 \in 16\mathbb{Z}$ (and, after Donaldson, $b_2 = 0$).
- `Exercise Index - §13.6 Characteristic Elements and Van der Blij's Lemma` — lists: [Ex - Characteristic Elements of the Diagonal Forms, Ex - Characteristic Elements are Unique Modulo Two, Ex - Van der Blij for the Hyperbolic Form and for E8, Ex - The Signature of an Even Definite Form is Its Rank]

### §13.7 Donaldson's Theorem
Standing hypothesis of §13.7 (Bär §5.3): $X$ is a compact, oriented, simply connected, smooth four-manifold with **positive definite** intersection form $Q_X$; $P \to X$ is the principal $SU(2)$-bundle with $\int_X c_2(P) = -1$ (in the convention $c_2(P) = [\tfrac{1}{8\pi^2}\operatorname{tr}(F\wedge F)]$ and instanton number $k(P) := \int_X c_2(P)$ of `conventions.md`), unique up to isomorphism (`[NEEDED FROM I–XI: the $SU(2)$-bundle over a simply connected closed four-manifold is determined up to isomorphism by $k = \int c_2 \in \mathbb{Z}$ — the completeness clause of `Thm - Classification of Principal SU(2)-Bundles over Four-Manifolds` (chapter III), scope-remarked there for lack of $\pi_3(S^3)$, now provable since chapter XII proves $\pi_3(S^3) \cong \mathbb{Z}$ on `Thm - Homotopy Groups of Spheres Below the Dimension`; the orchestrator wires chapter III's completeness clause to that page]`).
- `Def - The Instanton Moduli Space and its Compactification` — type: definition (compound: the space $\mathcal{A}(P)$ of self-dual (instanton) $SU(2)$-connections, the gauge quotient $\mathcal{M} = \mathcal{A}(P)/\mathcal{G}(P)$, the Uhlenbeck compactification $\mathcal{M}_c = \mathcal{M} \sqcup X$, and the truncation $\mathcal{M}'$); source items: D5.3.1, D5.3.2, R5.3.1, R5.3.2; prereqs: [Def - Instanton, Def - Self-Dual and Anti-Self-Dual Connections, Def - Gauge Transformation, Def - Curvature of a Principal Connection, Def - Yang-Mills Lagrangian and Action Functional, Def - The Hopf Bundle]; spec: with the standing hypothesis, $\mathcal{A}(P) := \{\omega \in \mathcal{C}(P) : \star \bar\Omega = \bar\Omega\}$ (self-dual connections on the adjoint bundle, `Def - Self-Dual and Anti-Self-Dual Connections`, chapter VII), $\mathcal{M} := \mathcal{A}(P)/\mathcal{G}(P)$ (`Def - Gauge Transformation`, chapter V); the analytic facts that make $\mathcal{M}$ a smooth $5$-manifold away from finitely many singular points, the Uhlenbeck topology on $\mathcal{M}_c := \mathcal{M} \sqcup X$ (a divergent sequence $[\omega_k]$ converges to its curvature-concentration point $x \in X$), and the truncation $\mathcal{M}'$ (cone neighbourhoods of the singular points cut off) are recorded here as the *statements* of the imported analytic package, with the full callout carried by `Thm - Donaldson Diagonalisation Theorem`; this page only fixes notation and states the energy normalisation (R5.3.2): for self-dual $\omega$, $\int_X |\bar\Omega|^2 \, \mathrm{dvol} = -8\pi^2 \int_X c_2(P) = 8\pi^2$ (from `Thm - Energy Identity and the Topological Bound for Yang-Mills`, chapter VII, and $k(P) = -1$; every sign per `conventions.md`). Examples: the trivial connection is not in $\mathcal{A}(P)$ since $k(P) \ne 0$; non-example: an anti-self-dual connection would need $k(P) = +1$. Calibration check: state why $k(P) = -1$ forces $\int|\bar\Omega|^2 = 8\pi^2 > 0$; explain what "concentration at $x$" means for the curvature density.
- `Def - Reducible Connections and the Singular-Point Count` — type: definition (compound: reducible self-dual connection via a splitting $P \cong L \oplus L^*$, the counting function $n(Q)$); source items: D5.3.4, D5.3.5; prereqs: [Def - The Instanton Moduli Space and its Compactification, Def - Reduction and Extension of the Structure Group, Def - Unimodular Symmetric Bilinear Form, Parity, and Signature, Def - Intersection Form of a Four-Manifold]; spec: a self-dual connection on $P$ is *reducible* if it is induced from a self-dual connection on a $U(1)$-bundle $L$ under a reduction $P \cong L \oplus L^*$ (`Def - Reduction and Extension of the Structure Group`, chapter III); the counting function $n(Q) := \tfrac12 \#\{\alpha \in H^2(X;\mathbb{Z}) : Q(\alpha, \alpha) = 1\}$ (finite because $Q$ is positive definite, so $\{Q(\alpha,\alpha)=1\}$ is the intersection of a lattice with an ellipsoid); the factor $\tfrac12$ pairs $\alpha$ with $-\alpha$. Examples: for $Q = \operatorname{diag}(1,\dots,1)$ of rank $r$, the classes of self-intersection $1$ are exactly $\pm e_i$, so $n(Q) = r$; for $E_8$, $n(E_8) = 0$ (no vector of norm $1$ in an even lattice); non-example: $H$ is indefinite, so $n$ is not defined by this finite count. Calibration check: compute $n(\operatorname{diag}(1,1,1))$ and $n(E_8 \oplus \langle 1 \rangle)$; explain why positive definiteness makes the set finite.
- `Thm - Cobordism Invariance of the Signature` — type: theorem; source items: I5.3.4, R5.3.3; prereqs: [Thm - Poincare-Lefschetz Duality for Manifolds with Boundary, Thm - Relative Fundamental Class of a Compact Manifold with Boundary, Def - Unimodular Symmetric Bilinear Form, Parity, and Signature, Thm - Cup Product Pairing is Unimodular on a Closed Oriented Manifold]; statement: If $W$ is a compact oriented smooth $(4k+1)$-manifold with boundary, then $\operatorname{sign}(\partial W) = 0$; more precisely, for a compact oriented smooth $5$-manifold $W$ with $\partial W = M$ a closed oriented $4$-manifold, the intersection form $Q_M$ has signature zero; proof: source: imported ("cobordism invariance of the signature"); the page proves it in full: on the closed oriented $4$-manifold $M = \partial W$ the image $L := \operatorname{im}(H^2(W;\mathbb{Q}) \to H^2(M;\mathbb{Q}))$ (restriction) is a Lagrangian subspace for the cup-product form $Q_M \otimes \mathbb{Q}$ — it is isotropic because a cup product of two classes extending over $W$ evaluates on $[M] = \partial[W,\partial W]$ to zero by `Thm - Relative Fundamental Class of a Compact Manifold with Boundary` and Stokes/naturality, and it is half-dimensional by the long exact sequence of $(W, M)$ and Poincaré–Lefschetz duality (`Thm - Poincare-Lefschetz Duality for Manifolds with Boundary`): $\dim L = \tfrac12 \dim H^2(M;\mathbb{Q})$ from the rank computation $\operatorname{rk}(H^2(W) \to H^2(M)) = \tfrac12 b_2(M)$; a nondegenerate symmetric form with a Lagrangian subspace has signature $0$ (Sylvester). Reference: the self-contained Lagrangian-subspace argument here (as in Kirby, *The Topology of 4-Manifolds*, Ch. II, and Donaldson–Kronheimer §1.1); Hatcher §3.3 for the duality inputs. spec: Why-true: "the classes that die on the boundary are exactly half of them and they are mutually orthogonal, so the positive and negative parts balance"; Sources: the boundary of any compact oriented $5$-manifold; Targets: `Thm - Donaldson Diagonalisation Theorem` step (b) ($\operatorname{sign}(X) = n_+ - n_-$).
- `Thm - Reducible Instantons Correspond to Classes of Self-Intersection One` — type: theorem; source items: I5.3.5, I5.3.6, R5.3.5, D5.3.4 (used); prereqs: [Def - Reducible Connections and the Singular-Point Count, Thm - Hermitian Line Bundles are Classified by the Integral Second Cohomology, Thm - Classification of Principal SU(2)-Bundles over Four-Manifolds, Def - Chern Classes, Thm - Axioms and Properties of Chern Classes, Thm - Intersection Form via de Rham Cohomology, Def - Intersection Form of a Four-Manifold]; statement: For $X$ and $P$ as in the standing hypothesis, the reducible self-dual connections on $P$ (modulo gauge) are in bijection with the pairs $\{\alpha, -\alpha\}$, $\alpha \in H^2(X;\mathbb{Z})$ with $Q_X(\alpha,\alpha) = 1$; hence their number is $n(Q_X)$; proof: source: asserted (step (d)); the page proves the topological content in full: (Lemma 1) a reduction $P \cong L \oplus L^*$ with $c_1(L) = \alpha$ has $c(L \oplus L^*) = c(L)c(L^*) = (1+\alpha)(1-\alpha) = 1 - \alpha^2$, and $c_2(L \oplus L^*) = -\alpha^2$, so the underlying $SU(2)$-bundle has $\int_X c_2 = -\langle \alpha \smile \alpha, [X]\rangle = -Q_X(\alpha,\alpha)$ (`Thm - Second Chern Number of an SU(2)-Bundle over a Closed Four-Manifold is the Clutching Degree`, `Thm - Axioms and Properties of Chern Classes`, and `Thm - Intersection Form via de Rham Cohomology` identifying $\langle\alpha\smile\alpha,[X]\rangle = Q_X(\alpha,\alpha)$); this equals $-1 = \int_X c_2(P)$ iff $Q_X(\alpha,\alpha) = 1$; (Lemma 2) each such $\alpha$ is realised by a unique Hermitian line bundle $L$ (`Thm - Hermitian Line Bundles are Classified by the Integral Second Cohomology`), and $L$, $L^*$ give the same reduction, so $\{\alpha,-\alpha\} \leftrightarrow$ reduction; (Lemma 3) $L$ carries a self-dual connection because $b_2^-(X) = 0$ (positive definite $Q_X$), so the harmonic representative of $\tfrac{i}{2\pi}\alpha_{\mathbb{R}}$ is self-dual (`Thm - Intersection Form via de Rham Cohomology` (iv)); the identification of these with the singular points of $\mathcal{M}$ is the imported analytic statement (Import 2, on the Donaldson page). Reference: Donaldson–Kronheimer §4.1; Freed–Uhlenbeck Ch. 3. spec: Why-true: "a reducible connection is a line bundle plus its inverse, its instanton number is minus the self-intersection of $c_1$, and matching $\int c_2 = -1$ forces self-intersection one"; Targets: the counting step of `Thm - Donaldson Diagonalisation Theorem`.
- `Thm - Donaldson Diagonalisation Theorem` — type: theorem (anchor); source items: T5.3.1, R5.3.1, R5.3.6, I5.3.1, I5.3.2, I5.3.3, `(source typo 30: p. 147 prints $n_+ - n_- = n$; it must read $n_+ + n_- = n$)`; prereqs: [Def - The Instanton Moduli Space and its Compactification, Def - Reducible Connections and the Singular-Point Count, Thm - Cobordism Invariance of the Signature, Thm - Reducible Instantons Correspond to Classes of Self-Intersection One, Thm - Intersection Form is Unimodular, Def - Unimodular Symmetric Bilinear Form, Parity, and Signature, Thm - Intersection Form of the Complex Projective Plane]; statement: A simply connected compact oriented smooth four-manifold $X$ with positive definite intersection form $Q_X$ has $Q_X \cong \operatorname{diag}(1, \dots, 1)$ over $\mathbb{Z}$; proof: this page carries the pre-approved import callout (Import 2) `Imported without proof: the analytic package of Donaldson's theorem`, stated exactly as the four facts Bär's steps (a$'$)–(c$'$) use: for $X$ and $P$ ($\int c_2(P) = -1$) as above, (I) the moduli space $\mathcal{M}$ of self-dual connections modulo gauge is, for a generic metric, a smooth oriented $5$-dimensional manifold with finitely many singular points $p_1, \dots, p_n$ (Freed–Uhlenbeck generic-metric transversality; Taubes' theorem that $\mathcal{M} \ne \emptyset$); (II) each $p_i$ has a neighbourhood homeomorphic to the cone on $\mathbb{CP}^2$ (the structure near a reducible connection); (III) every divergent sequence $[\omega_k]$ has a subsequence whose curvature concentrates at a point $x \in X$, so that $\mathcal{M}_c = \mathcal{M} \sqcup X$ is compact and $\mathcal{M}'$ (cones cut off) is a compact $5$-manifold with $\partial \mathcal{M}' = X \sqcup \bigsqcup^{n_+}\mathbb{CP}^2 \sqcup \bigsqcup^{n_-}\overline{\mathbb{CP}^2}$, $n_+ + n_- = n$ (Uhlenbeck compactness and removable singularities). Source of a complete proof: Donaldson–Kronheimer, *The Geometry of Four-Manifolds*, Ch. 4, 7, 8; Freed–Uhlenbeck, *Instantons and Four-Manifolds*. Architecture of the proof: elliptic gauge theory (the ASD deformation complex is Fredholm), the Sard–Smale/Freed–Uhlenbeck genericity argument (chapter X tools), Taubes' gluing for existence, and Uhlenbeck's $L^2$-compactness with the removable-singularity theorem; the difficulty is the analysis of bubbling and the local structure at reducibles. Why imported: book-length. Then the **deduction is proved in full** (conventions.md item 2): (Step b) by `Thm - Cobordism Invariance of the Signature` applied to $\mathcal{M}'$, $0 = \operatorname{sign}(\partial\mathcal{M}') = \operatorname{sign}(X) + n_+ \cdot \operatorname{sign}(\mathbb{CP}^2) + n_- \cdot \operatorname{sign}(\overline{\mathbb{CP}^2}) = \operatorname{sign}(X) + n_+ - n_-$ (using $\operatorname{sign}(\mathbb{CP}^2) = +1$, `Thm - Intersection Form of the Complex Projective Plane`), so $\operatorname{sign}(X) = n_+ - n_-$; (Step c/d) by `Thm - Reducible Instantons Correspond to Classes of Self-Intersection One`, $n = n(Q_X) = \tfrac12\#\{\alpha : Q_X(\alpha,\alpha)=1\}$; (Step, counting) $n(Q) \le \operatorname{rk}(Q)$ with equality iff $Q \cong \operatorname{diag}(1,\dots,1)$, proved by induction on the rank: a class $\alpha$ with $Q(\alpha,\alpha) = 1$ splits $Q = \langle\alpha\rangle \oplus \alpha^\perp = \langle 1\rangle \oplus Q|_{\alpha^\perp}$ (unimodularity, `Thm - Intersection Form is Unimodular`), and, $Q$ being positive definite, every class of self-intersection $1$ lies in $\{\pm\alpha\} \cup \alpha^\perp$ (Cauchy–Schwarz: $\beta = c\alpha + \beta'$ with $\beta' \perp \alpha$ and $Q(\beta,\beta) = c^2 + Q(\beta',\beta') = 1$ forces $c \in \{0,\pm 1\}$), so $n(Q) = n(Q|_{\alpha^\perp}) + 1$; (Conclusion) $\operatorname{rk} Q_X = \operatorname{sign} Q_X = n_+ - n_- \le n_+ + n_- = n = n(Q_X) \le \operatorname{rk} Q_X$ forces $n_- = 0$ and $n(Q_X) = \operatorname{rk} Q_X$, hence $Q_X \cong \operatorname{diag}(1,\dots,1)$. spec: the page's `# Imported Results` entry (Import 2) is listed on the topic page and the Series Map. Why-true: "instantons build a five-dimensional cobordism from $X$ to a union of $\mathbb{CP}^2$'s, one per class of self-intersection one; the signature is conserved and can equal the rank only for the diagonal form"; Targets: `Thm - E8 Plus E8 is Not Smoothable`, the exotic $\mathbb{R}^4$, the homeomorphism classification.
- `Thm - E8 Plus E8 is Not Smoothable` — type: theorem; source items: T5.3.2; prereqs: [Thm - Donaldson Diagonalisation Theorem, Thm - The E8 Form is Even Positive Definite and Unimodular, Thm - Direct Sums of Unimodular Forms, Def - Fake Complex Projective Plane and the E8 Manifold, Thm - Intersection Form Equals the Cup Product Form]; statement: The simply connected closed topological four-manifold with intersection form $E_8 \oplus E_8$ (existing by Freedman) admits no smooth structure; proof: source full; $E_8 \oplus E_8$ is positive definite (`Thm - The E8 Form is Even Positive Definite and Unimodular`, `Thm - Direct Sums of Unimodular Forms`) but not diagonal (it is even, so no vector has self-intersection $1$, whereas $\operatorname{diag}(1,\dots,1)$ has $2\cdot 16$ of them); a smooth structure would make it a simply connected compact oriented smooth four-manifold with positive definite form, forcing $Q \cong \operatorname{diag}(1,\dots,1)$ by `Thm - Donaldson Diagonalisation Theorem` — a contradiction (the smooth intersection form equals the topological cup-product form, `Thm - Intersection Form Equals the Cup Product Form`). spec: Targets: the exotic $\mathbb{R}^4$ argument (a smoothly embedded $S^3$ in the wrong place would build such a manifold).
- `Ex - The Instanton Energy of a Self-Dual Connection` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: R5.3.2; prereqs: [Def - The Instanton Moduli Space and its Compactification, Thm - Energy Identity and the Topological Bound for Yang-Mills, Thm - Classification of Principal SU(2)-Bundles over Four-Manifolds]; spec: From the decomposition $\bar\Omega = \bar\Omega^+ + \bar\Omega^-$ and $-8\pi^2 \int_X c_2(P) = \int_X(|\bar\Omega^+|^2 - |\bar\Omega^-|^2)\,\mathrm{dvol}$ (chapter VII), show that a self-dual connection minimises $\int|\bar\Omega|^2$ in its bundle and that on $P$ with $\int c_2 = -1$ the minimum is $8\pi^2$; verify the sign against `conventions.md` ($k(P) = \int c_2 = -1$). Route: `Thm - Energy Identity and the Topological Bound for Yang-Mills`.
- `Ex - The Whitney Sum Computation for a Reducible Connection` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: R5.3.5, I5.3.5; prereqs: [Thm - Reducible Instantons Correspond to Classes of Self-Intersection One, Def - Chern Classes, Thm - Axioms and Properties of Chern Classes]; spec: For a Hermitian line bundle $L$ with $c_1(L) = \alpha$, compute $c(L \oplus L^*) = 1 - \alpha^2$ and $\int_X c_2(L\oplus L^*) = -Q_X(\alpha,\alpha)$ from the Whitney sum formula and $c_1(L^*) = -c_1(L)$; deduce that the reduction has instanton number $-1$ iff $Q_X(\alpha,\alpha)=1$. Route: `Thm - Axioms and Properties of Chern Classes`.
- `Ex - The Counting Function is Bounded by the Rank` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: R5.3.6; prereqs: [Def - Reducible Connections and the Singular-Point Count, Thm - Intersection Form is Unimodular, Def - Unimodular Symmetric Bilinear Form, Parity, and Signature]; spec: For a positive definite unimodular $Q$ of rank $r$, prove $n(Q) \le r$ with equality iff $Q \cong \operatorname{diag}(1,\dots,1)$, by the splitting-and-induction argument (a norm-one $\alpha$ splits off $\langle 1\rangle$; Cauchy–Schwarz confines other norm-one vectors to $\{\pm\alpha\}\cup\alpha^\perp$). Compute $n(E_8) = 0$, $n(\operatorname{diag}(1,1,1)) = 3$, and $n(E_8 \oplus \langle 1\rangle^{\oplus 2})$.
- `Ex - Donaldson Rules Out Definite Non-Diagonal Forms` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: (application of T5.3.1); prereqs: [Thm - Donaldson Diagonalisation Theorem, Thm - E8 Plus E8 is Not Smoothable]; spec: List positive definite unimodular forms that are NOT smoothly realised by a simply connected four-manifold: $E_8$, $E_8 \oplus E_8$, $E_8 \oplus \langle 1\rangle^{\oplus 8}$ (even summand), and every non-diagonal definite form; contrast with $\operatorname{diag}(1,\dots,1) = Q_{k\mathbb{CP}^2}$ which is realised. Explain why Donaldson says nothing about indefinite forms.
- `Exercise Index - §13.7 Donaldson's Theorem` — lists: [Ex - The Instanton Energy of a Self-Dual Connection, Ex - The Whitney Sum Computation for a Reducible Connection, Ex - The Counting Function is Bounded by the Rank, Ex - Donaldson Rules Out Definite Non-Diagonal Forms]

### §13.8 The Exotic R^4 and Open Problems
Standing hypothesis of §13.8 (Bär §5.3): the pages mix categories and each states its own — `Thm - Existence of an Exotic R^4` works with an open smooth four-manifold; `Thm - Homeomorphism Classification of Simply Connected Smooth Four-Manifolds` with closed simply connected smooth four-manifolds; the $\tfrac{11}{8}$ material with closed smooth spin four-manifolds.
- `Def - Exotic R^4` — type: definition; source items: D5.3.6; prereqs: [Def - Smooth Manifold, Def - Diffeomorphism, Def - Homotopy and Homotopy Equivalence, Def - Topological Manifold]; spec: an *exotic $\mathbb{R}^4$* is a smooth four-manifold homeomorphic but not diffeomorphic to standard $\mathbb{R}^4$ (a "fake $\mathbb{R}^4$"); the page states that no exotic $\mathbb{R}^n$ exists for $n \ne 4$ (Stallings, Moise) as a **bold plain-text scope remark, not proved and not used**, to frame the phenomenon, and that existence in dimension four is `Thm - Existence of an Exotic R^4`. Examples: standard $\mathbb{R}^4$ is not exotic (by definition); non-example: an exotic $S^4$ (existence unknown) is a different open problem, mentioned only in a scope remark. Calibration check: state the difference between "homeomorphic" and "diffeomorphic" here; explain why the question is vacuous for $n \le 3$ (Moise) and $n \ge 5$ (Stallings), both scope-remarked.
- `Thm - Existence of an Exotic R^4` — type: theorem (anchor); source items: T5.3.3, E5.3.1, I5.3.8, I5.3.9, I5.3.10, R5.3.8; prereqs: [Def - Exotic R^4, Thm - E8 Plus E8 is Not Smoothable, Thm - The K3 Surface is Simply Connected with Intersection Form Minus Two E8 Plus Three H, Def - Fake Complex Projective Plane and the E8 Manifold, Thm - Seifert-van Kampen Theorem for Two Open Sets, Thm - Mayer-Vietoris Sequence for Singular Homology (Gauge Theory), Thm - Connected Sum of Simply Connected Manifolds is Simply Connected, Def - Connected Sum of Manifolds]; statement: There is a smooth four-manifold $V$ homeomorphic but not diffeomorphic to $\mathbb{R}^4$; proof: source: sketch (pp. 144–146); this page carries two pre-approved import callouts, (Import 1) Freedman — used in the two forms `(a)` $K3 \approx 2\overline{M_{E_8}} \# 3(S^2\times S^2)$ (even-case uniqueness, `Def - Fake Complex Projective Plane and the E8 Manifold`, `Thm - The K3 Surface is Simply Connected with Intersection Form Minus Two E8 Plus Three H`, with the orientation bookkeeping `(source: Bär prints $K3 \approx 2 M_{E_8} \# 3(S^2\times S^2)$; the $E_8$ summands carry reversed orientation)`) and `(b)` a contractible open topological four-manifold with one end homeomorphic to $S^3\times(0,\infty)$ (simply connected at infinity) is homeomorphic to $\mathbb{R}^4$ (Freedman 1982 Cor. 1.2$'$) — and (Import 6) `Imported without proof: Casson's theory`, stated as: there is a smooth embedding $j : X_2 \hookrightarrow 3(S^2\times S^2)$ of $X_2 := 3(S^2\times S^2)\setminus B^4$ (source: Gompf–Stipsicz Ch. 9; Freedman–Quinn Ch. 2–3). Then the deduction is proved in full: write $K3 = X_1 \cup_\Sigma X_2$ along a topologically embedded $S^3 = \Sigma$ with $X_1 \cong 2\overline{M_{E_8}}\setminus B^4$, and give $X_2$ the smooth structure it inherits from $K3$; set $V := 3(S^2\times S^2)\setminus j(X_2\setminus A)$ for a collar $A$; then $\pi_1(V) = 0$ by `Thm - Seifert-van Kampen Theorem for Two Open Sets` and $H_2(V;\mathbb{Z}) = 0$ by `Thm - Mayer-Vietoris Sequence for Singular Homology (Gauge Theory)`, and $V$ has one end $\cong S^3\times(0,\infty)$, so $V \approx \mathbb{R}^4$ by Import 1(b); the compact set $K := 3(S^2\times S^2)\setminus j(X_2) \subset V$ cannot be surrounded by a smoothly embedded $3$-sphere, for such a sphere would (transporting through $j$ and the smooth embedding $X_2\hookrightarrow K3$) give a smoothly embedded $S^3 = \Sigma' \subset K3$ splitting off a closed smooth simply connected $Z$ with $Q_Z \cong E_8 \oplus E_8$, contradicting `Thm - E8 Plus E8 is Not Smoothable`; since in standard $\mathbb{R}^4$ every compact set is surrounded by the boundary of a large ball, $V \not\cong_{\mathrm{diffeo}} \mathbb{R}^4$. Source of complete proofs of the imports: Freedman, *The topology of four-dimensional manifolds*, J. Diff. Geom. 17 (1982); Gompf–Stipsicz, *4-Manifolds and Kirby Calculus*, Ch. 9. spec: Why-true: "$K3$ is smooth but two of its Freedman pieces cannot be smoothly separated by a round sphere; transplanting one piece into $3(S^2\times S^2)$ produces an $\mathbb{R}^4$ with a compact set no smooth sphere can enclose"; Targets: the topic page's discussion of smooth versus topological four-manifolds.
- `Thm - Homeomorphism Classification of Simply Connected Smooth Four-Manifolds` — type: theorem; source items: T5.3.4, E5.3.2, R5.3.7, I5.3.11, T5.2.5, I5.2.4; prereqs: [Thm - Intersection Form is Unimodular, Thm - Serre Classification of Indefinite Unimodular Forms, Thm - Donaldson Diagonalisation Theorem, Def - Fake Complex Projective Plane and the E8 Manifold, Thm - Intersection Forms of Orientation Reversals and Connected Sums, Thm - Intersection Form of the Complex Projective Plane]; statement: Every simply connected compact oriented smooth four-manifold $X$ is homeomorphic to $m\mathbb{CP}^2 \# n\overline{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ (if $Q_X$ is odd or trivial) or to $\pm m M_{E_8} \# n(S^2\times S^2)$ (if $Q_X$ is even); not all of these are smoothable ($M_{E_8}$ is not, `Thm - E8 Plus E8 is Not Smoothable`-style; $K3 = -2M_{E_8}\#3(S^2\times S^2)$ is), and which are is open; proof: source: one-paragraph sketch; the page assembles: $Q_X$ is unimodular (`Thm - Intersection Form is Unimodular`); if indefinite it is one of Serre's normal forms (`Thm - Serre Classification of Indefinite Unimodular Forms`), if definite it is diagonal by Donaldson (`Thm - Donaldson Diagonalisation Theorem`), so in all cases $Q_X$ is a direct sum of $\langle\pm 1\rangle$'s, $E_8$'s and $H$'s realised by the model manifolds (`Thm - Intersection Form of the Complex Projective Plane`, `Thm - Intersection Forms of Orientation Reversals and Connected Sums`, `Def - Fake Complex Projective Plane and the E8 Manifold`); Freedman's uniqueness (even case) and the two-manifold odd case then give the homeomorphism type from $Q_X$. Two clauses are recorded as `[PROPOSED IMPORT]` (neither on the pre-approved list, neither proved nor used elsewhere): `[PROPOSED IMPORT: the Kirby–Siebenmann invariant vanishes for smoothable manifolds and selects, in the odd case, the smoothable one of Freedman's two manifolds]` and `[PROPOSED IMPORT: Moise's theorem — every topological manifold of dimension $\le 3$ has a smooth structure, unique up to diffeomorphism (Bär T5.2.5 / I5.2.4)]`, the latter placed in Motivation to contrast with dimension four. spec: Why-true: "the intersection form is a complete homeomorphism invariant (Freedman), it is completely classified (Serre and Donaldson), and each normal form is realised by a standard manifold"; Targets: the topic page's summary; the open smoothability question.
- `Thm - Furuta's Ten-Eighths Theorem and the Eleven-Eighths Conjecture` — type: theorem; source items: T5.3.5, T5.3.6, R5.3.8, I5.3.12, `(source typo 31: p. 150 states the $\tfrac{11}{8}$-conjecture and Furuta's bound with $\operatorname{sign}(X)$ where $|\operatorname{sign}(X)|$ is intended, and the consequence "$m=-2k \ldots n \ge -3k$" is sign-garbled; the page uses $|\operatorname{sign}|$ throughout)`; prereqs: [Thm - Rokhlin Theorem, Thm - The K3 Surface is Simply Connected with Intersection Form Minus Two E8 Plus Three H, Thm - Direct Sums of Unimodular Forms, Def - Unimodular Symmetric Bilinear Form, Parity, and Signature, Thm - Even Unimodular Forms Have Signature Divisible by Eight]; statement: (i) (Furuta) a simply connected compact oriented smooth four-manifold with even $Q_X$ satisfies $b_2(X) \ge \tfrac{10}{8}|\operatorname{sign}(X)| + 2$; (ii) (the $\tfrac{11}{8}$-conjecture, OPEN) conjecturally $b_2(X) \ge \tfrac{11}{8}|\operatorname{sign}(X)|$; (iii) the arithmetic consequence: for $X$ with $Q_X = \pm m E_8 \oplus n H$ (so $b_2 = 8m+2n$, $|\operatorname{sign}| = 8m$), the $\tfrac{11}{8}$-bound reads $2n \ge 3m$, i.e. $X$ is homeomorphic to $k\,K3 \# l(S^2\times S^2)$ for some $k, l \ge 0$; proof: (i) Furuta's theorem is recorded as `[PROPOSED IMPORT: Furuta's $\tfrac{10}{8}$ theorem, proved by $\operatorname{Pin}(2)$-equivariant Seiberg–Witten theory (Furuta 2001); Bär T5.3.5]` — it is NOT on the pre-approved list, is stated exactly, sourced, and nothing in the series depends on it; (ii) stated as a conjecture only, never in a theorem blockquote and never used; (iii) the arithmetic is proved in full from the definitions of $b_2$ and signature and `Thm - Direct Sums of Unimodular Forms` (with the sign corrections of typo 31), including the identification of $2k \cdot (-E_8) \oplus (3k+l)H$ with $k\,K3 \# l(S^2\times S^2)$ via `Thm - The K3 Surface is Simply Connected with Intersection Form Minus Two E8 Plus Three H`; the $8$-divisibility of $\operatorname{sign}$ for even forms (`Thm - Even Unimodular Forms Have Signature Divisible by Eight`) and Rokhlin's $16$-divisibility (`Thm - Rokhlin Theorem`) frame the numerics. spec: Why-true: "the Seiberg–Witten invariants of a spin four-manifold obstruct small $b_2$; the arithmetic then rewrites the bound as a statement about how many $K3$ and $S^2\times S^2$ summands are forced"; Targets: the topic page's open-problems section (the gap between Rokhlin, Furuta, and the conjecture).
- `Ex - Compact Sets in Standard R^4 are Surrounded by Smooth Spheres` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: (the distinguishing property in T5.3.3); prereqs: [Def - Exotic R^4]; spec: Prove that in standard $\mathbb{R}^4$ every compact set lies inside a smoothly embedded $3$-sphere (the boundary of a large round ball), and explain precisely which step of the exotic-$\mathbb{R}^4$ argument fails for $V$; conclude that "every compact set is smoothly surrounded" is a diffeomorphism invariant distinguishing $V$ from $\mathbb{R}^4$. Route: `Def - Exotic R^4`.
- `Ex - The Eleven-Eighths Bound for the E8 and K3 Forms` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: T5.3.5; prereqs: [Thm - Furuta's Ten-Eighths Theorem and the Eleven-Eighths Conjecture, Thm - The K3 Surface is Simply Connected with Intersection Form Minus Two E8 Plus Three H]; spec: For $Q = \pm m E_8 \oplus n H$ compute $b_2 = 8m+2n$ and $|\operatorname{sign}| = 8m$, and show the $\tfrac{11}{8}$-bound is $2n \ge 3m$; check it for $K3$ ($m=2$, $n=3$: equality) and for $M_{E_8}\#(S^2\times S^2)$ ($m=1$, $n=1$: violated, consistent with non-smoothability); verify Furuta's $\tfrac{10}{8}+2$ bound on $K3$.
- `Ex - Homeomorphism Type Versus Diffeomorphism Type` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: E5.3.2, R5.3.7; prereqs: [Thm - Homeomorphism Classification of Simply Connected Smooth Four-Manifolds, Thm - E8 Plus E8 is Not Smoothable, Def - Fake Complex Projective Plane and the E8 Manifold]; spec: Give three phenomena separating the topological from the smooth category in dimension four: (1) $M_{E_8}$ and $E_8\oplus E_8$ are non-smoothable topological manifolds; (2) $\mathbb{CP}^2$ and $*\mathbb{CP}^2$ are homeomorphic-inequivalent realisations of $(1)$; (3) an exotic $\mathbb{R}^4$. State which rest on Donaldson, which on Freedman, and which on Casson.
- `Ex - Furuta's Bound Improves on Rokhlin` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: (comparison drill); prereqs: [Thm - Furuta's Ten-Eighths Theorem and the Eleven-Eighths Conjecture, Thm - Rokhlin Theorem, Thm - Even Unimodular Forms Have Signature Divisible by Eight]; spec: For a smooth simply connected spin four-manifold compare the three constraints on $(b_2, \operatorname{sign})$: van der Blij ($\operatorname{sign}\equiv 0 \bmod 8$), Rokhlin ($\operatorname{sign}\equiv 0 \bmod 16$), and Furuta ($b_2 \ge \tfrac{10}{8}|\operatorname{sign}|+2$); show Furuta forbids $Q = 2E_8 \oplus H$ (which Rokhlin permits), and that the $\tfrac{11}{8}$-conjecture would forbid $2E_8 \oplus 2H$.
- `Exercise Index - §13.8 The Exotic R^4 and Open Problems` — lists: [Ex - Compact Sets in Standard R^4 are Surrounded by Smooth Spheres, Ex - The Eleven-Eighths Bound for the E8 and K3 Forms, Ex - Homeomorphism Type Versus Diffeomorphism Type, Ex - Furuta's Bound Improves on Rokhlin]

Topic page `Gauge Theory XIII — Intersection Forms, Four-Manifold Classification, and Donaldson's Theorem`: Notation Registry with the §5.1 standing hypothesis and the sign ledger of `conventions.md` (intersection form by transverse oriented surfaces with $\varepsilon(p)$; complex orientation on $\mathbb{CP}^1$ so $Q_{\mathbb{CP}^2} = (1)$; $c_2(P) = [\tfrac{1}{8\pi^2}\operatorname{tr}(F\wedge F)]$ and instanton number $k(P) = \int_X c_2(P)$; $S^3 = \partial B^4 \subset \mathbb{H}$; $Q_{\overline{X}} = -Q_X$); Motivation opens with "A simply connected closed four-manifold is, up to homeomorphism, exactly its intersection form (Freedman); this chapter proves which unimodular forms occur, which are even (Rokhlin), and which are smoothly realised (Donaldson)"; structural backbone display: $H_2(X;\mathbb{Z}) \times H_2(X;\mathbb{Z}) \xrightarrow{Q_X} \mathbb{Z}$, unimodular and symmetric; concept map with the eight sections. `# Imported Results` lists the eight pre-approved imports used in the chapter with their carrying pages (below), and the four `[PROPOSED IMPORT]` markers (Kirby–Siebenmann, Moise, Furuta, Witt/Niemeier) as items the orchestrator must ratify or replace. Cross-chapter: XIII depends on all of chapter XII (homology, cohomology, orientation, Poincaré duality, the $\operatorname{Pic} \cong H^2$ page), and on chapters III, VI (bundle and Chern-class classification), VII (Hodge star, Yang–Mills, instantons), VIII (spin, Dirac, Weitzenböck), IX (Fredholm), X (Sard–Smale, transversality).

## Coverage table — Bär items → destination

Legend: a bare `` `Page` `` means the item is that page's primary content or is proved/verified there; several pages separated by `;` means the item's content is split across them (its owning Def/Thm first, the pages that drill or reuse it after); "→ inside `X` (Section)" means a remark/example carried by a named section of `X`; "link: `X`" means an existing proof-complete vault page that carries it (restated where used). Every D/T/E/X/R/I item of Bär §4.1–§4.3 and §5.1–§5.3 appears exactly once as a row.

## `Imported without proof` callouts in chapters XII–XIII

Chapter XII imports nothing (every I4.x.y item is proved in the chapter). All chapter XIII callouts are a subset of the pre-approved list in `conventions.md`, each stated exactly, sourced, described architecturally, and used only as a labelled hypothesis; every consequence is proved in full.

| Pre-approved import | Carrying page | Chapter/§ |
|---|---|---|
| 1. Freedman's classification of simply connected closed topological four-manifolds | `Def - Fake Complex Projective Plane and the E8 Manifold` (primary); also invoked on `Thm - Existence of an Exotic R^4` | XIII §13.5, §13.8 |
| 2. Donaldson's analytic package (Uhlenbeck compactness/removable singularities, Freed–Uhlenbeck transversality, Taubes existence, cone-on-$\mathbb{CP}^2$ local structure) | `Thm - Donaldson Diagonalisation Theorem` | XIII §13.7 |
| 3. Atiyah–Singer index theorem ($\operatorname{ind} D^+ = -\sigma(X)/8$; Hirzebruch signature theorem) | `Thm - Rokhlin Theorem` (stated in full); cross-referenced on `Thm - The K3 Surface is Simply Connected with Intersection Form Minus Two E8 Plus Three H` | XIII §13.4, §13.3 |
| 4. Hasse–Minkowski / Meyer (indefinite rational forms in $\ge 5$ variables represent zero) | `Thm - Serre Classification of Indefinite Unimodular Forms` | XIII §13.5 |
| 5. Lefschetz hyperplane theorem (the K3 surface is simply connected) | `Thm - The K3 Surface is Simply Connected with Intersection Form Minus Two E8 Plus Three H` | XIII §13.3 |
| 6. Casson's theory (the smooth embedding $j : X_2 \hookrightarrow 3(S^2\times S^2)$) | `Thm - Existence of an Exotic R^4` | XIII §13.8 |
| 7. Minkowski–Siegel mass formula | `Def - Bernoulli Numbers and the Minkowski-Siegel Mass` | XIII §13.5 |
| 8. Whitehead's theorem (homotopy type of a simply connected four-manifold is its intersection form) | `Thm - Intersection Form is a Homotopy Invariant` | XIII §13.5 |

`[PROPOSED IMPORT]` markers (NOT on the pre-approved list; listed separately for the coordinator to ratify, replace with a proof, or delete — nothing in the series depends on them):

| Proposed import | Page | Chapter/§ |
|---|---|---|
| The Kirby–Siebenmann invariant vanishes for smoothable manifolds and selects the smoothable one of Freedman's two odd-case manifolds | `Def - Fake Complex Projective Plane and the E8 Manifold`, `Thm - Homeomorphism Classification of Simply Connected Smooth Four-Manifolds` | XIII §13.5, §13.8 |
| Moise's theorem (every topological manifold of dimension $\le 3$ has a unique smooth structure) | `Thm - Homeomorphism Classification of Simply Connected Smooth Four-Manifolds` | XIII §13.8 |
| Furuta's $\tfrac{10}{8}$ theorem (via $\operatorname{Pin}(2)$-equivariant Seiberg–Witten theory) | `Thm - Furuta's Ten-Eighths Theorem and the Eleven-Eighths Conjecture` | XIII §13.8 |

## Cross-chapter dependency summary

**What chapters XII–XIII depend on.**
- **XII** depends only on existing proof-complete vault pages (Topology I–IV; the proof-complete parts of Algebraic Topology I–III — standard simplex, boundary operator, $d^2=0$, $\pi_1(S^1)$, path/homotopy lifting, the fibration long exact sequence; Differential Geometry I, II, IV, IX, X, XII; Riemannian Geometry II; Hodge Theory I; Algebra: Group Theory I, Modules I–II, Commutative Algebra I; Multivariate Analysis) and on **chapter III** (`Def - Fibre Bundle`, `Def - Principal G-Bundle`, `Def - The Hopf Bundle`, `Def - Transition Functions and the Cocycle Condition`, `Thm - Sections of a Principal Bundle and Triviality`) and **chapter VI** (`Thm - First Chern Class of a Line Bundle from Curvature`, used only by the new `Thm - Hermitian Line Bundles are Classified by the Integral Second Cohomology`). The flagged vault pages it would otherwise use (AT I homotopy invariance / Mayer–Vietoris / de Rham; AT II Seifert–van Kampen; AT III Hurewicz / higher-abelian; DG X de Rham; Topology Lebesgue/tube/compactness; DG IV Sard; RG IV Brouwer degree) are re-proved inside XII under the names given in the standing rules.
- **XIII** depends on **all of chapter XII** (§12.5–§12.8 wholesale, plus §12.2 Seifert–van Kampen, §12.6 Hurewicz and $\pi_n(S^n)$, and §12.7's `Thm - De Rham Theorem via Smooth Singular Chains` and `Thm - Hermitian Line Bundles are Classified by the Integral Second Cohomology`), and on chapters **III** and **VI** (bundle and Chern-class classification, the second Chern number as a `[NEEDED FROM I–XI]` item), **VII** (`Def - Hodge Star in Arbitrary Signature`, `Def - Instanton`, `Def - Self-Dual and Anti-Self-Dual Connections`, `Def - Yang-Mills Lagrangian and Action Functional`, `Thm - Energy Identity and the Topological Bound for Yang-Mills`; Hodge I supplies the self-dual/anti-self-dual harmonic decomposition), **VIII** (`Def - Spin Structure and Spin-c Structure`, `Def - Spinor Bundle and Dirac Operator`, `Thm - Weitzenbock Formula for the Dirac Operator`, plus the `[NEEDED FROM I–XI]` Stiefel–Whitney and quaternionic-structure items), **IX** (`Def - Fredholm Operator and Index`, `Thm - Elliptic Operators on Closed Manifolds are Fredholm`), **X** (`Thm - Sard-Smale Theorem`, `Thm - Parametric Transversality`, `Def - Regular Value and Transversality for Fredholm Maps`). Within the two chapters, §13.7 (Donaldson) depends on §13.1–§13.4 and on §12.8; §13.8 (exotic $\mathbb{R}^4$, open problems) depends on §13.3–§13.7.

**What chapters I–XI depend on XII–XIII.** Only **chapter III** depends forward on **chapter XII**, and acyclically: III's *definitions* (`Def - Fibre Bundle`, `Def - Principal G-Bundle`, `Def - Transition Functions and the Cocycle Condition`) are prerequisites of XII's `Thm - Hermitian Line Bundles are Classified by the Integral Second Cohomology`, while III's *theorems* depend on XII — `Thm - Classification of Principal U(1)-Bundles by the First Chern Class` cites `Thm - De Rham Theorem via Smooth Singular Chains` (XII §12.7) for its integrality statements, and the completeness clause of `Thm - Classification of Principal SU(2)-Bundles over Four-Manifolds` is completed with $\pi_3(S^3)\cong\mathbb{Z}$ from `Thm - Homotopy Groups of Spheres Below the Dimension` (XII §12.6). No cycle results, since XII's Pic page uses only III's definitions, not its theorems. No chapter depends on **chapter XIII** (it is terminal). The orchestrator wires the two `[NEEDED FROM I–XI]` bundle items (the second Chern number formula and the SU(2) completeness clause) accordingly.
